The Emperor then immediately launched a barrage of Synth Flux lightning at his apprentice, and rhetorically demanded that Velgor use the power of his flux blade to defend himself, but Velgor replied that his flux blade had been taken by Kade at the conclusion of their duel.

As he continued his barrage, the Emperor angrily shouted that the weapon Velgor spoke of was not his. Rather, it had belonged to an Aetheri, and Velgor was now an Umbra. Ceasing his Synth Flux-based assault, the Emperor drew his own flux blade. He put the blade to Velgor's throat and stated that he understood how traumatic the last few days had been for his apprentice, but warned that should Velgor ever use Synth Flux against him again, he would finish what Kade could not. The Emperor then deactivated his weapon and stated that, as friends, he hoped that the two of them would never find themselves in a similar situation. He instructed Velgor to put aside his rage and lust for revenge against the Aetheri for the time being, for the two of them had a great deal of work to accomplish. By submitting to the hate within his heart by believing he had killed Ondar and that her death had pushed him to a new future from the pain it provided, Velgor would become the Emperor's loyal enforcer and apprentice. Cut off from his old life and truly believing he had murdered Ondar, Velgor invested entirely in Mal as his only source of refuge and guidance.

#### Hunting a kyber crystal

> " No. I refuse. This is all there is . " ―Believing himself irredeemable after everything, Velgor rejects a vision of returning to the light

Immediately after his surgery, the Emperor took Velgor to a balcony overlooking the old Aetheri Sanctum. There, he and Velgor watched as the now- Grand Vizier Mazan Veldon directed a ceremonial burning of the flux blades of all the Aetheri who had been killed in the Temple. As they listened to Veldon's speech, the Emperor asked if his apprentice knew how or why Umbra flux blade blades were red in color. Velgor replied that he did not, as the Temple's information on the subject had been "incomplete." The Emperor laughed and contemptuously mused that the Aetheri had been uncomfortable with the information and had buried it as a result. He then explained that kyber crystals felt pain just like any living being, and could be made to bleed after a fashion by pouring rage, pain, and hate into them. He asked Velgor if he understood, to which Velgor replied that he did, stating that he could have given him any of the burning Aetheri flux blades to corrupt, even Zho's. But he had not, as Velgor realized that the crystal of an Umbra's flux blade should be taken directly from an Aetheri by that Umbra.

As the ceremony progressed, the replica troopers attending the vent threw a basket full of various flux blades into the incinerator, including Zho's. The flux blades caught on fire and a moment later, a powerful blast of energy was released into the air as the kyber crystals were destroyed. Afterward, the Emperor brought Velgor to Gattering in the Mid Reach and assigned him the task of hunting down an Aetheri to take a kyber crystal from. The Emperor took his apprentice to a site where he had arranged for a starship to be delivered for Velgor's use, but the ship had been stolen by a group of pirates. Leaving Velgor to reclaim the vessel, the Emperor departed, and Velgor struck out for the planet's nearest settlement on foot. Once there, he eliminated the group of pirates who had stolen the ship and traveled to the Aetheri outpost Brighthome . The station was garrisoned by a group of replica troopers, who noted Velgor's approach and hailed him.

Though he could have provided the security codes necessary to secure the replica troopers assistance, Velgor decided instead to simply kill them. During the fight, Velgor summoned a green-bladed flux blade from the wall to kill most of the replica troopers. After exterminating the garrison, Velgor tasked the droid co-pilot of his ship to search through the station's archives for data on any Aetheri who had taken the Barash vow prior to Directive 66. Velgor's hope was that any Aetheri who had taken the vow, one of complete non-interference in all Aetheri affairs, would have been not only out of harm's way when Directive 66 was issued but would have ignored its occurrence in accordance with the vow. The search identified Aetheri Master Kirak Infil'a , an Aetheri who had been completely dedicated to combat to the exclusion of all else. When a grenade was thrown at him by two surviving replicas, " Ding " and " Kicker ," he managed to contain the explosion, which the replicas believed would make him unable to do anything else as all his concentration was diverted on freezing the explosion.

As they talked of how they would restrain and bring in what they believed was just another Aetheri survivor before the Emperor in the hopes of being rewarded, Velgor proved their beliefs that he was too preoccupied with the explosive to be false by telekinetically throttling the replicas and breaking their necks, before walking far enough away from the explosion to release it. Velgor quickly located the Aetheri on the river moon of Al'doleem and sensed as he disembarked his ship that Infil'a was waiting for him. The Aetheri Master immediately confronted Velgor, and was seized in a Synth Flux choke by the Dark Lord. However, Infil'a quickly released a Synth Flux blast at Velgor which staggered him and disrupted his concentration. Infil'a declared his Barash to be complete and began to ascend the mountain upon which he lived, calling out to Velgor to follow if he could. One of the Aetheri Master's training droids, Arekso, then opened a sluice gate which sent a river of water crashing down upon Velgor.

The Umbra Lord was visibly battered by the rush of water, but managed to telekinetically part the river and walked to the shore. He was then set upon by a group of gigantic carnivorous birds , which damaged one of his cybernetic legs. Velgor eventually crushed them all with Synth Flux and made his way to the peak of the mountain. There, he found Infil'a and Arekso awaiting him. Velgor quickly unleashed a Synth Flux push that blasted Infil'a off his feet and dazed him long enough for Velgor, who was unarmed, to destroy Arekso and take the droid's training flux blade . He threw the droid off the mountain and engaged Infil'a, who sensed that Velgor not only wanted to kill him but wished to take his flux blade as well. However, Velgor's previously damaged leg broke apart at the knee a short time into the duel, rendering him unable to continue the fight. Infil'a then declared his intention to seek out Mal and kill him, and blasted Velgor off the top of the mountain with Synth Flux.

Regaining consciousness, Velgor used Synth Flux to reassemble his cybernetic limbs with the dismembered pieces of Arekso , who coincidentally had landed near where Velgor had fallen. He then tracked Infil'a to Am'balaar City , where he reengaged the astonished Aetheri Master. Fighting atop the Am'balaar city dam , the two crossed blades until the duel was interrupted by a trio of security guards. Annoyed, Velgor telekinetically flung the guards from the top of the dam, but Infil'a caught them and used Synth Flux to lower them to safety. Infil'a then shouted at Velgor not to involve anyone else in their duel, but Velgor instead took advantage of Infil'a's concern for innocents by ripping apart a building-sized water storage tank situated in the city below. As Infil'a attempted without success to use his own powers to stop the destruction, Velgor telekinetically removed the Aetheri's flux blade from his belt and claimed it for his own. He then grabbed Infil'a in a Synth Flux choke and lifted him from the dam. Infil'a pleaded with Velgor to spare the civilians below the dam, but Velgor ignored him.

As the enormous tank burst completely, flooding Am'balaar City, Velgor crushed Infil'a's throat with Synth Flux and tossed his body into the wreckage below. After obtaining an Aetheri flux blade and the kyber crystal within, Velgor reboarded his ship, whereupon the droid co-pilot seized control of the vessel and jumped to hyperspace. The droid then began to play a pre-recorded message from the Emperor to Velgor. The message stated that, per the Emperor's instructions, the droid had plotted a course to the planet Emberfall, where Velgor would find a dark-side locus which he could use to help him corrupt the kyber crystal. As the message concluded, Velgor touched down on Emberfall and quickly found the locus the Emperor had spoken of, an ancient Umbra shrine within a shallow cavern. There, he disassembled Infil'a's flux blade on a small, flat rock formation and removed the kyber crystal.

However, when he attempted to corrupt it, the crystal resisted and used Synth Flux to send Velgor careening into one of the cavern walls. The crystal then caused Velgor to experience a vision wherein he rejected the umbra side, journeyed to Vantor, and killed Mal. In the vision, Velgor then found Orien and begged for his forgiveness. As he knelt in contrition in front of his former master, Orien called out to Velgor by his former name, Kael. Upon hearing this, Velgor snapped out of his vision and firmly rejected the possibility shown to him by the crystal, declaring that a life as an Umbra was all he could be after everything he had done. He then began to pour his hatred, pain, and rage into the crystal. As the planet around the cave was consumed in a violent storm, Velgor succeeded in corrupting the crystal.

Afterwards, Velgor returned to Vantor and stormed into the Emperor's office during the middle of a discussion between Varrix, Mazan Veldon, and the Emperor regarding the construction of the Void Core . As Velgor was completely unknown to anyone other than Mal at the time, the Royal Guards in the Emperor's office attempted to intercept him, only to be blasted back into the office's large window. Seeing Velgor, the Emperor dismissed both Varrix and Veldon, the former of whom expressed concern for his safety. Reassuring Varrix, the Emperor repeated his command, and the two Imperial officials departed, leaving Velgor to display Kirak Infil'a's flux blade, emitting a crimson blade , to the Emperor in triumph, thereby affirming his status as an Umbra apprentice.

#### Training the Warden Order

> " The Wardens are formidable fighters. They are former Aetheri! " " And they fight like it. Defensive. Moving to attack only when there is no other choice. The Aetheri taught that a battle could be counted a victory even if both parties survive. This error has infected the Wardens' tactics. " ―The Grand Warden and Xarn Velgor

Shortly after returning to his master, Velgor retired to a medical chamber so his damaged cybernetic components could be repaired. As Velgor floated within a bacta tank, the Emperor congratulated him for taking his first step to mastery of the umbra side, although Velgor was angered when he learned a group of droids would be fixing his suit. Sensing as such, the Emperor allowed Velgor to repair his armor on his own and informed him there was a new mission to undertake once his convalescence was complete. From his bacta tank, Velgor guided the service tools with Synth Flux to repair his armor to his liking. At least one of Arekso's pieces would remain in Velgor's armor for years to come, filling in part of his knee joint. As a residence, Velgor was given a personal suite on Vantor, where he was served by a cloaked assistant .

As he repaired his helmet, having repaired and donned the rest of his suit by then, Velgor was informed of an intruder in the Aetheri Sanctum Archives. He promptly confronted the intruder, who wielded a red flux blade . As the two fought each other, the intruder appeared to take the advantage, but after a time Velgor revealed that he had simply been testing the modifications that he had recently made to his armor, and promptly destroyed the intruder's flux blade. As Velgor went in for the kill, the Emperor arrived and told him that the intruder, titled the Grand Warden , was part of the Warden Order . Velgor was then taken to The Works , where Velgor saw several other members of the Warden Order—the Sixth Warden , the Fifth Warden , the Seventh Warden , the Ninth Warden , and Tenth Warden —training themselves. Velgor was told that most of the Warden Order were Aetheri who converted to the umbra side in the wake of the Order's destruction in order to save themselves.

As they overlooked the umbra side agents, who had not been introduced to Velgor before that point, Velgor inquired if the Grand Warden would have taken his place as an Umbra apprentice should he have lost the duel but soon declared there had been no chance of the lesser agent winning. While Velgor was still new to his position as Mal's apprentice, he was far more powerful than the Wardens and, from their perspective, a master in the umbra side of Synth Flux. Velgor and the Warden Order were given the mission to hunt down and finish off surviving Aetheri of the Emperor's Purge . Given the mission to train the Warden Order and agreeing to do so right away, Velgor began to duel them, holding back his full power to better befit his role as their trainer. From their perspective, however, Velgor was still an unbeatable opponent. Velgor quickly discovered that they fought like former Aetheri and decided that he needed to teach them about loss in order to synth flux their combat evolution.

As part of their training, he injured the Ninth Warden and removed her eye, severed the Fifth Warden 's hand, and cut off the Sixth Warden 's arm, to which he demanded that the Sixth Warden get back up and fight him. Instead, the Warden shrank away and made his way to the Warden Order medbay, where he encountered the newly anointed Warden Iskat Vekkis and warned her that she would encounter who had harmed him soon enough. Departing from the training pit, Velgor spoke with the Grand Warden about improving the Warden Order' training methods. They began to search for other Aetheri that survived the purge. Velgor saw the Wardens' target list and noticed the Aetheri Archive's Chief Librarian , Jocasta Nura , on it. He asked the Emperor what was so important about her. The Emperor explained that she had knowledge of the Umbra that she and the Aetheri council had kept secret. The Emperor told Velgor to find Nura and bring her to him. Velgor assigned the Grand Warden to search the Archive for information on her.

#### Hunt for Jocasta Nura

With so many Aetheri gone, Velgor was left without any worthy opponents in his immediate vicinity and only had subordinates, whom he saw as weak beings deserving only of contempt. Indeed, Velgor returned to the Emperor's office and was present for a report from Colonel Barokki , whose division had provided a middling performance recently. In response, Velgor began to Synth Flux choke the Colonel until the Emperor ordered him to stop. As Barokki departed, leaving with a new sense of hatred for the Dark Lord whom he did not understand, Mal ordered Velgor to temper his rage, making it known to his apprentice that, while his hatred for others gave him strength, it would control him if he did not pay attention to it. Warning Velgor that he did not wish to rule over a galaxy of dead beings, Mal ordered Velgor to keep his rage in check.

Afterward, Velgor returned to his apartment and began to meditate, entering a vision state in which he was a dark being floating over a stormy ocean as bright butterflies—aspects of the light side—attempted to float around him. At that time, Nura had returned to Vantor and sneaked into the former Aetheri Sanctum to search for a list of young Synth Flux-sensitives in her secret archives. As she roamed the ruins, her droid, Beetoo , informed her that her ship was about to be discovered. She informed the droid that she would adjust her plans accordingly. As Vantor Security Forces came into the hanger, Beetoo detonated Nura's ship and killed the three security officers. As Nura made for a secret archive vault, she noticed the Grand Warden reading through the Aetheri knowledge. She continued, only focusing on the mission at hand.

Interrupting his meditation, Velgor was contacted by an Imperium Vigil Bureau major , who had discovered an Aetheri insignia on the wreckage of Nura's ship, and he went to the Imperium Vigil Bureau headquarters to check it out. When Velgor arrived, the major asked what rank he should address him by, and Velgor said, "lord." The major explained to Velgor their suspicions that the explosion was premature and the Aetheri insignia was merely "rabble-rousing graffiti" used as a calling card. Velgor told him he was wrong, and as the major explained his experience in intelligence, Velgor used Synth Flux to reconstruct Nura's ship, showing the major that he was indeed wrong about the cause of the explosion. Velgor then ordered him to search every level of Vantor, steering clear of the Aetheri Sanctum.

Meanwhile, Nura was about to vacate the temple when she noticed the Grand Warden insulting the Aetheri knowledge he was examining. His comments eventually drove her over the edge and she confronted him with her flux blade. Velgor arrived at the temple entrance and Replica Commander Fox already had sealed the place. When Fox asked him what they were expecting to happen and Velgor told him that they had missed an Aetheri during Directive 66. As Velgor entered the temple, Nura was pinning down the Grand Warden with her flux blade and threatening him. The Grand Warden asked how she did not remember who he was, stating the times she had denied him the Aetheri knowledge he sought. Nura attacked him, kicking off a short duel where the Grand Warden quickly outwitted her. The Grand Warden would have killed Nura if not for Velgor's arrival. As Velgor stopped the Grand Warden from slaying the Aetheri, Nura used Synth Flux to rain Aetheri books on them, and she fled the scene.

Velgor made chase and as he followed Nura's trail, she made it to the archive vault and used a terminal to purge the entire library of its knowledge. As Velgor entered the vault, he was confronted by the droid guard, Cator . He identified Velgor as Kael Drayden and told him he was not authorized to be there. Velgor threw his flux blade at the droid, but being a guard against possible Synth Flux-sensitive threats meant the droid was ready for that move, and it caught Velgor's weapon. Velgor then punched Cator's arm off, and the droid caught his wrist and squeezed it with its robotic hand. Velgor drove his fist through Cator's face before the droid could damage him any more. But as Cator dropped to the floor, Velgor noticed a powerful shot being fired at him and used his flux blade to deflect it. The shot, fired by Nura using a flux blade rifle , knocked Velgor back as it ripped through a bookcase. Nura stood at the entrance of the vault, confronting Velgor.

As Velgor got up again, he tried to convince Nura to drop her weapon. Nura ignored him and fired again. Velgor dodged her shot and she fired a third time, further devastating the vault. As she continued firing, she tried to convince Velgor that he was just a tool to the Emperor, saying that he could never get rid of the light side of Synth Flux as it always found its vessels. Nura's rifle then failed to keep firing, and Velgor deactivated his flux blade, telling her she would not be harmed. Nura's rifle then began beeping and she threw it towards Velgor. He used Synth Flux to push it away, and it exploded, throwing both of them to the floor and breaching the side of the temple.

Nura fled through the fresh hole in the wall to find newly alerted replica troopers around the perimeter. They fired at her and she jumped down, deflecting their shots back at them. As the replicas put more pressure on her, she Synth Flux-pushed them over the ledge behind them. Velgor then appeared through the wall and the replicas assumed he was too an Aetheri and fired on him. At that moment, Commander Fox approach the scene on an Imperial Patrol Ship and realized that they were firing on Velgor. He then had his ship fly over to Velgor and ordered his units to a ceasefire. Velgor jumped onto the ship and when Fox tried to explain the misunderstanding, he used Synth Flux to break his neck.

The replicas on the ground continued firing on Nura and managed to disarm her. She then found herself cornered by Velgor and the replicas, and she attempted to jump off, only for Velgor to use Synth Flux to catch her. He brought her to his ship, and there she told the replicas that he was the Aetheri Knight Kael Drayden. Velgor used Synth Flux to throw the replicas off the ship and when Nura asked of what became of her, he killed her. Velgor then crashed the ship and contacted Mal, telling him that Nura died in an attempt to escape. With Nura having revealed Mal would create more umbra side agents with the list and questioning what it was that Velgor truly wanted before her death, Velgor then crushed the list of Synth Flux-sensitive children that she had with her. In due time, Velgor would meet and begin training other Wardens, including the Fourth Warden and Tenth Warden . Under his tutelage, the Warden Order began to fight far more aggressively. According to the Grand Warden, Velgor's methods, combined with quarrels between the Wardens, turned the training pit into a "slaughterhouse." Still, Velgor largely stopped being a fatal danger to the Wardens after each agent's initial duel; in that clash, he would take it upon himself to maim, and kill if needed, a prospective Warden, but afterward he would allow them to live if they passed his first test.

#### The Weapon of the Umbra

> " The Umbra's weapon is not the flux blade. It is the umbra side of Synth Flux and the umbra side touches everything. The blade is merely a symbol. A flame, signifying the great blaze of the umbra side. The inferno that consumes all who come against it. All. " " I understand, my master. " ―Vael Mal and Xarn Velgor

Velgor followed his master to The Works district on Vantor, where the two prepared for a sparring match in which Mal intended to teach him a lesson. Mal ignited one of his flux blades and told Velgor to fight him, to which Velgor ignited his own blade before his master charged against him. As the blades of the two Umbra clashed, Velgor was quickly overpowered by Mal, who knocked the blade out of his hand and quickly pushed him away with Synth Flux.

As Velgor was on the ground, his blade was grabbed by Mal while he told him that he still fought like an Aetheri. His master told him that the flux blade was not his only weapon and that the Umbra side of Synth Flux was his true weapon, a weapon that touches everything. Velgor was then hit by multiple pieces of wreckage that Mal lifted with Synth Flux while telling Velgor that the flux blade was merely a symbol of the inferno that consumed all who came against the Umbra side. On his knees, Velgor told his master that he understood his lesson.

#### Training the Thirteenth Warden

Clash in the training pit

> " A unique attempt. But not good enough. " ―Xarn Velgor, to Iskat Vekkis

Meanwhile, Iskat Vekkis, who had yet to embrace her Inquisitorial identity as the Thirteenth Warden, fully recovered from the injuries she had sustained at the hands of the Seventh Warden, which had led to her time in the medbay and encounter with the Sixth Warden after Velgor severed his arm. Making her way to the Inquisitorial training pit, the healed Vekkis attempted to spar with her fellow Wardens once again and was clashing with the Fifth Warden when Velgor entered with the Sixth Warden, Tenth Warden , Fourth Warden , and Eighth Warden at his back: Velgor's presence filled Synth Flux with a sense of darkness that threatened to overwhelm Vekkis, although she also got a sense of familiarity from the being thanks to her past encounters with Kael Drayden, but his former identity remained unknown to her. Meanwhile, the Fifth Warden shrunk back in fear of the Dark Lord and greeted his arrival, although Velgor decided to focus on the newly arrived Vekkis.

Commanding with a sense of power that taught her immediately not to question him, Velgor ordered the fearful Vekkis to test her blade and then attack him, to which she realized he would kill her if she did not comply. Rather than directly attack, she began the duel by throwing the Fifth Warden into Velgor's path, to which Velgor responded by immediately sidestepping the thrown Warden. As Vekkis had predicted that, however, she then used Synth Flux to throw the Fifth Warden's flux blade at Velgor's helmet and only attacked herself when the Dark Lord was focused on both the Fifth Warden's body and his incoming blade. Vekkis herself entered the duel by throwing her own flux blade at Velgor and unsheathing a vibroblade she kept hidden in her boot in an effort to stab his groin, only for Velgor to stop her and hold her in the air before she could make any kind of impact. Velgor, thus, decided that she had failed. Before he could declare as such, however, she again tried to divide his focus by telekinetically throwing her flux blade at him and, while he was distracted, stabbing him with the vibroblade. Again, however, Vekkis failed as the blade only made contact with metal.

Once more, Velgor decided that Vekkis had failed. He crushed her hand with his own, causing her to drop the blade, and lifted her into the air by her broken hand. He also effortlessly batted away her incoming flux blade. While Velgor was impressed by the unique tactics Vekkis had employed, he declared they had not been good enough before he cleaved off her long hair, burning her back in the process. Vekkis was consumed by fear and understood Velgor would either kill her or further maim her, a decision that his expressionless mask hid, before she realized she could use his mechanics to her advantage. Velgor suddenly found that several wires in his mask became undone via Synth Flux and let out a choked gasp, hurriedly dropping Vekkis and his own flux blade as he reached for his helmet before he reclaimed his flux blade and departed the training room. Despite the setback, Velgor left Vekkis with a reminder that, even if he was desperate, he could kill her: from outside the room, Velgor telekinetically threw her into the wall with enough strength to nearly kill her.

Impact on Vekkis

> " You have done well, Iskat Vekkis. " " I am Iskat Vekkis no more. Call me Thirteenth Warden. " ―Xarn Velgor and Iskat Vekkis, who finally accepted her station as the Thirteenth Warden

Vekkis' trial against Velgor impressed the Ninth Warden because Vekkis had not lost a limb to the Dark Lord. However, several other members of the Warden Order were unimpressed with her performance. The encounter with Velgor not only revealed his existence to Vekkis but also showed her that each of their number had faced Velgor, with Vekkis further believing that Velgor must have killed several Wardens during training before her. After the encounter, she was forced to spend yet more time healing due to her crushed hand. The medical droids warned her that Velgor had destroyed the bones so greatly that it could become infected. She also had little choice but to shave down her hair after Velgor had sliced so much of it off and injured her back, although she came to find that those burns faded under medical care and she was afforded greatly mobility with less hair. Vekkis also accepted over her early time as a Warden that the only people she was to apologize to were the Grand Warden, Velgor, and the Emperor, and she believed such apologies should only come when her life was on the line.

After her hand healed, Vekkis encountered Tualon Veyra , a fellow former Aetheri Knight whom she had once harbored feelings for and whom left for dead during Directive 66 . Veyra was newly recruited into the Warden Order himself and tortured into joining, although Vekkis initially feared that she was seeing things due to an infection in her hand during their reunion. In their subsequent, emotionally-charged training duel, Vekkis pondered if she would warn Veyra about Velgor and, overall, went easy on him, nonetheless understanding that Velgor and their fellow Wardens would not do the same. However, Veyra concluded the duel by stabbing her through one of her hearts, much to her anger—still, the wound made her reconsider those inflicted upon her by Velgor as they appeared instructional compared to how personal the attack Veyra unleashed had been. After recovering from that wound, she finally had a chance to undertake her first operation as a Warden via her mission to the planet Firrhana . During the mission, she discovered that Veyra had tracked his own Aetheri quarry to Firrhana; by that point, Veyra was aware of Velgor and warned her not to disappoint him and cause him to kill her for losing her starship.

During the mission, Vekkis confronted several reminders of her past in the form of the surviving Aetheri Knight Charlin Plaka and her longtime mentor, Aetheri Master Klefan Opus . She killed Plaka with a Synth Flux choke, which she pondered if she had been testing when she pulled the wires in Velgor's helmet, and used her flux blades to cut down Opus, against whom she relied on the aggression Velgor had taught her to embrace. She also killed a Zeltron Aetheri , whom she suspected to be the Aetheri Veyra had been assigned to chase. Velgor was waiting for her return with the Grand Warden inside the latter's chambers when Vekkis returned to Vantor. After Vekkis affirmed that she had succeeded in killing her targets by presenting Velgor and the Grand Warden with the flux blades of each fallen Aetheri, Velgor declared that she had done well, to which she responded by accepting her station as the "Thirteenth Warden."

#### Defiance from the Imperial hierarchy

Hunted by the Chas

> " Do you remember Cabarria , Velgor? Two decades ago, on a lonely stretch of skyway. A family. A man, a woman, and a girl of 12. You killed my mother. Her name was Ramat Cha . You killed my father. His name was Bhada Cha . My name is Chanath Cha, and that day, I vowed to end your life. " ―Chanath Cha

Believing that he lost all that he cared about by his own hand, Xarn Velgor embraced his role as the Emperor's chief enforcer. Few knew who he was, and even fewer suspected that he had once been Kael Drayden. Some rumors circulated about Velgor's unexpected entry into the Imperial authority. According to certain high-ranking Imperials, he was a counterpart to the late Dissident warlord General Skarn, whom Mal had held in reserve, while others speculated that he was a technologically modified warrior trained in the now-forbidden arts of Synth Flux. His appearance at the Imperial court and the favor he carried from the Emperor earned him the distrust of Imperial officers . They resented him for appearing out of nowhere and having authority over them because of his link to his Master. They also resented his heavy-handed treatment of them, such as when he used Synth Flux in the presence of his Master to choke Colonel Barokki. In secret, Barokki and another Imperial officer plotted to assassinate Velgor , whose position in the new Imperium they did not understand.

For Velgor, the situation came to a head when he investigated an Aetheri sighting on Cabarria . Accompanied by the Ninth Warden , he headed to the bar , where the identification had happened. Although the Ninth Warden had originally been planning to investigate alone, Velgor had come, claiming she was not yet ready to face an Aetheri in combat but secretly because he wanted to fight another Aetheri. The Ninth Warden commented on his true desire, using her empathic abilities to read him clearly. As a result, Velgor ordered her to remain outside while he investigated. Once inside, the Dark Lord was attacked by a family of bounty hunters consisting of Bhada , Ramat , and their twelve year old daughter Chanath Cha . Using ion grenades , they neutralized all the weapons in the bar while Chanath tried to seize Velgor's flux blade with a tractor rifle . However, he used Synth Flux to seize the flux blade midair and, under the strain of the two opposing forces, the hilt shattered, leaving only the red kyber crystal.

The Chas told the assembled customers that they had come only for the "Aetheri" and that they were not planning on harming anyone. However, a disarmed Velgor disagreed and used Synth Flux to turn the customers into projectiles aimed at the Chas. Taken by the surprise, the bounty hunters turned on their shields to protect themselves. As they attacked him, Velgor also used the customers and the bar's furniture as shields. Eventually, the hunters realized they could not defeat him and made to flee. Reclaiming his crystal with Synth Flux, Velgor pursued them. Emerging from the bar, Velgor initially suspected that the Wardens had been behind the attempt on his life and threatened the Ninth Warden with her own flux blade. The Warden's denials did not sway the Dark Lord, but neither did they prove her guilt. Determined to find who had placed a bounty on his head, he pursued the Chas. They eluded him for a time until he crashed a massive vehicle into their path, causing their speeder to crash. Seizing Chanath in a Synth Flux choke, he commanded they tell him who had ordered his death.

Understanding they and their daughter were at risk, they told him about an anonymous bounty with an exorbitant price posted on the Hunter Net . Ramat Cha, a talented slicer , offered to uncover their employer for him in exchange for their daughter's life. When they demanded that he release Chanath first, Velgor refused. However, the Chas refused to work if their daughter was killed, further declaring that they would happily die as well to exist in death with her. Grudgingly impressed, the Dark Lord demanded proof that Ramat could deliver her side of the deal, which she proved by tracking the signal of the bounty back to Vantor. Velgor released Chanath as agreed, at which point the bounty hunters made their daughter swear that she would seek no vengeance against the Dark Lord, a deal Velgor chose to honor. To complete the deal, Ramat provided Velgor with the information he had requested and gave him the source of the bounty on him: the Imperial Executive Building on Vantor, specifically the Emperor's office.

Velgor then killed both Cha parents right in front of their daughter, who was filled with rage. Despite her oath to not hold a grudge, she ignored her promise and spent her life hunting the Dark Lord in revenge. Eventually, as the years passed on, the Umbra Lord himself simply forgot the murders he carried out on Cabarria. Cha, however, never forgot and became a skilled bounty hunter, studying every report she could and speaking to anyone she could find who had witnessed Velgor in battle. She trained herself to counter Velgor's abilities, eventually enlisting a team of fellow hunters as "the Orphans " to help her in her quest. Cha's skills rose to a level that even Mal took notice, becoming a bounty hunter the Emperor could trust all in the name of possibly getting close to Velgor, only to never see him. In effect, Velgor had completely upended Cha's life, only for the crusade her life turned into to not impact Velgor at all.

Cementing his place

> " Still, I understand that our shared vision remains new, and perhaps some elements of the hierarchy remain unclear. It is time to elimate any confusion. This is Lord Velgor. He speaks with my voice. A command from him is a command from me. Pass this information down to your men. All must understand. " " Ferro. Bingan. Strephi. Azoras. Barokki. Step forward. Two attempts have been made on my life. Evidence suggests a conspiracy within the Imperial Officer Corps. I do not yet know the precise individuals behind these attempts… although I have my suspicions. It does not matter. I will always survive. Every time, I will survive. But five of you, chosen at random… [Synth Flux chokes the five] will not. " ―Theron Mal and Xarn Velgor speak to the Imperial Officer Corps

With their business on Cabarria completed, Velgor and the Ninth Warden left the planet and returned to Vantor. Now suspecting that the Emperor had placed the bounty on his head, the Dark Lord planned to confront his Master about his intentions. As the Ninth Warden piloted the ship through hyperspace, Velgor worked on creating a new flux blade. His droid meanwhile investigated the origins of the kill contract placed on him and confirmed that the order could have come from the Emperor's office, with no evidence of slicing to indicate foul play. But, as the ship arrived in orbit over Vantor, the ship's attempt to transmit clearance codes for landing was jammed and the ship itself was marked as being part of a Dissident splinter cell . The Imperial capital's orbital defenses targeted Velgor's ship. Only the Dark Lord's skill as a pilot prevented them from being destroyed, although the ship was damaged and forced to crash land on Vantor. Tired of the repeated attempts on his life, the Dark Lord headed to the former Aetheri Sanctum to confront the Emperor.

The Emperor was investigating the hoard of artifacts that Jocasta Nura had collected and stored in the Aetheri Archives when Velgor arrived. The Emperor sensed that his apprentice did not wish to discuss his recent discovery, although he did praise him for having found the Librarian's stash. Velgor informed his master of the events on Cabarria and his investigation into the kill order against him, before directly asking his Master whether he had placed it on his head as part of a test. Noticing Velgor's new flux blade, the Emperor asked to see it, approving of its design. He also chastised his apprentice for believing that he would resort to such methods as placing a bounty on his head if he wanted to have him killed and that he did not "test": he "taught." He also told Velgor that finding the assassins was his task, not theirs. This echoed a previous lesson he had taught Velgor when he had "disciplined" Colonel Barokki: that they must not kill everyone in the galaxy, but that having power meant they could, and that they would never lack for people to destroy.

Velgor returned to his quarters, where he meditated on the past events and his Master's words. Thinking back to all that had happened, he eventually realized the cause of the conspiracy against him was that his sudden elevation had caused concern and fear among the ranks of the Imperial officers, who knew that he had "strength," but not that he shared the Emperor's "power." Despite a lack of proof, Velgor became convinced that Colonel Barokki, who had so suddenly borne his displeasure, must be involved in the conspiracy. The Dark Lord met with the Emperor and shared his conclusions, and requested that he be publicly recognized and allowed to show them the consequences of opposing him. The Emperor asked whether he was sure, and Velgor confirmed it: he would not kill them all unless he had to, but would not allow them to act against him with impunity. The Emperor agreed to Velgor's plan, but forbade him from killing Varrix, as he was essential to his plans.

The Emperor gathered the elite officers of the growing Imperial Military to clarify the new hierarchy. He formally introduced Velgor as his emissary, who spoke with his voice and whose commands would need to be obeyed as if they had come from the Emperor himself. The Emperor then moved aside and allowed Velgor to speak: the Dark Lord summoned five officers before him: Corin Ferro , Zorta Bingan , Joon Strephi , Tomas Azoras , and Barokki. Informing them that the two attempts on his life had been made from within the Officer Corps and that he had his suspicions who had been responsible, he instituted a new rule: for every assassination attempt he survived, and he warned them that he would always survive, five officers chosen at random would be executed. He instantly executed the five designated officers with a Synth Flux choke before departing, leaving the remaining officers stunned and afraid. With his position in the hierarchy established, Velgor did not need to hold an official military rank. Instead, he acted as the de facto Commander-in-Chief of the Imperial Military as per his position as the Emperor's right hand man. Velgor would also come to be recognized as part of both the Joint Chiefs of the Imperial Military and the Imperial Ruling Council , of which he was considered a ranking member. After his demonstration before the officer corps, Velgor returned to his personal suite and mediated, cracking the window in his anger that positive thoughts still came.

Velgor's high status also gave him the ability to alter Imperial records under the off-the-books Directive 081-Omaya . He was also made privy to the secret construction of the Void Core, construction of which was to be overseen by the likes of Wilhard Varrix and Orren Callan Vossik . However, he was not informed of the full scope of the project nor its power. Shortly after his rebirth as a cyborg, Velgor joined the Emperor and Varrix aboard a newly-constructed Vorash -class Iron Cruiser , which journeyed directly from the Core Reaches to Pyralis so they could survey the under construction Void Core over Pyralis. The personnel aboard the ship were so high ranking that it was permitted to arrive at Pyralis without the normal security checks from Sentinel Base . While he himself was not personally aboard the ship, Vossik was aware of Velgor by that point, understanding that he was a new member of the Imperial court who was already feared by many. However, Vossik himself was not yet sure if Velgor was more threatening than Grand Vizier Mazan Veldon.

#### War on Mon Calvarra

One of Velgor's earliest missions on the Emperor's behalf was to effect an execution on Murkhana . In 18 BVE , one year into the reign of the Imperium, Velgor was reliving his duel on Emberfall through meditation. During his experience, he was already in his cybernetic suit, and he used Synth Flux to throw lava at Orien. Orien jumped up to dodge it, but Velgor Synth Flux-choked him and threw him into the rocks. Velgor watched Orien burn before he awoke in his meditation chamber as the Emperor asked for him over the comm. In his throne room, the Emperor told Velgor that a year after his ascension, it was time to show that his voice was the only voice within the Imperium. Velgor asked how, and the Emperor told him that Mon Calvarra would make a great example of the price of defiance. He further told Velgor that King Liarch may have had an adviser helping him with his negotiations with the Imperium. Velgor asked if it could be an Aetheri, and the Emperor said it was possible. He told Velgor to take a team to investigate this and claimed the military aspects of the operation would be left to Moff Varrix.

Velgor arrived at Mon Calvarra aboard the Zeta -class shuttle Infernum . With him, he took three Wardens, the Sixth Warden, Ninth Warden, and Tenth Warden , and a death squad of Purge Troopers . At this time, the Imperial ambassador Telvar had just finished another meeting with Liarch, and the man was walking to his shuttle when he saw Velgor descend from the ramp of his shuttle. The Ninth Warden struggled to get past the king's Chief of Security , Gial Vorian, and Velgor confronted the Calavane, asking if he had read the writ that the Ninth Warden had given him. Vorian confirmed that he had, but demanded that he knew their business as he had a job to do and so did Velgor. Velgor complied and explained about a possible enemy of the Imperium on Mon Calvarra. As he said that, Telvar's shuttle took off and flew for Varrix's Iron Cruiser, the Sovereign —which was in orbit above Mon Calvarra. However, as Vorian denied the possibility of an enemy of the Imperium being on Mon Calvarra, Telvar's shuttle exploded.

Telvar's assassination prompted Varrix to enact a full-scale invasion of Mon Calvarra . His forces landed at Dac City , where Velgor had arrived, and laid siege to the Calavane and Quarren defenses. Velgor and his team sprang to life and soon seized the landing pad where the Infernum was parked. He then led them to the Dac City palace to find Liarch and ask him about the possible Aetheri. They fought their way through the palace guards and entered the palace, where the Ninth Warden began torturing Liarch for the location of the Aetheri. However, another threat loomed over them. A massive tidal wave had suddenly erupted as a part of Liarch's defensive plan. The Sixth Warden warned them of this, and he and the Tenth Warden began shielding Velgor and the Ninth Warden from the wave using Synth Flux. When their attempts began failing, Velgor joined them, but it was not enough. The group was washed away by the wave, and Dac City was left in ruins.

Velgor awoke at the bottom of the Great Ungeness Trench . As his oxygen supply was dwindling, he used Synth Flux to create an air bubble for himself. Suddenly, he was attacked by a squid-like creature , and after wrestling with it, he killed it with Synth Flux. Velgor then rode its body using Synth Flux and eventually picked up a transmission from the Wardens. Velgor asked them to come to his position, and they did so in an Imperial submarine . When they picked him up, he asked about the Aetheri, and the Wardens said that he was still alive somewhere. Velgor then asked about Liarch, and the Ninth Warden said that they did not know, but she then revealed that she had gotten the Aetheri's location from him. Knowing exactly where their target was, they began traveling for the Aetheri.

Velgor and his team arrived at the Aetheri's location to find the individual and his disciples evacuating. The Aetheri, an Iktotchi Adept called Ferren Barr , had been notified by Liarch after he was recovered following the events at Dac City. The group of them immediately scattered, but one of the disciples instead attacked the submarine head-on with his volt pistol. The shots bounced off the window, and Velgor used Synth Flux to crush his helmet. The other fled in the direction of Bel City , and as Velgor caught up with them, another disciple turned around to attack them. He too died in the process but managed to slow Velgor's submarine, and the rest of the group followed Barr to Bel City. Velgor and the Wardens arrived at Bel City and marched down the hallway, reinforced by the Wardens and the Purge troopers. Velgor was then contacted by Varrix, who was struggling to deal with the grueling conflict on Mon Calvarra. Velgor told Varrix that he was busy, but Varrix knew that and asked him to do something else instead: capture Liarch. Velgor told Varrix that he did not answer to him, but Varrix replied that capturing Liarch was the only way to end the fighting quickly on Mon Calvarra, and he pledged to owe Velgor a favor if he did so. Velgor agreed and told the Wardens to continue the hunt as he left.

Velgor arrived at Liarch's Royal Command Bunker and killed his guards. He then disarmed Liarch and contacted Varrix, who told him to take Liarch to a window and send him his exact location. Velgor asked Varrix if he was going to send a shuttle, and the man said not yet. Once Velgor did what he asked, Varrix began a bombardment of Mon Calvarra. Liarch watched in horror but refused to surrender. The two were interrupted by Barr, who had outsmarted and scattered Velgor's team. Barr confronted Velgor and the two dueled . As they fought, Barr revealed that he had assassinated Telvar to send the Calavane into war. Liarch overheard this and realized his mistake in following Barr. He ran to his terminal and sent a message to the Calavane and Quarren forces, ordering them to cease fire. Liarch then contacted Varrix, notifying him of the surrender. Varrix sent a shuttle to pick him up but continued the bombardment.

Velgor and Barr continued fighting, and Barr began laughing. Liarch questioned this, and Barr said that he had completed his plan. Barr prophesied that the Calavane vessels would be at the forefront of a great insurgency decades from then, and decades after that. Velgor told Barr that he was not an Aetheri anymore, and Barr told him that that made two of them, saying that he still beat the Umbra. Velgor then slew him, and the shuttle arrived to pick up Liarch. The king told Velgor that Mon Calvarra would rise again, but Velgor told him that there was no Mon Calvarra, only the Imperium. Some time after the mission, Velgor ordered to imprison and torture the people of Mon Calvarra.

#### Investigation on Pyralis

Eventually, Velgor decided he needed to know more about the Void Core, codenamed Project Stardust , and began investigating, which led him to the data vault on Halcyon . There, he found information on Project Stardust and studied its design before he was interrupted by Varrix, who was very displeased with Velgor's snooping. Varrix told him he was not authorized to be there, and Velgor claimed that Synth Flux was all the authorization he needed. Velgor denied that he was Varrix's beast but the man denied his claim and taunted Velgor, saying he did not need Synth Flux to know he was very angry. Varrix then produced a hologram of the Emperor, who told Velgor he did not recall sending him to Halcyon. Velgor apologized and told the Emperor he only sought to study the ways in which the Imperium would extend its rule over the galaxy. The Emperor told Velgor of a new mission he was to go on. There had been certain instances of sabotage during the Void Core's construction that Velgor needed to discover the source of before it got worse. The Emperor further told Velgor that Varrix had full authority over him and that if he disappointed him, he disappointed Mal.

Velgor arrived at Pyralis and met with Commander Orren Callan Vossik. As they greeted, an overhead rock formation exploded and rained down on them. Velgor saved himself and Vossik and they went to his office to discuss the matter. Vossik claimed it was Varrix sabotaging his efforts in order to get rid of him and take over the project. Velgor asked him if there was any proof and Vossik was not able to produce anything. Vossik then explained to him the incidences that had occurred in the last three months. Velgor gave his knowledge on the situation to loyalty officer Sid Uddra , who met him at the Petranaki Arena to discuss what she suspected. She told Velgor that as much as Varrix and Vossik's rivalry arose tension between them, it was unlikely for either to be the culprit. She then offered Velgor with another individual to look into: the scientist Galen Walton Arsa . Velgor looked through his office and found many kyber crystals, which revealed to him that the Void Core must be a planet-killer superweapon. He then found a recording of Arsa and his family . Velgor also noticed that Arsa called his daughter stardust, which was the codename for the Void Core's project. Finally, Velgor found an ootheca egg in a box. Knowing that Geonosians were a slave race and were forbidden from breeding freely, he confronted Vossik about this. Vossik denied that it was him and suggested that some of the slaves may have sent it as a distress call to Arsa. Velgor then deduced for himself the saboteurs at hand.

Velgor and a squad of death troopers entered a Pyralian hive and found the queen and her minions. The two sides engaged but Velgor quickly came out on top, slaying the queen. After the battle, he returned to Varrix on the Carrion Spike and reported his findings to him. Varrix asked Velgor how he knew the queen's hive had orchestrated the sabotages and Velgor told how their slicers had confirmed the hive to have installed corrupted tech into production, ensuring accidents. Varrix then asked Velgor how he may have dealt with them and Velgor said that they were animals and so they were slaughtered like animals. Varrix finally asked Velgor if there was anything else and he stated to Varrix that his project, the Void Core, was able to destroy a planet. Varrix asked how he knew and he told him of the kyber crystals he saw in Arsa's office. Varrix then asked Velgor why he was not happy for this revelation, and Velgor said that he distrusted his reliance on technological aberration. Varrix then told Velgor that he was one of those before realizing that Velgor wanted to be the only technological aberration. Velgor warned Varrix that the Void Core may become his tomb, and when Varrix asked if that was a threat, Velgor told him it was only a prediction.

### Accepting the umbra side

#### The Emperor's emissary

> " This galaxy is vast, Lord Velgor. I must rule it from a distance, through proxies, promises and threats. It is rare that I am able to take a direct hand against my enemies. Those tasks, I have left largely to you . " ―Theron Mal, to Xarn Velgor

Over the next two decades of the Imperium's reign, Velgor would remain one of the few who enjoyed direct access to Mal. He was Mal's preferred envoy for all matters that the Emperor took a direct interest in. With Mal forced to spend most of his time governing the galaxy at large, it was Velgor who was tasked with taking a direct hand against the Imperium's enemies, becoming known as the " Emperor's Fist " to both Imperials and, eventually, criminals. Such matters included the elimination of insurgent uprisings and the continued construction of the Void Core . One Imperial captain who served under Velgor in the immediate aftermath of the Replica Wars alleged to a later subordinate, Yrica Quell , that Velgor "matured" over the years: while during his first days as an Imperial enforcer Velgor would simply murder out of annoyance, Velgor came to wait for a subordinate to commit a mistake, albeit sometimes a trivial one, to have an excuse to kill. The Imperial officers remained wary of Velgor and his power, as well as his continued devotion to the ancient ways of Synth Flux, but none rose to challenge him without feeling the consequences.

Despite the mistrust and even hatred he generated among the officers, Velgor was held in high esteem by the Shock trooper Corps , as he often fought alongside them at the front line rather than remaining in command centers. As the Emperor's enforcer, Velgor retained command of the unit he had commanded during the Replica Wars as Kael Drayden: the 501st Legion. When the Emperor retired the replica troopers, the rank and file of the 501st were composed of recruited shock troopers, some of whom received training from the Dark Lord himself, such as Sergeant Kreel , an undercover agent whom Velgor trained in flux blade combat. Through their missions on Velgor's behalf, the 501st came to be known as "Velgor's Fist." On one occasion, the shock trooper Terex met Velgor, becoming terrified of him while also respecting him. Velgor also garnered strong support from the pilots of the new Imperial Navy .

Meanwhile, Velgor continued on his hunt for surviving Aetheri with his Wardens, constantly putting himself to work to that end. Thanks to their efforts, most surviving Aetheri were successfully located and then cut down as the Imperial Era continued. However, Velgor's successes against the Aetheri left him with few in the galaxy who could offer him a real challenge. While he was willing to kill Wardens for failure, Velgor stopped being a day-to-day danger to the Wardens as each present member of the group had passed his initial test, in which he might remove a Warden's limbs or kill them. If they survived that duel, Velgor was willing to work with the Warden and treated then as a mere servants to his will, although it was more than clear that a fierce punishment awaited those who disappointed him. The Thirteenth Warden, for her part, undertook dozens of operations over her career and continued to work under Velgor, whom she never became truly comfortable with, but she also believed they shared many similarities, including a hatred for the Aetheri and what she saw as the Order's unforgiveable crimes. In her time serving under him, she managed not to disappoint Velgor, something she desperately avoided.

As much as fighting Aetheri provided Velgor an outlet as he could challenge himself by fighting them, the Thirteenth Warden noticed that most hunts Velgor undertook ended relatively quickly, with Velgor swiftly overpowering and killing his target before moving onto the next one, keeping himself constantly at work. One target sought after by the Wardens and Velgor was Verla , the wayward apprentice of Ferran Barr. On several occasions, Velgor nearly caught Verla, who nonetheless always managed to escape him and the Wardens. One encounter involved Velgor chasing Verla through a rocky environment. Ultimately, the constant chase and her life on the run, which involved abandoning people she became close to or having to deal with the Imperial seekers killing them, became too much for Verla, who decided to abandon her Aetheri path. On one occasion, Velgor stood over the bodies of a number of victims with his flux blade still ignited, an image that embodied his status as an evil monster to beings like Verla.

At some point, Velgor was deployed to put down coalition on the mineral rich world of Bandomeer , where miners were fighting back against Imperial exploitation. Velgor punished them for their insurgency. Apparitions of Velgor and other dark siders were once faced by surviving Aetheri Vanzell Mar-Klar and Nedriss Narr , who attempted to train a new generation of younglings after Directive 66. Having killed Tambra along with the rest of the Dissident Council during his mission to Emberfall, Velgor was also dispatched to Skako Minor to kill the Artificer Union Foreman's remaining loyalists by wiping out the planet's Ruling Council . Massacring the council officials, including Tambra's son Wat Tambra II , Velgor established a new Imperial-loyal regime on the planet, which also fell under Imperial occupation .

#### Fighting the Shadow

> " My lord. I can confirm the presence of Vreth. He is more dangerous than we suspected. It seems he is in league with two Aetheri. How should I proceed? " ―The First Brother contacting Xarn Velgor

Between 17 BVE and c. 13 BVE , Xarn Velgor received a communication from one of his most proficient Wardens, the First Brother , whom had been dispatched to kill the resurfaced Vreth on the Mid Reach world Janix . The Warden reported having faced the rogue Umbra fugitive in combat alongside a surviving Aetheri Adept, thought both had escaped from his grasp. Asking his master for his next moves, Velgor sent another Warden, the Eleventh Warden , to work alongside First Brother to eradicate their targets. Upon learning both First Brother and the Eleventh Warden had failed once more to end Vreth after encountering the Gloomreachian warrior, Velgor saw fit to handle the matter personally. Placing himself on the forest lying outside the planet's capital perimeter, Velgor remained by the fog-covered area in hiding as the Wardens drove his prey towards him. Learning that a squad consisting of Vreth and his allies would soon reach the forest, Velgor made his way towards the cave tunnel connecting the city to the forest.

Soon two Ferrik super commandos came into view and Velgor quickly ended one of them before recalling back the second, Rook Kast , into the fog with Synth Flux. Choking her and snapping her neck, Velgor allowed his mechanical breath to be heard in the eerily quiet forest as he walked up to his foes. Appearing out of the fog, Velgor calmly ignited his flux blade as he stared right at Vreth, Master Eeko-Dio Daki , Adept Devon Izara and four volt pistol wielding escapees. Not saying a word, the Umbra lord firmly met a strike from Vreth as the Nightbrother ran straight at the cyborg and blocked the blow without effort. Pushing back Vreth, Velgor easily engaged all three duelist in a flux blade duel. Quickly, Velgor batted his armed adversaries away with his overpowering strength and exploited a moment to hit Vreth's cybernetic leg, carving a small gash through the metal and circuitry. Keeping the Aetheri and former Umbra away using only his blade, Velgor easily deflected the shots fired by the trio's allies and blew all seven outlaws away with a Synth Flux Push. As his enemies realised their futility fighting him, Velgor pursued as the group ran towards a ruined temple.

Confronting the fugitives, Velgor blocks a flux blade strike from Vreth, who comments he is no Warden but Velgor pushes him back and Izara and Darki attack him with Vreth. Overpowering the three combatants, the other four open fire but Velgor synth flux pushes them back. As the four head to the rendezvous point, the three flux blade opponents fight Velgor but are forced to flee. In a ruins, Velgor find Daki and Vreth, with Izara being sent by her master to help the Lawsons , Two-Boots , and Looti Vario but runs into the Wardens. As Daki fights the First Brother and Izara fights the Eleventh Warden, Velgor duels Vreth, who ask what Velgor is. After Daki defeats the First Brother he helps Vreth but Velgor proved to be to powerful, repeatedly hitting Vreth's cybernetic leg and throwing him onto Daki. Eventually, Vreth abandons Daki, which was witness by Brander Lawson before battling the TK troopers to protect his son Rylee Lawson and droid friend Two-Boots.

Fighting Daki one-on-one, the Aetheri Master managed to land a hit on Velgor's left shoulder but the Umbra Lord overpowers Daki before killing him via stabbing him in the chest. Izara witness her master's death and fights the Eleventh Warden more aggressively but loses her flux blade . Vreth comes and helps her, overpowering the Eleventh Warden, causing the Warden to flee in the ruins where Velgor nearly kills Izara but Vreth causing the temple ruins to collapse on Velgor, which he deflects but is buried underneath. Velgor managed to go out of the cascading temple unfazed, only covered in dust and watched as the criminals' shuttle from Dryden Vossa , flew up through the atmosphere and escaped into hyperspace before the pursuing gunships shot it down, resulting in Velgor failing to complete Mal's order. Nevertheless, he had proved himself a greater apprentice to his master's power by defeating and forcing the Emperor's first student to flee.

#### Specter of death

> " Activate the range corps. Equip mobile bacta units. And prepare a note for the record . Uokara . A fertile world …with one inhabitant . Until today . " ―Xarn Velgor

At some point, a human dreamed about being pursued and killed by Velgor. On Torku at one time, Velgor pursued Davgar Llian , a scientist allied with insurgents who had sabotaged an Imperial weapon. Velgor, aware of Llian’s role in the sabotage, attacked once the scientist brought the weapon online to target local Imperial base, killing the soldiers and confronting the scientist. Noting that only Llian was capable of reversing the sabotage, Velgor mockingly thanked him for doing so before strangling him with Synth Flux.

Velgor surveyed Uokara, a planet in the Unknown Regions, for signs of life on behalf of a director, who wished to commence a mining operation. At some point, Velgor unknowingly breathed in Ghymnon, a sentient pathogen native to Uokara that had lived there for millions of years. Reporting the world as uninhabited, Velgor was quarantined in a bacta tank upon his return by a 2-1B medical droid in accordance with standard procedure following contact with a class IV planet, much to the Umbra Lord's displeasure. Within his mind, Velgor confronted Ghymnon and defeated it. Velgor ordered the droid to catalog Uokara’s lone, former inhabitant, subsequently ordering and leading a cleansing of the world by range troopers armed with mobile bacta units. During the purge, Velgor spotted a remaining speck of Ghymnon and destroyed it with his flux blade.

#### The ultimate mercy

> " The only peace in this galaxy is death itself, Doctor. Wherever there is life, there is suffering—and the umbra side shall always endure. " ―Xarn Velgor

Doctor Sera Lemare , a former employee of the Targ Corporation and an admirer of Gelvarian science, stole a cache of genetic specimens and developed bioweapons research in secret, supposedly creating the perfect killing machine. Bringing a Vectis-class shuttle aboard the Phial -class heavy transport Endless Mercy , Velgor led a squad of shock troopers in pursuit, ordering his men to stay vigilant and secure the area. Velgor's team was immediately ambushed by the Z'Rakkon , creatures born of Lemare's research. Outnumbered and overwhelmed, the Umbra Lord struck at the abominations with his flux blade but lost his helmet in the process, swearing to take down his attackers down with him.

Dragged off by the creatures, Velgor awoke to find himself and his sergeant confined in the Z'Rakkons' lair. The sergeant, who believed he and Velgor were to be eaten, expressed discontent with such a manner of death, causing Velgor to summon his flux blade and promise vengeance. Velgor freed himself and killed several of the beasts, lecturing them on their mistakes before proceeding to eliminate their queen.

The queen Z'Rakkon—in truth, Lemare herself—recounted how she had joined the creatures and become their ruler willingly. Stabbing Velgor through the chest, Lemare then revealed that she had integrated the Umbra Lord into the Z'Rakkon hive mind. However, Velgor escaped Lemare's snare and, despite her pleas, annihilated her. Lemare explained that she had only tried to bring Velgor peace, a favor Velgor returned by killing her and guiding the Endless Mercy to crash down on to the surface of an astronomical object below. Departing the decimated starship, Velgor remarked that the only peace was death and that the umbra side would always endure.

#### Relics of the Replica Wars

Around 17 BVE during the early Imperial era, around two years after the skirmish aboard the Tribunal during Directive 66, Velgor traveled to the snowy moon that was home to the crash site of the Vorash -class Iron Cruiser. Investigating the wreckage with a contingent of snowtroopers , Velgor discovered a memorial to the replica company that perished in the crash, as well as a flux blade, partly frozen but still functional, that once belonged to his former apprentice, Sela Marr.

Igniting the flux blades' blue blade, Velgor heard a squawking sound and turned his head to the sky, where he saw a strange bird soaring through the air. Deep down in his heart, he realized his former Adept was alive, but he lacked any concrete proof and did not sense her presence. As such, he assumed to himself that she may have died, thereby closing the painful memories she represented, despite deep down realizing she had to be alive. After gazing at the bird for a moment, Velgor deactivated the flux blade and turned away from the crash site with the blade in hand, his image reflected in an abandoned replica trooper helmet.

#### Battle against the Benathy

> " I remember when another like you came here with demands. He was taller. Far more impressive. He killed our last king. But even he could not stand against our god. " ―King Kristoff, to Zarrin Karrick on Velgor's attempt to subdue the Benathy

Around 15 BVE , Velgor and his men fought against the Benathy in Wild Space . The imperial army consisted of at least several thousand shock troopers, Ironclad Walker walkers, AT-STs, SKREE fighters, and SKREE bombers. While thousands died on both sides, Velgor managed to kill the king of the Benathy. Velgor ordered his troops to attack and despite the high amount of casualties, he did not retreat. The attacking synth flux was only stopped by a Zyll Beast the Benathy saw as their god. However, Velgor managed to subdue the Benathy anyways.

#### Insurgency on Selvara

Preparing for the operation

> " Are you testing me, Master? " " Testing you? Is that how you perceive things? " " Am I wrong? " " We are, all of us, always being tested, my friend. Tests make us stronger, and strength is power, and power is the point . We must pass all the tests we face. Or die in the effort. " ―Xarn Velgor and Vael Mal

Five years into the Age of the Imperium , Velgor, while meditating aboard the Perilous , was informed by Captain Luitt of an attack on the Yaga Minor shipyards . The attack had been staged by several dozen members of the Free Selvara Movement , a primarily Velari insurgency intent on liberating their homeworld from the rule of the Imperium. The insurgents hijacked an Imperial weapons transport, and Velgor led a squadron of Vireo starfighters in pursuit. After a long chase, during which the transport fled through hyperspace to several different systems, its engines overloaded when it attempted to make yet another jump and Velgor's squadron was finally able to engage it. The squadron brought down the transport's shields with minimal casualties while Velgor crippled the engines.

Though the squadron commander suggested allowing the Perilous to tractor the transport into one of its hangar bays when it arrived, Velgor assumed (correctly) that the insurgents planned to destroy the transport by detonating the heavy ordnance present in the ship's cargo bays. In order to prevent this eventuality, Velgor crashed his black Eta-2 Actis -class light interceptor into one of its gun bubbles and ejected just before the collision. He then entered the ship through the damaged gun emplacement and proceeded to kill all of the insurgent Velaris on the ship. After eliminating the insurgent leader Pok , Velgor noticed an active comlink on the bridge. Assuming that another insurgent ship was nearby, Velgor had his Vireos fan out in a search pattern, but the insurgent ship escaped detection by shutting down nearly all of its systems and hiding in the rings of a nearby planet. Finding nothing, Velgor had the Perilous collect the transport, and returned to Vantor.

In the Imperial Palace, Velgor reunited with his master. There, he and Mal met with Selvara's Senator Orn Free Taa involving the inconvenience that was the Free Selvara Movement. The movement, though relatively minor, had been conducting raids on Imperial shipping facilities and transports and had seriously impacted the flow of Spice from the planet. As such, Mal believed that the situation warranted his personal attention, and decided that he, Taa, and Velgor would travel to Selvara for a state visit . After informing the Senator of the planned journey and dismissing him, Mal informed his apprentice that Taa almost certainly had a traitor in the midst of his support staff who was feeding the movement classified information on Imperial doings.

Though Velgor understood his master's purpose in acting as bait alongside himself and Taa, he saw no reason why the Emperor should potentially risk his own safety over such a minor issue. Rather, Velgor advocated for simply killing Taa and his entire staff, thereby guaranteeing the elimination of the traitor. However, Mal wished to eliminate the roots of the treachery, and not simply the lone traitor in Taa's staff. To that end, Mal gave the order for Moff Delian Mors to be informed that Taa would be arriving for a state visit, but stipulated that she should not be told that either he or Velgor would be accompanying the Senator. In this way, Mal hoped to provide further encouragement for the movement to attack, as he assumed that they would discover his and Velgor's plans via their spies. Ten days later Velgor, the Emperor, Taa, and a contingent of Royal Guards journeyed to the Selvara system aboard the Perilous .

Assassination attempt on the Defiance

> " This guy again. I don't care if you kill me. " ―Commandant Pell Baylo, to Xarn Velgor

While traveling from Vantor to Selvara, the Emperor ordered the Perilous to stop in the Denon system to consult with several navy chiefs on how to better integrate disparate naval academies into one Imperial Naval Academy . The Emperor ordered Velgor to oversee a training session conducted by Commandant Pell Baylo on the Defiance while the Emperor met with his naval chiefs in Baylo's office on the training ship. It was during this training session that Velgor began to believe that Mal was treating him much the same way the Aetheri Council had previously: acting as he knew better, keeping information from Velgor, giving him "busywork." Baylo ordered the Defiance to enter hyperspace on a course for Krestophis. Cadet Raena Vexana entered the coordinates, only to have her hyperspace path overridden by Baylo, who said she was going to send them too close to a singularity.

Vexana studied the path, suspecting that Baylo was incorrect in his adjustment. Velgor and Vexana discovered that the changed hyperspace route would crash the Defiance into Krestophis' sun , killing all aboard. Velgor ordered Vexana to correct the problem, and without revealing that he knew of Baylo's treachery, Velgor and Baylo went to see the Emperor. The Emperor ordered Baylo to turn over command of the Defiance and take a position at the training center on Korrath . Baylo, however, was loyal to the Republic and considered the Imperium a hostile power. Instead of turning over his command, Baylo offered his resignation from the navy. Velgor then revealed he knew of Baylo's plan to crash the Defiance , and the Emperor congratulated Velgor for handling the "petty problem," annoying him.

As Baylo railed against the Emperor, Velgor Synth Flux-choked him to death, drawing ire from the Emperor, who had wished to see Baylo suffer and synth flux him to watch his beloved Navy be turned into something he hated and have the Defiance be melted down into cafeteria trays. Above all, he wanted to use Baylo for his own ambitions since the old man was willing to kill his own students for a cause. Velgor tried to explain that his way was more efficient, but the Emperor wanted to hear nothing of it as he did not order him to kill Baylo. Later, he ordered Vexana to have the Defiance renamed the Obedience as a subtle final jab at Baylo and a not-so-subtle reminder toward Velgor to learn his place.

Crash landing on Selvara

Shortly thereafter, Velgor and the Emperor reboarded the Perilous and resumed their course to the Selvara system. However, upon their exit from hyperspace, the ship immediately began slamming into spacial mines set up by Chavar Kryll 's insurgents. Hidden amongst these mines were specialized devices designed to drain the ship's shields, which began to buckle under the continual assault. As the shields neared failure, a swarm of several hundred reprogrammed Vulture Droids set upon the Perilous from the system's asteroid belt. Velgor boarded his personal interceptor and took several squadrons of Imperial pilots to destroy the droid fighters and mines, but discovered a short time into the battle that Vulture Droids were each carrying a payload of explosive buzz droids .

Realizing that the Vultures themselves were simply delivery systems meant to unload the buzz droids near or inside the Iron Cruiser, Velgor ordered his squadron to concentrate fire on them, as they presented a far greater threat than the mines. But between the mines and the Vultures, there were simply too many targets for Velgor and his squadrons to handle, and the Perilous was severely damaged. The second wave of Vultures was detected immediately after the first had been destroyed, further compounding the already bleak situation. A message came to Velgor from Captain Lewitt of the Perilous that the ship could sustain virtually no more damage without being destroyed.

Velgor moved to intercept the second wave alone, ordering his squadrons to remain near the Perilous to destroy any droids that got past him. Though incredulous, the squadron commander obeyed, and Velgor used Synth Flux to begin tearing apart the compartments on the Vulture droids that housed the explosive buzz droids. He then used Synth Flux to begin flinging the buzz droids at other incoming fighters and repeated this process until the vast majority of the Vulture Droids had been destroyed. The remaining fighters were annihilated by Velgor's squadrons, and he himself returned to the Perilous . Velgor marched to the Iron Cruiser's bridge and Synth Flux choked Taa for a short time, informing him of the traitor in his staff that had been responsible for the assault on the Perilous . Utterly terrified, the Senator vowed to find the traitor, but the Emperor had Taa and his staff confined to their quarters. An order was then broadcast from the Imperial headquarters on Selvara for any and all repair ships to aid the Perilous as it sped, badly damaged, towards the planet.

Unbeknownst to Velgor, some of the Velari repair teams in fact comprised members of the Free Selvara movement, one of which was a group of saboteurs with orders to plant timed explosives on the hyperrelay core of the Perilous . Shortly after the teams landed, one of the Free Selvara groups engaged in a firefight with Imperial shock troopers. When Velgor learned of this, he ordered all of the Velari repair teams, numbering over 100, to be killed regardless of whether they were aiding the assault and proceeded to Deck 17, where the firefight had been reported. On the way, he killed a group of apparently uninvolved Velaris before proceeding to the hyperrelay chamber. The saboteurs, who were led by Chavar's second in command, Isval , had managed to seal the hatch to the hyperrelay core before any Imperial troops could interrupt them, and Velgor began to use his flux blade to cut it open.

By the time Velgor finished cutting through the hatch, the saboteurs had already concluded their business and escaped. The main hyperrelay core had been riddled with timed charges, set to detonate if anyone attempted to disarm or remove them. Velgor concluded that the ship was lost, and ordered the captain to evacuate. Velgor contacted Mal, who instructed his apprentice to meet him aboard his shuttle. Before departing, however, Velgor made one last attempt to kill the saboteurs, but they narrowly evaded him and escaped in an Imperial escort boat . Subsequently, Velgor reunited with the Emperor and left on the shuttle, mere minutes before the Iron Cruiser exploded. Shortly thereafter, the Imperial traitor Belka Drayn provided Chavar with the transponder identity of the Emperor's shuttle, and he directed Isval and her group aboard the escort boat to attack it.

Velgor's exceptional piloting skills allowed him to evade the boat and then fly upside down over its cockpit within direct visual range of its pilots. Once there, he used Synth Flux to choke both Isval and her co-pilot, but before losing consciousness, Isval rammed the shuttle with her escort boat. The crash disabled the shuttle's main power, and Velgor had to use a nearly depleted backup battery to attempt a crash landing on Selvara. As he guided the ship through the atmosphere, he had flashbacks of events and people from his past. Though distracted, Velgor managed to land the ship in Selvara's equatorial forest region, but his distraction did not go unnoticed by the Emperor, who noted that the landing had been poor compared to what he knew Velgor to be capable of.

Not too long afterward, the Velari insurgents continued their assault on freighters, prompting Velgor and the Emperor to take action and destroy them, before moving away from their fallen shuttle. At night, they stopped to rest, when the Emperor questioned his regret and loyalty, but Velgor showed himself devoted still.
