They were soon attacked by a lylek horde, forcing them to retreat into a tunnel, unaware that they had entered their nest. They stood their ground against hundreds of lyleks and their significantly threatening queen, and killed them all before exiting the tunnel and finding a young Velari, Drua . When the Emperor tried to kill her, Velgor stopped him, intending to have the girl guide them to her village , where they obtained a communicator to speak with Moff Mors.

After giving them their coordinates, they were finally surrounded by Kryll's forces, but so did Mors's, aiding them in quelling the insurgency. As the shock troopers killed the Velaris, Velgor noticed a trio of Velaris: Kryll, Isval, and Goll watching from afar and went on to kill them, but Goll and Kryll escaped, leaving Velgor to capture Isval and bring her before the Emperor. He mocked her, telling her that her people had accomplished nothing, and Velgor subsequently executed her. On the Emperor's orders, Velgor then killed the villagers to leave no witnesses, concluding their mission.

#### Teller's campaign

> " We haven't stood on Vantor in some time, Governor. " " The needs of the Imperium keep us elsewhere occupied, Lord Velgor. " " Just so. " ―Xarn Velgor and Wilhard Varrix

When Moff Varrix's Sentinel Base was attacked , Velgor was stationed in the Imperial Palace on Vantor, where he held a court with the Deputy Director of the IVB, Harus Ison , berating Vantori criminals as well as Prefect Phoca Soot of Level 1331 , for ignoring the Emperor's decrees. Telling them to move their operations out of Vantor, Velgor then made an example of Soot for his disrespect and crushed his heart with Synth Flux, and adjourned the court to meet with Varrix and Grand Vizier Mazan Veldon. The latter politely asked Velgor to refrain from killing all those who displeased him, to which the Umbra replied that he would give thought to the matter, before greeting Varrix and sharing the latest developments of the Void Core's construction.

The pair separated when Velgor left Varrix, who met with the Emperor alone. Afterward, Velgor (like Varrix and Veldon) partook in a meeting between the Imperial Ruling Council, and the leaders of Imperium Vigil Bureau and Naval Intelligence Agency in the palace's audience chamber , overseen by the Emperor. He then assigned Varrix and Velgor to a mission on Murkhana to investigate recently discovered Shadowfeed technology and its relationship to the attack on Sentinel Base. The pair traveled to the planet on the Carrion Spike , which Velgor made to carry his meditation chamber .

On Murkhana, however, the Spike was stolen by unknown dissidents , prompting Velgor and Varrix to kill the Sulie crime lord Faazah , and take his Parsec Predator to pursue the stolen ship, hidden by its cloaking systems. Making use of his connection to his meditation chamber, Velgor pinpointed the insurgents' location to the Fial system and later, the Galidraan system , where they engaged the Spike before they jettisoned his meditation chamber and escaped to Lucazec . With the Predator disabled, Velgor and Varrix boarded the Liberator , from which Velgor issued his black Eta-2 interceptor to be transported on the Goliath from Vantor.

On the Goliath , Varrix told Velgor that the insurgency was surely being supported by someone high up in the Imperial Military , and predicted that the insurgents would go to Phindar to refuel the Spike . Subsequently, Velgor led a squadron to attack the stolen corvette in the Phindar system . Though they greatly damaged the ship, it escaped, prompting Varrix and Velgor to calculate its trajectory to Obroa-skai system . Teller transmitted false intelligence that the insurgents were heading to the Obroa-skai system. Hoping to capture the Carrion Spike , Varrix and Velgor deployed substantial Imperial resources including three Interdictor vessels. The prototype Immobilizer 418 cruiser malfunctioned, yanking several ships out of hyperspace and causing a massive accident .

Despite failing to catch the Spike , the Imperials managed to capture the YT-1000 light freighter Reticent , which Teller had sent as a decoy to throw the Imperials off-scent. While Velgor interrogated its Koorivar captain on the Executrix , Varrix accessed Imperial databases and discovered the identities of the insurgents, including its leader Berch Teller . Velgor also interrogated the Reticent ' s Syra Myrthian navigator, who suffered a heart attack. However, Velgor learned from one of his contacts in the Crymorah Syndicate that a lieutenant had negotiated a deal with the Sulie crime lord Faazah for a supply of custom fuel cells . These fuel cells had reached Murkhana shortly before Varrix and Velgor's arrival, thus confirming that Faazah had been colluding with the insurgents.

After a private holo transmission with the Emperor, Velgor told Varrix that Vice Admiral Dodd Rancit was sure the insurgents would attack the Imperial academy at Carida next. Then, Velgor parted ways with Varrix to meet with Rancit, but not before asking for the meaning behind the Carrion Spike ' s name and Varrix told him about his test in Eriadu 's Carrion Plateau . On the Carida system , Velgor boarded the Secutor -class Iron Cruiser Conquest and met Rancit just as the Spike reverted to realspace , astrogating on autopilot. Aware of Rancit's treason, as a co-conspirator of Teller, Velgor had Lieutenant Crest place Rancit aboard an escape pod and had Rancit give himself the command to issue the fire order that destroyed it. Elsewhere, Varrix dealt with the insurgents, ending the insurgency .

Following the defeat of Teller's insurgents, Velgor spent the next three weeks helping Varrix to interrogate the captured insurgents with the exception of Teller, who managed to escape. While none of the insurgents had died under questioning, the Emperor had the insurgents executed privately. Velgor also led a crackdown on the warehouse workers and salvages who supplied Teller's dissidents along with several scientists at Desolation Station who had provided Teller with information. Velgor also took action against the Tenloss Syndicate and lower-level members of the Crymorah Syndicate who had assembled Berch Teller's warship , a modified Providence -class Dreadnought.

#### Hunt on Chandar's Folly

> " Kill me, if you can. " ―Xarn Velgor, to Varrix

Varrix still owed Velgor a favor for capturing Liarch on Mon Calvarra. Sometime after the Teller campaign, Velgor found himself eager to face some kind of test that would actually challenge his skills, something made more complicated by the greatly reduced number of Aetheri active in the galaxy thanks to his and the Wardens' efforts. As such, Velgor came to Varrix with his request: as he had learned of Varrix's hunting, Velgor asked Varrix to hunt him down. Velgor told him to keep him on his feet and capture or kill him if he could. Varrix was shocked at this and began considering why Velgor would ask him to hunt him. Varrix looked through the reports from the Warden Order and saw that Velgor's hunt for the Aetheri had left him with little to no challenge. Varrix deduced that Velgor wanted to be challenged again and was flattered that he had chosen him.

The hunt took place on the Outer Reach planet Chandar's Folly , and Varrix immediately began trying to take on the Dark Lord of the Umbra. Knowing of Velgor's brutal abilities, Varrix outfitted the hunters with slugthrowers and flamethrowers to avoid the possibility of Velgor deflecting their shots. On the second day, they cornered Velgor at a copse of Thurian trees , and four hunters engulfed him with their flamethrowers. As Varrix watched Velgor get covered by the flames, he thought he saw fear in his body language. Velgor's possible fear was brief, however, as he targeted the hunters' flamethrowers, incinerating the four hunters. Through the following days, Varrix saw that Velgor was not hard to track, leaving clear signs wherever he went. They learned why as on multiple occasions Velgor ambushed them. On one occasion, Varrix and his hunters were tracking Velgor near a cliff when he revealed himself and killed one of the hunters with his. Two others fired on him but failed to kill him.

Varrix realized that as long as Velgor had his flux blade, he could not be beaten. So over the course of the next four days, he attempted to steal Velgor's weapon. After six men perished, one of Varrix's hunters managed to snatch it out of Velgor's grip while he was fighting. After this, Varrix saw that not only was Velgor turning to new tactics, he was angrier. Varrix knew that enraged prey was usually easier to take out, but he admitted that this rule did not apply to Velgor. Soon after, Velgor fought five of Varrix's hunters as they fired on him, hitting him many times and scarring his armor. Varrix watched from a hilltop as Velgor came close to the hunters and used Synth Flux to snap one's neck. The other hunters retreated, and Velgor Synth Flux-choked another to death. Once understanding how capable Velgor was with Synth Flux, Varrix assumed that the hunt would soon end. However, Varrix soon realized that Velgor was so keen on getting back his flux blade, that so long as Varrix's team hunted him, he hunted them.

On the ninth day of the hunt, Varrix was down to his last seven hunters. Velgor managed to kill a Valath , the apex predator on the planet, and take its hide, which could help him camouflage better. One of Varrix's Chadra-Fan hunters, Sissian , spotted Velgor northwest of Varrix's camp, and the hunter Yerga notified Varrix of this. Varrix got his binoculars and saw Velgor on top of a peak wearing the Valath hide. Varrix had his hunters form a group around him, and they walked in Velgor's direction. Velgor lured them into a ravine of blue crystals, where his breathing could echo and be untraceable by Sissian and his grandfather, Hardhear . Hardhear heard Velgor's breathing, but although he could say Velgor was near, he could not say which direction. Velgor loomed over them at the top of the ravine and watched as Varrix ordered his team to run. The group ran down the ravine, and Velgor made his move, Synth Flux-pushing one of the hunters into the ravine wall. Velgor then Synth Flux-choked Hardhear before leaping down, under the cover of his Valath hide, and attacking the droid hunter.

Varrix had the rest of his team run into the Stormlands , where it was open ground. They set up camp, and Sissian listened for Velgor's breathing. However, Velgor had deactivated his suit, which stopped his breathing apparatus from giving him away, and he slowly crept up to the camp, before Synth Flux choking Sissian. Yerga attempted to attack Velgor, but he took her by the neck and killed her. The last two hunters opened fire, and Velgor used Synth Flux to retake his flux blade. Varrix then jumped at the two hunters and slaughtered them. Varrix ran away from Velgor, but after realizing he would catch up with him eventually, he stopped and fell to his knees. Velgor loomed over him, not realizing that as he was standing higher than Varrix, he was more susceptible to the lightning in the Stormlands.

Without warning, Velgor was struck by lightning and he fell to the ground. Varrix got up and contacted his flagship, the Carrion Spike , to pick them up. Varrix sat by Velgor and waited for the Carrion Spike to arrive. The corvette soon approached them, being frequently struck by lightning due to its height in the Stormlands. Velgor remained immobile and Varrix told him that whatever he had hoped to learn from this madness, he hoped he had learned it. Varrix then told Velgor that he would get him to a repair bay soon enough. However, Velgor was not finished yet, and he Synth Flux-choked Varrix, showing him that he had not lost just yet. Varrix soon crawled from the range of Velgor's Synth Flux abilities, and Velgor continued lying there.

#### Hunt for Etha Korrin

> " Fear. Anger. Resentment. I sense them in you. You have let yourself feel , Etha Korrin. It gives you power . More than the Aetheri would ever have allowed you. " " Perhaps it's the Zyrrak in me. Or perhaps…now I have something to lose. " ―Xarn Velgor duels Etha Korrin

Continuing his hunt for surviving Aetheri even as more and more of the Order was destroyed, wiping out many of the individuals whom Velgor could actually find a challenge against, Velgor gathered the Fifth Warden, Tualon Veyra, and the Thirteenth Warden for the latest of his personal missions : Velgor and the Warden Order had successfully tracked down the former Aetheri Master Etha Korrin, whose time on the Aetheri High Council had ended with his removal from the body. After that, Korrin had elected to leave the Aetheri altogether and, as a consequence, escaped the execution of Directive 66. The Imperium had finally tracked Korrin to a largely unknown desert planet , where he had become a priest in the Church of the Ganthic Enlightenment and taken a wife, a fellow Zyrrak named Mira . Ahead of the mission, the Thirteenth Warden felt pleased to be welcomed onto one of Velgor's personal missions and was given a select few details about the mission, such as Velgor's urge to finish what Directive 66 had failed to do by killing Korrin.

Velgor and his Wardens arrived on the planet in a Zeta -class shuttle and entered a city, into which Velgor led the Wardens until Velgor located Korrin in a nearby building just as Mira gave birth to their daughter . Ordering the Wardens to stay back and wait, Velgor entered just as Korrin had the chance to hold his daughter and announced his presence within the doorway, confusing Mira—who had not known her husband's original name nor that he had once been an Aetheri until Velgor's appearance —and horrifying Korrin. The daughter's existence, meanwhile, was felt by the Thirteenth Warden, who informed her comrades of the infant's presence. While Veyra was quick to wonder if Velgor would kill the child, the Dark Lord decided the child, who was Synth Flux-sensitive and filled with potential, should be taken and transformed into subject for Project Harvester . As Velgor entered, Korrin attempted to plead with him by reminding Velgor that he was no longer an Aetheri and offered Velgor codes, secret contact frequencies, and leads on other Aetheri survivors so long as Velgor left him and his family alone, all while covertly urging Mira to leave with their child.

Velgor, however, remained firm in his desire to see Korrin killed. In response, Korrin threw their nurse droid into Velgor and used Synth Flux to break open a hole in the building that his family could use to escape, creating a boom that the waiting Wardens overheard. Rather than incur Velgor's wrath, however, they decided it was best to hold back as he had ordered. As Mira and the child made their escape and Korrin claimed his hidden flux blade, Velgor, recovered from the nurse droid attack, ignited his own flux blade and engaged Korrin in a flux blade duel, the sounds of which were soon overheard by the Wardens. As the two dueled and the duel moved outside, Velgor observed that Korrin had embraced their emotions since his days as an Aetheri, specifically noticing signs of fear, anger, and resentment in the former Aetheri Master, who was seemingly holding his own against Velgor. Advancing on Korrin, Velgor voiced what he felt in Korrin and proclaimed that his emotions gave him a power denied to him by the Aetheri, words which the Thirteenth Warden agreed with but made her wonder if Velgor was inviting Korrin into the Warden Order, a prospect which did not make sense to her since there were so few Aetheri left in the galaxy.

Instead of responding to the potential offer, Korrin, calling upon his attachment to his family, continued to clash against Velgor and actually put up a fight against him. In the Thirteenth Warden's experience, Velgor, during a normal hunt, would quickly kill his target without much effort. However, at least as the Thirteenth Warden saw things, the former Aetheri Master fought with enough skill and valor to actually challenge Velgor, an increasingly rare gift for the Dark Lord of the Umbra. Unconcerned with the source of Korrin's emotions, Velgor declared it was too late for him to change and threw him back with Synth Flux, knocking him into the ground before turning his attention to the still waiting Wardens, informing them that Korrin's wife and daughter had escaped before ordering them to bring him the child. When asked about Mira by the Fifth Warden, Velgor simply responded that her life did not matter before he returned to his duel against the angered Korrin, who affirmed that he was no longer an Aetheri and instead a family man who would use his attachments to fuel him.

Entrusting the Wardens to complete their mission, Velgor focused on Korrin and struck against him, a strike that Korrin evaded with a summersault before he struck Velgor in his helmet. As the clash continued, Velgor's cape and waist cape were ripped, but Velgor managed to catch Korrin with a Synth Flux choke, with his continued attacks also managing to rip Korrin's clothing and injure his face. As Korrin held his ground and stood against the Dark Lord in the name of his family, however, Velgor noticed the return of his Wardens with his child, which he quickly pointed out to Korrin. Despite the minor damage to his capes, Velgor appeared pristine and powerful to the Thirteenth Warden, whereas Korrin looked far worse for wear, all leading the Thirteenth Warden to wonder if Velgor had been toying with the former Aetheri Master and dragging the duel out for his own enjoyment. Seeing they had captured his daughter, Korrin lost all his focus on Velgor, who stabbed the former Aetheri Master through the chest and allowed his body to crumble to the ground, dead almost instantly from the flux blade wound. Immediately, Velgor then demanded the Wardens give him the screaming infant, whom he used Synth Flux upon to demand she quiet herself. Immediately, the infant stopped crying and did not wail again. With that, Velgor and his Wardens returned to their Zeta -class shuttle with the child, ready to make their way back to Vantor.

#### Aftermath of the Korrin hunt

> " This is a list of the surviving Aetheri. Others may remain, but of these we are certain. Unfortunately… they have hidden themselves extremely well . We know they're out there, but there have been no signs, no signals. For all intents and purposes, they have vanished. Aetheri remain, but as of now… we have no way to find them. " " What do you purpose, Grand Warden? " " We'll continue with Project Harvester, of course. Beyond that, well, one of them is bound to slip up eventually. We just have to wait " ―The Grand Warden and Xarn Velgor

At some stage, Velgor was informed by the Fifth Warden that the Thirteenth Warden had nearly allowed Mira and the child to escape her grasp, leaving Velgor to believe that she had only captured the infant because that apparent moment of weakness had been discovered. For her part, the Thirteenth Warden had, indeed, been overcome by the memory of her own mother, Feyra Vekkis , giving her up to the Aetheri as an infant and allowed the mother and daughter to briefly escape in a moment of apparent tiredness, but she then claimed that she never intended for them to actually get away. Instead, she used the moment to claim for herself a bout of cruel satisfaction; only after her fellow Wardens reached her and Veyra asked her what Velgor would do to her for letting them escape did she reach out and claim the child from Mira via Synth Flux, telekinetically pulling the child from Mira so as to destroy her trust in other people. To the Thirteenth Warden in that moment, the child belonged either with Velgor or dead. The Warden was not even entirely sure why Velgor wanted the infant and did not care.

Velgor, for his part, had become suspicious of the Thirteenth Warden's loyalty. She had, indeed, become dissatisfied with her life among the Wardens as she felt trapped, feeling as though she had no freedom and was left to perform work for superiors who took all the credit for themselves. She had forced herself to continue her service lest Velgor discover her doubts and invite her into a maiming-filled sparring match. Still, she found solace in a romantic relationship with Veyra, something Velgor had also sensed. While relationships were not strictly disallowed among the Warden Order, Velgor regarded their connection as a weakness and believed the death of Korrin should serve as a warning to both of them. Later, he would claim that he believed that the Thirteenth Warden was building a coalition to usurp control of the Warden Order from himself and the Emperor, using Veyra as her first follower. In truth, however, she simply wanted to retire to her homeworld of Pkori , potentially with Veyra joining her.

Arriving at the Warden Order Headquarters in the Works, Velgor disembarked from the Zeta -class shuttle while telekinetically holding Korrin's child aloft, the Thirteenth Warden, Fifth Warden, and Veyra behind him. Present for their return was the waiting Grand Warden, who welcomed Velgor's return to Vantor and applauded his efforts, and two nursemaids , nanny droids that served the Warden Order as part of Project Harvester. As he disembarked, Velgor informed the Grand Warden that the mission had been a complete success and that the Wardens—whom he referred to as the Grand Warden's subordinates, not his own—had performed their roles well. The Thirteenth Warden found the praise unusual and welcome, yet also demeaning: as she saw things, she was solely responsible for the capture of the child and alone deserved praise for the mission, something she believed Velgor knew. However, Velgor's brief comment had robbed her of individual credit. Such a feeling had plagued her as an Aetheri and made her further doubt her station as a Warden, with Velgor's words sticking with her as the day continued.

As he handed the child off to the nursemaids, Velgor asked the Grand Warden who their next target was, something which made the Warden pause and lead Velgor away. Within the building, Velgor was led to a holographic list of confirmed Aetheri survivors who had hidden themselves away too well to be actively hunted. As such, the death of Korrin ushered in a new phase of the Aetheri Purge: the Grand Warden informed Velgor that Project Harvester would continue but that there were no active hunts for surviving Aetheri, effectively meaning they would have to wait for an Aetheri to reveal themselves or offer a trail. Suddenly, Velgor was faced with a galaxy where there were so few Aetheri left that he had no active targets against whom he could challenge himself. Rather than respond, Velgor silently exited the room and returned to his personal apartment. As time passed, he bathed in a bacta tank, telekinetically modified his life support equipment, walked through Imperial halls, dueled with a training droid, and meditated, in which state he found himself back over the stormy ocean, albeit with calm waves. However, Velgor felt no calm and clenched his fist in silence.

#### Chasing "rogue" Wardens

After feeling that frustration, Velgor decided he had to look elsewhere for a challenge and turned his attention to the Thirteenth Warden, who Velgor knew he would be able to paint as a traitor because of his suspicious about her loyalty and her decision to briefly allow Mira to escape with Korrin's daughter. Velgor made his way into the Warden Order common room, where all the present Wardens had been gathered by the Grand Warden to spend time together. There, the Thirteenth Warden and Veyra were sharing a bottle of dust juice from Korrin's planet to celebrate his death and discussed what would become of Velgor once they had finished off the last of the Aetheri, sliding her eyes towards Velgor in the process. As Veyra mused that Velgor would keep himself busy and find someone new to kill, the Umbra Lord approached their table, catching the Thirteenth Warden's attention as she felt his cold shadow approach them. Meanwhile, the increasingly inebriated Veyra failed to notice his presence. Once he loomed over them both, Velgor announced his presence by silently igniting his flux blade, finally catching Veyra's attention and shocking both them. Likewise, other present Wardens, including the Grand Warden, were caught off-guard and focused their attention on the suddenly unfolding scene.

After a long, tense moment, Velgor was asked by the cautious Grand Warden if he was beginning a test or more training, which the Thirteenth Warden was initially certain of before starting to doubt the prospect. Veyra, meanwhile, was too inebriated to keep his feelings in check, making his concern for the Thirteenth Warden incredibly clear to Velgor. Finally, Velgor affirmed to all gathered that his actions were not a test and swung his flux blade down to catch the Thirteenth Warden, which Veyra managed to block in time with his own flux blade, much to Velgor's interest and to the Thirteenth Warden's surprise. As the Thirteenth Warden took the opportunity to run at Veyra's urging, Velgor kept Veyra's blade in a lock and revealed he had felt their connection before he Synth Flux threw the Velari Warden into a wall. Telling Veyra not to worry about why he had turned his weapon on the Thirteenth Warden, Velgor urged Veyra to concern himself with something else. In response, Veyra ran from him, which Velgor declared meant that Veyra doing the same as his lover. Preparing to chase both down, Velgor then rejected an offer from the confused Grand Warden for Inquisitorial assistance, declaring he had no need of their help. Meanwhile, the Thirteenth Warden and Veyra escaped from the headquarters, understanding that they needed to kill Velgor if they wanted to escape and be free.

The Thirteenth Warden also assured Veyra that she had no idea why Velgor had attacked her, although she reflected that Velgor must have felt her love for Veyra and anger at her limited freedom. Still, she feigned ignorance to her lover and claimed Velgor must have just wanted to kill someone. Meanwhile, Velgor left the Inquisitorial Headquarters and requisitioned two replica shock troopers and a speeder to hunt down both Wardens, whom he claimed to be surviving Aetheri who would attack in an unorthodox manner. Indeed, just as Velgor said as such, the Thirteenth Warden—having taken control of a speeder with Veyra and noticed Velgor's pursuit through Synth Flux—threw an engine from another passing speeder at Velgor, missing him but crippling his speeder by destroying one of its engines. Noticing the strike in time, Velgor lightly leapt from his speeder before it exploded and landed on another passing speeder, much to the confusion of its driver, before both rogue Wardens threw their flux blades into the craft. Velgor fell from the destroyed craft but effortlessly landed onto yet another speeder, all as his previous ride crashed into a park and killed the picnicking Senator Maklooq .

When the rogue Wardens decided they needed to throw something bigger at Velgor, Velgor became the target of a speeder thrown at him at great velocity by the Thirteenth Warden, but he managed to effortlessly throw it right back at her, horrifying her as she began to realize the true power of Xarn Velgor. The worried Wardens responded by throwing another speeder at Velgor, who effortlessly batted it away by throwing it back to both Wardens, much to the Thirteenth Warden's surprise once again. As he closed the distance between their stolen speeders, yet another speeder was thrown at Velgor, who cut the speeder in two with his flux blade, before he leapt at them both, but the two managed to pull down a statue onto Velgor, hitting him in the back and knocking him onto a nearby deck, where he made a hard landing and was left badly damaged. The two rogue Wardens closed in, with the Thirteenth Warden determined to either escape after killing Velgor or claim superiority over the other Warden Order through the act. She declared that Velgor deserved the death he was about to receive and and prepared to strike down the Dark Lord. Velgor, however, then held both Wardens in Synth Flux Stasis by using his pain to fuel his power. Velgor silently raised both Wardens into the air.

The Thirteenth Warden, for her part, tried to overcome Velgor by calling upon her connection to the umbra side, but she found Velgor's umbra side powers far surpassed her own. Realizing she was defeated, she looked over to Veyra but refused to say what she wanted to in front of Velgor. Quickly moving their bodies together, Velgor forced both Wardens to stab each other through the other's chest with their own flux blades, killing both after the Thirteenth Warden's blade stabbed Veyra through the chest and his blade ran through one of hearts. Unknown to Velgor, however, killing the Thirteenth Warden provided her a sense of freedom she had never found as either Aetheri Iskat Vekkis or the Thirteenth Warden Warden. She also felt there was something right about about her and Veyra killing each other, even if Velgor was the one pulling their strings. Velgor then dropped both of their bodies and stood over both, all as the Vantor cityscape around him smouldered from the damage the chase had caused—beyond the crash in the park and the explosion it had caused, the various thrown or damaged speeders had interrupted the flow of Vantori traffic, fallen from the sky, and even crashed into nearby buildings.

#### Building Fortress Velgor

Choosing his own world

> " I have destroyed the remaining Aetheri for you. I will perform any other tasks you require. But first…I ask that you give me a world. " ―Xarn Velgor, to Vael Mal

Velgor then informed the Emperor of the two Wardens' treachery. Velgor told the Emperor that the female Warden allowed Mira and Korrin's daughter to briefly escape and only captured the child because she was seen. He also believed they were building a coalition against himself and the Emperor. Despite this, the Emperor was displeased with Velgor's pursuit across Vantor that cost the life of a senator. The Emperor decided to move the Wardens to another world. The Emperor asked Velgor how Korrin tried to hide. Velgor said he was posing as a priest, which was the closest thing to an Aetheri. Velgor was given a reward: Lira Ondar's Ondara Royal Starship, which a P-100 pick-up droid reported was in working order inside and only needed around two more days for repairs to the hull to be finished up. Wanting the exterior of the craft to retain a damaged appearance, Velgor was content with the current progress and quickly destroyed that droid.

Velgor was then ordered by the Emperor to go to Cindervale to deal with Senator Bael Solen. However, to the surprise of his master, Velgor refused. Having carried out Mal's will for so long, Velgor was content to continue to do so, but he first he demanded a world of his own. Rejecting Mal's offer to give him Dunemere or Ondara, Velgor asked to be given Emberfall, the world where he had been defeated by Kade. The Emperor agreed to his request and would send Colonel Alva Brenne to build a facility for him there. Before leaving for Emberfall, Velgor was then shown the Mask of Vael Mornick , which Mal gave him to help with construction. When Velgor questioned how his master had learned of an Umbra Lord not mentioned in any Umbra nor Aetheri reliquary he had studied, Mal vaguely answered that the mask itself had told him about Mornick.

Return to Emberfall

> " Perhaps your Lira does await you on Emberfall. Perhaps the umbra side will bring her to you. Go. I think, one way or another, you will learn a great deal. That is good. After all, your power…is my power. " ―Mal to Velgor, before the latter departs for Emberfall

Velgor, along with Colonel Brenne and her aide, Lieutenant Roggo , took Velgor's Ondara starship to Emberfall. During the flight, Velgor saw a vision of his younger self with a face that matched his mask. Upon entering the planet's atmosphere, Velgor deactivated the starship's shields and allowed it to burn as they landed. Once on the planet's surface, Velgor commented that the ship looked better burnt. He then told Brenne that he was there to understand a great mystery. While Brenne and Roggo got to work designing Velgor's castle, Velgor returned to the cave where he bled his kyber crystal. As he reached out to the power within the cave's locus, he was presented with a design for the castle from Brenne, which Velgor rejected. However, shortly after leaving, Velgor heard Brenne scream and returned to the starship to find her dead. He also discovered that Roggo had donned Mornick's mask. Velgor attacked Roggo, but stopped upon seeing that he had created a new design for the castle. Removing the mask from Roggo's corpse, Velgor took the mask into the cave and placed it upon the locus. He then asked the mask who he was, to which he replied "I am Mornick".

Mornick proceeded to show Velgor his entire life, from creating morbid works of art from the deceased to being trained in the ways of the Umbra by Vael Shaa . After killing his master and studying the lore of the umbra side, Mornick constructed a weapon which, along with his use of Synth Flux, would freeze a city's population forever at the moment of their deaths. However, an attack by the Aetheri caused him to lose control, destroying both the city and himself in the process. As a result, Mornick's essence became trapped within his mask, forcing him to take on host after host in order to create his art. Velgor placed the mask upon his head, but quickly cast it aside and then departed with it from the cave. Once outside, he engaged in a fight with two Emberfallians, one of which he killed and the other he placed the mask upon. With his new body, Mornick explained that his design for the castle was actually a key to open a door to the umbra side, which Velgor could use to reunite with Lira Ondar. Velgor threatened Mornick not to offer him things he could not provide and Mornick responded that he only wanted another chance to create. Velgor accepted Mornick's offer, but warned the ancient Umbra not to betray him.

Under Mornick's guidance, Velgor used his Imperial resources to begin construction of his castle. With each new design, Mornick insisted that this would be the one to open the door. Though each attempt to do so would subsequently end in failure. And with each failure, Velgor would cut down Mornick's current body in frustration and place his mask upon a new host. Each new attempt to open the door would also bring ruin to Emberfall itself, prompting the Emberfallians to attack in retaliation. The cycle of creation and destruction carried on for two years. Velgor did not remain on Emberfall for that entire time but always returned to test Mornick's latest design.

Mission to Nur

> " You have failed me, Warden. " ―Xarn Velgor, to the Second Warden

Velgor controlled the entire Emberfall system . Concurrent to the construction of Fortress Velgor, the Wardens, due to Velgor's destruction caused when he chased the rogue Wardens on Vantor, moved to a similarly designed Fortress on the ocean moon of Nur in the Emberfall system. In 14 BVE, Velgor journeyed to the Fortress Warden Order to retrieve a reliquary containing a list of Synth Flux-sensitive children, which the Second Warden had been hunting for . Instead, he found that the Second Warden had failed to stop surviving Aetheri Cal Kessara and Cere Jundar , who was pleading for the Warden to return to the light and apologizing for failing her; during the early Aetheri purge, Jundar, while under torture by Velgor, had revealed the hiding location of Trilla, who became the Second Warden, and a number of younglings under her protection.

As Jundar and Trillia talked, Velgor approached, his breathing alone scaring the Second Warden. Jundar also recognized Velgor on sight, telling the worried Kessara that he was right to be scared. Executing the Second Warden for her failure and before she could return to the Aetheri, Velgor used Synth Flux to throw Jundar into a pit with little effort, then suggesting to Kessara that he surrender. Regardless, Kessara launched multiple futile flux blade attacks against Velgor only for Velgor to effortlessly neutralize all of his assaults and telekinetically choke him. Quickly realizing his disadvantage, Cal fled the encounter, after barely surviving by pulling a piece of machinery at his and Velgor's direction, forcing Velgor to defend himself from that and toss Cal aside, with Velgor in hot pursuit.

Despite Cal managing to escape from Velgor moments before he could enter the elevator, Velgor soon managed to catch up to him just as he was about to escape the fortress and attacked him. As he quickly gained the upper hand on Kessara and began to overpower him in a blade-lock, Cal's droid, BD-1 , attempted to short-circuit Velgor's life-support suit, forcing Velgor to disengage and stop the droid. Before he could crush BD-1, Cal made use of the distraction to impale Velgor in the mid-section, only for Velgor to retaliate by tossing Cal aside once again with Synth Flux and as Cal desperately attempted to fight back and summon his flux blade back, Velgor stopped Cal's flux blade before it could return to his hand and once again offered a chance for Cal to surrender the reliquary and when Cal refused, Velgor simply activated the flux blade and forced it into Cal's mid-section, the same way that Cal attempted to defeat him.

Moments later, Jundar reappeared to aid Kessara, only to be easily defeated after a short clash of blades, being flung aside. Commenting on the strength of Jundar's hatred, Velgor was impressed when Jundar was able to temporarily bring him to his knees with the umbra side of Synth Flux and commented how he could feel it inside of her. Once Jundar resisted his efforts to goad her into the darkness, Velgor attempted to kill both her and Kessara, but Jundar was able to hold it at bay with a synth flux-barrier long enough for Kessara to use Synth Flux to shatter a number of windows surrounding them, causing water to pour into the complex and forcing Velgor to use Synth Flux to hold back the flood, as Kessara grabbed Jundar and escaped through the opening.

Helping fellow troopers

Between 13 BVE and 10 BVE , Velgor participated in a battle in the Mid Reach, where a few shock troopers, including the future bounty hunter Beilert Varrick , were pinned down by enemy forces. Velgor used synth flux to disarm and then kill the enemies with the blades from their own weapons. When the troops looked confused, Velgor emerged from a fire and told them to march onwards.

The final castle

> " Let the past die. Kill it if you have to. " ―A voice during Xarn Velgor's vision

In 12 BVE , Mornick's ninth design for Fortress Velgor was constructed, which Velgor stated would be his final chance. Upon activating this castle, Velgor found that he was finally able to open the door. However, before he could enter, he was informed of another attack by the Emberfallians. And while Velgor dealt with the attackers, Mornick took the opportunity to open the door himself. He then summoned his younger self from the past and placed his mask upon him, thus finally creating his masterpiece; himself.

Leaving the battle, Velgor reentered the castle while the Emberfallians continued their attack. Upon returning to the locus, he used Synth Flux to unleash a devastating attack, wiping out the rest of the Emberfallians. Velgor was then met in combat by Mornick, who mocked Velgor for thinking he could control the umbra side. As he chastised the Dark Lord for his ignorance about Synth Flux, Velgor used Synth Flux to ram Mornick with a rock, crushing his new body and killing him. With Mornick dead, Velgor finally opened the door and stepped inside.

Abandoning his physical body, Velgor entered a realm in which he experienced visions of his past, present, and future. He saw himself as a child haunted by the very form of what he would become. Becoming a child once again, he gradually grew up along with the memories of his upbringing. After experiencing a vision of him dueling his former apprentice Sela Marr, Velgor entered another vision of the Aetheri Sanctum and fought against the specters of various Aetheri. After disposing of them, Velgor journeyed further into the Temple until coming upon the forms of Theron Mal and Orien Kade, who both claimed to be his father. After killing Kade, the vision of Mal motioned at Velgor to stand down, to which Velgor responded with a barrage of Synth Flux lightning that destroyed the specter.

Finally, Velgor found himself on a balcony with a vision of Ondar standing before him. Velgor, now in the form of Kael Drayden, told Ondar to come with him. However, Ondar simply told him that Kael Drayden was dead before leaping from the balcony. Velgor screamed in agony as the specter of Ondar was destroyed by a bolt of lightning. He then saw a beacon of blue light in the distance. Within the beacon was a figure who activated a blue flux blade, pushing Velgor back and returning him to his body. After awakening in the castle and retrieving his flux blade, Velgor destroyed the umbra side locus, sealing off the door, because he was unable to restore his wife.

Sometime later, Velgor contacted his master and informed him that he was still alive. The Emperor asked Velgor if he would return to Vantor, to which Velgor said he soon would. The Emperor then asked Velgor if the truth he learned of on Emberfall was what he needed. Velgor then hung up and, while standing in front of the finally completed Fortress Velgor , answered "Yes". Velgor went on to return Mornick's mask to Mal, who placed it in his personal yacht , the Imperialis , under the watch of two Royal Guards.

### Hunting Kade

#### Duel on Mapuzo

> " What have you become? " " I am what you made me. " ―Orien Kade and Xarn Velgor

In 9 BVE , Velgor at last had a chance for his long-awaited rematch with Kade after a Warden known as the Third Warden—who was obsessed with finding Kade because she was in fact the youngling Revai, who wanted to use Kade as bait to get close to Velgor and enact revenge for his slaughter at the Aetheri Sanctum years before —arranged for the kidnapping of Elira Solen to draw Kade to the planet Daiyu . Although he escaped with Solen, the Third Warden told Kade that Drayden had survived his injuries. As Kade grabbled with the information, Velgor awoke in his bacta tank in Fortress Velgor on Emberfall. Assembling his suit, Velgor moved to his throne room and spoke to the Third Warden via hologram, demanding to know where Kade was. Dismissing the apparent death of the Grand Warden, whom Revai had stabbed in her desire to claim credit for Kade's capture herself, Velgor ordered her to prove herself capable, with the position of Grand Warden as her reward should she succeed. In truth, he knew the Third Warden was the youngling Revai and that she intended to betray him, but Velgor kept her alive so long as she was useful.

Velgor also warned the Third Warden that if she failed, she would not live to regret it. Later, Velgor was informed that Orien was on Mapuzo . Velgor arrived with his three Wardens and a squad of shock troopers and walked through the streets of the settlement, terrorizing and killing random civilians in order to draw Orien out. Velgor pursued the Aetheri Master into the quarry. Horrified at what had become of his former apprentice, Orien tried to flee, but Velgor ambushed him. Velgor taunted Orien with regards to his lost strength, and Orien attempted to flee again. Once again, Velgor caught up to Orien, this time lifting him with Synth Flux, as he ignited flammable rocks with his flux blade. Promising to make Orien suffer, Velgor lowered Orien to the ground and dragged him through the flames, scorching him. Velgor extinguished the flames as a squad of shock troopers arrived. Velgor ordered Orien brought to him, but before the shock troopers could comply, a hidden Tala Durith opened fire, killing several shock troopers and reigniting the flammable rocks. This gave NED-B the opportunity to retrieve Orien and for Tala to escape with the injured Aetheri.

Following the successful rescue of Elira from Fortress Warden Order, Velgor angrily made to kill the Third Warden, lifting her into the air with Synth Flux and choking her while reminding her that she was warned what would happen if she failed. Velgor allowed her to speak, which enabled her to reveal that she had planted a tracker on Elira's droid, Lola , which would reveal the location of the Hidden Path network. Velgor acknowledged that he had underestimated the Third Warden, and spared her, though he warned her that he would not tolerate any further mistakes.

#### Attacking Jabiim

> " He was wise to use you against me. " ―Xarn Velgor to Third Warden

Aboard the Reckoning , Velgor reflected on a long ago training duel against Kade in the Aetheri Sanctum as the Aetheri's Adept before his captain alerted him to the Third Warden's arrival. Ordering the pleasantries dispensed with, Velgor demanded to know Orien's location. The Third Warden reported that he had been tracked to Jabiim . Satisfied, Velgor demanded that the Third Warden kneeled before him, and he promoted her to the rank of Grand Warden. He then ordered the bridge captain to set a course for Jabiim.

En route to Jabiim, Velgor ordered the Path's base locked down. The Third Warden warned Velgor that if they trapped them, their opponents could hold out for several days. Velgor responded that he did not intend to break their enemies. After the Third Warden and her forces secured the Path's hangar, Velgor arrived on the surface. The Third Warden told him that Kade was inside, but Velgor stated that he wished to bring him in himself. Velgor forced his way into the hangar and found a transport attempting to leave. He used Synth Flux to ground it and rip it apart, only to find that it was a decoy and the real transport with the Path—and Kade—aboard took off successfully. The Aetheri Master had remembered the old training duel where his Adept had shown shortsightedness because of his anger, which inspired him to exploit Velgor's ruthlessness and single-minded focus.

The Third Warden attempted to use that moment as a distraction to stab Velgor, but Velgor used Synth Flux to stop her blade, stating that Kade was wise to use her against him. Velgor hurled her aside, but she charged at him again, and he used Synth Flux to deflect her blade and throw her. The Third Warden activated her flux blade's twin blade spinning mode, but Velgor used Synth Flux to stall the blades rotation, and then ripped her blade from her grip. Velgor detached her flux blade into two singular hilts, and tossed one on the ground at the Third Warden's feet, taunting her. She retrieved the weapon and continued to attack Velgor, but the Umbra Lord quickly disarmed her again, before stabbing her in the abdomen. As she collapsed, the Grand Warden arrived, revealing he had survived, and Velgor stated that he had known her intent for some time, but had decided to allow it because it was useful in the hunt for Kade. Velgor declared that the Third Warden was no longer useful, and he and the Grand Warden left her to die.

#### The second rematch

> " Kael… " " Kael is gone. I am what remains. " " I'm sorry. I'm sorry, Kael. For all of it. " " I am not your failure, Orien. You didn't kill Kael Drayden. I did. The same way, I will destroy you. " " Then my friend is truly dead. Goodbye, Vael. " ―Orien Kade says goodbye to Xarn Velgor

Velgor returned to the Reckoning as it pursued the Path's freighter. Kade left the Path's freighter on a drop ship with Lola. Against the advice of the Grand Warden, who stated that he should continue the pursuit of the Path's ship, Velgor ordered the Reckoning after Kade to a rocky moon. Velgor departed the Reckoning alone aboard a shuttle and chased Kade above the moon's moon's dry lands before landing in those dry lands, where he confronted Kade and asked if his former master was there to destroy him. Kade replied that he will do as he must before igniting his flux blade. Velgor vowed that Kade would die and the two engaged in a flux blade duel, with Velgor utilizing a combination of one-handed and two-handed combat, that passed from the dry lands into a labyrinth of rock pinnacles . During the duel, Kade attempted to collapse a pinnacle on Velgor, who stopped it with Synth Flux. Remarking that Kade's strength had returned since their last duel but that his weakness remained, Velgor hurled the pinnacle at Kade before resuming their duel.

Velgor collapsed the ground beneath Kade and hurled rocks to keep him down there. Before leaving, Velgor taunted Kade for thinking that he could win against his former apprentice. However, Kade, drawing on his desire to protect Kael's children, managed to break free and charged at Velgor again. On the offensive, Kade used his superior use of Soresu and the more aggressive Ataru utilized by his late master, Sorin Vael to overwhelm the Umbra Lord before using Synth Flux to hurl Velgor against a rock. With Velgor stunned, Kade pummeled him with a maelstrom of rocks then reengaged the duel, critically damaging Velgor's chest plate with multiple strikes from his flux blade's pommel and slashing him across the back before pushing him back with Synth Flux again. Finally, Kade leapt at Velgor, striking the left side of his helmet with his flux blade and revealing the scarred face of Kael beneath. Kade addressed him as Kael Drayden, prompting Velgor to respond that Kael was gone and that he was what remained.

Kade was visibly distraught and apologized to Kael. Velgor replied that he was not Kade's failure and that Kade did not kill Kael: instead, Velgor replied that he killed Kael and vowed to destroy Kade as well. With that, Kade was left to believe his friend was truly dead before bidding Velgor, whom he simply called "Vael," farewell. As Kade left, Velgor called out to him while trying to rise to his feet, unable to catch Kade before he slipped through his grasp once more. Velgor was left with the belief that he had been but a learner in their latest duel, convincing him to increase his strength to cement his power over Kade for whenever their next encounter came. Velgor's suit was repaired and he returned to Fortress Velgor, where he conferred with the Emperor via hologram, stating that he had dispatched probes along all possible routes and that Kade would not escape him again. The Emperor noticed Velgor's agitation and suggested that Velgor could not move beyond his past. Velgor, seeing the threat hidden in Mal's reply, affirmed that Kade meant nothing to him and that he only served the Emperor.

### Attack on the Archive

> " It’s been a while. I was hoping you drowned on Nur when we blew a hole in your underwater base. " " It is delusional to think your actions have had any consequence. The Fortress stands. The Warden Order continues its work. And I have come for you. " ―Cere Jundar and Xarn Velgor

After Bode Akuna , secretly an Aetheri Purge survivor, tipped off the Imperium about the location of the Archive on the holy moon of Embermoor , Velgor launched an assault , acting long before Akuna's superior, Lank Denvik of the IVB, intended to attack. During the attack, he breached the burning Archive and came face-to-face with Cere Jundar once more, beginning a heated duel between the Umbra Lord and the prepared Aetheri Master, whose skills had grown considerably since their battle on Nur; having let go of her fear, Jundar proved to be a challenge for Velgor over the course of their duel, during which Velgor also used the various Aetheri texts around them as projectiles. As the conflict continued, Velgor managed to back Jundar into a shelf, but she rolled out of his next strike in time to send the entire shelf crumbling down onto him. After a moment, a damaged Velgor rose from the flaming wreckage and re-engaged Jundar. The duel would end after Jundar managed to Synth Flux push Velgor against another shelf and rushed to stab him, but Velgor managed to strike her through the chest, ending the conflict with injuries but leaving Jundar dead. Velgor later traveled to the II-0810 Satellite Station , where he killed Denvik for treason.

### Chase over Ryarten

In 6 BVE , two starships were trying to escape above Ryarten . They had sliced into property of the Imperium, and thus became a target. Velgor pursued them in his SKREE fighter, along with a group of other Skrees. One of the starships intentionally let themselves get blown up from SKREE fire, allowing the other starship to escape. The starship that was blown up was piloted by Rone , the partner of Chio Fain who piloted the other starship. Fain would hold a hatred for the Imperium due to this, and especially against Xarn Velgor himself.

### War with the Insurgency

#### Lorath

> " We will squeeze Lorath until someone reveals the whereabouts of these traitors. " ―Xarn Velgor to Minister Maketh Tua

Velgor continued to look for leads on Kade's location, albeit in a more patient manner that did not jeopardize his service to the Imperium, as per Mal's order. In 5 BVE , the Umbra Lord was approached by Mal, who informed Velgor that he had seen a new threat—the "children of Synth Flux"—arising against them. Velgor then contacted the Grand Warden via hologram to inform him of the Emperor's vision, ordering the Grand Warden to hunt down these children to either press them into Imperial service or be eliminated. The Grand Warden promised Velgor that the mission would be done. In time, the Grand Warden's focus would be centered on the Shades insurgent cell that mainly operated around the world of Lorath , as two Aetheri worked as part of the cell: Directive 66 survivor Brenn Aldric , who had gone by the name Caleb Dume during the Replica Wars, and his Adept Cass Virel . The failure of the Grand Warden and other Imperial leaders to defeat the Shades led to Varrix being deployed to resolve the issue in 4 BVE .

After Imperial forces under Varrix captured Aldric during the Shades' raid against the Lorath Communications Center , he was scheduled to be taken aboard Varrix's Iron Cruiser Sovereign to Emberfall, where he would be taken to Fortress Velgor. By that point, Aldric had indeed heard rumors that Emberfall was where Aetheri went "to die," which he told to his fellow insurgent Lena Kryll . However, over Emberfall, the union of multiple insurgent cells into a growing free coalition was revealed when the Shades were rescued by the Ember Squadron , with the Grand Warden also being killed aboard Varrix's crippled Iron Cruiser. As rumors of what had happened over Emberfall became known, riots and uprisings began to occur on several worlds, including Lorath. The Emperor, concerned at the rise in insurgent activity, sent Velgor to Lorath with Varrix to end the growing insurgency. Velgor also made sure the Grand Warden's service was not yet finished: Velgor prevented the Warden's spirit from passing into the Netherworld of Synth Flux and forced him to stand guard over an Aetheri outpost on Tempes as a trap for any would-be Aetheri searching for artifacts. With that, the Warden's spirit eternally suffered from the flames of the Sovereign yet was not allowed to die.

Arriving on Lorath, Velgor stepped off a Sentinel -class landing craft with Varrix and passed high-ranking IVB Agent Alexsandr Kallux . Shortly thereafter, Kallux was assigned to report directly to the Umbra Lord. In an effort to track down the root of the insurgent problem, Velgor concocted an elaborate trap in which he intended to track the Shades back to their primary base of operations. Sometime into the siege, Velgor met with Kallux and Minister Maketh Tua to discuss his plans with dealing with the Shades. During this meeting, he informed Tua that Varrix intended to hold her accountable for her failure to stop the insurgents. Fearing for her life, Tua contacted the Shades, promising them information in exchange for smuggling her off of Lorath before her meeting with Varrix. The insurgents agreed and returned to Lorath. However, Velgor had intended for Tua to contact the insurgents in order to lure them back to Lorath and saw to it that a bomb was placed on Tua's shuttle. Once the insurgents arrived, they attempted to escape the planet with Tua in her shuttle, only for the bomb to go off as Tua boarded the ship, killing the Minister.

Immediately after the incident, Velgor had it broadcast all over the planet that the insurgents had assassinated Tua in order to discredit them. Velgor then ordered Kallux to lock down the planet's spaceport and publicly announce that any ship attempting to reach orbit would be destroyed. Velgor knew that the lockdown, combined with the populace's mistrust of the insurgents, would essentially guarantee that they would need to steal a ship in order to escape. As such, he provided a tempting target at the local Imperial garrison; a shuttle capable of hyperspace travel. When the insurgents attempted to steal it, Velgor confronted them with a small group of shock troopers. Velgor engaged the insurgents' Aetheri members, Aldric and Virel, while his troops engaged in a small scale firefight with the other insurgents. As his intent was for the insurgents to escape, Velgor lazily toyed with the two Aetheri while their cohorts attempted to launch the shuttle.

The confrontation came to an end when two of the insurgents threw thermal detonators at a number of walkers on the landing pad, damaging their legs and scattering the shock troopers underneath them. When Velgor glanced up at the collapsing walkers, Cass and Brenn combined their power to telekinetically push him into the path of the falling war machines. Believing him to be dead, the two insurgents were instead astounded to see Velgor, completely unharmed, use Synth Flux to lift both of the walkers off of himself and drop them behind him. Recognizing that they were hopelessly outmatched, Brenn ordered Cass to run with him to the commandeered shuttle. Hoping to prevent Velgor from pursuing them, Ryn Wrenna fired a number of volt pistol bolts at Velgor. He reflected two of them back into her chest and helmet but made no attempt to board the shuttle or otherwise stop it. After the shuttle successfully launched, one of the shock troopers who had been present for the confrontation assured Velgor that he would scramble fighters immediately to intercept it.

However, Velgor informed him that doing so would not be necessary, as he believed that the insurgents would not attempt to leave the planet for some time. Velgor later ordered Kallux to destroy Tarkintown in order to draw out the insurgents or, failing that, to demoralize them. Shortly thereafter, the insurgents used the stolen shuttle to escape the planet and rendezvous with the insurgent fleet. However, Velgor had placed a tracking device on board the shuttle, and mere minutes after the insurgents had reached the fleet, Velgor's SKREE Advanced x1 fighter dropped out of hyperspace and attacked Ember Squadron . The insurgent CO deployed the Arrow interceptors of Ember Squadron , but they were unexpectedly decimated by Velgor's lone assault. Weaving through the defensive screen of fighters, Velgor attacked the insurgent command ship , Ember Home , disabling it in short order.

#### The apprentice lives

> " The apprentice lives. " ―Xarn Velgor senses Sela Marr

The Shades quickly boarded their ship, the Wisp , and fought back against the Dark Lord. However, neither the Wisp nor the remainder of Ember Squadron could land so much as a hit on Velgor's craft, and he continued to bombard the now-crippled Ember Home while picking off interfering fighters at his leisure. With its fighter escort all but destroyed and its weapons, shields, and engines disabled, the insurgent command ship appeared to be doomed as Velgor prepared for a final assault. However, on board the Wisp , Sela Marr decided to combine her powers with Brenn's in order to probe Velgor's mind with Synth Flux. The probe enabled Sela to see deeply enough into Velgor's mind for her to recognize him as her former master, and for Velgor to recognize the one probing him as his former apprentice. Sela promptly lost consciousness as a result of the psychic backlash of the probe but Velgor was unaffected.

Upon discovering Sela's presence aboard the insurgent craft, Velgor's priorities immediately changed. In an unexpected turn of good fortune for the insurgents, Velgor broke off what would have been a killing run at Ember Home in favor of engaging the VCX-100 light freighter . As Velgor peppered the Wisp with volt pistol fire, Brenn and Lena Kryll managed to convince Jun Savren to evacuate Ember Home as Admiral Kassius Konstantine entered the system with three Star Destroyers. The Admiral contacted Velgor in order to receive instructions, and Velgor ordered Konstantine to block the Wisp ' s escape, but not to fire at it, as he wanted the insurgents aboard it alive. Meanwhile, the crew of Ember Home abandoned the crippled command ship and fled the system with the tattered remains of the insurgent fleet. As Velgor closed in on the Wisp , it jumped to hyperspace just as the Destroyers activated their tractor beams , catching Velgor instead of the Wisp .

Following his victory, Velgor boarded Konstantine's Destroyer and contacted the Emperor. He informed his master that he had "broken" the insurgents, but the elder Umbra Lord sensed disquiet in his apprentice's emotions. Velgor then revealed to the Emperor that he had discovered that Marr was alive, and told his master that he believed that she was in league with the insurgents. Pleased by this news, the Emperor declared that she could lead the two Umbra Lords to other Aetheri who survived Directive 66. Velgor immediately postulated that Kade might be amongst them, to which the Emperor concurred; if Kade was still alive. The Emperor urged Velgor to be patient, and instructed him to send another Warden to hunt the insurgents down.

As per his master's orders, Velgor assigned the Fifth Warden the task, with the Seventh Warden also joining the hunt on Velgor's order. He made it clear to both they were not to fail. At some point the Shades infiltrated an Iron Cruiser that Velgor was aboard. He stalked the insurgents, but they successfully evaded capture. After several unsuccessful attempts to capture the insurgent Aetheri, the Fifth Warden and Seventh Warden pursued Aldric, Virel, and Marr to a still-standing Aetheri Sanctum on Lorath in 3 BVE . Though the insurgents managed to flee, the Wardens informed Velgor of their discovery. Velgor came to inspect the site, informing them that the Emperor would be most pleased by their discovery. The Wardens warned Velgor that the Aetheri were becoming more powerful, but he retorted it would be their undoing.

#### Duel on Searmark

> " Sela...! Sela... " " Kael... I won't leave you. Not this time. " " ...Then you will die . " ―Xarn Velgor and Sela Marr

Afterwards, Velgor dispatched the Fifth Warden, the Seventh Warden, and the Eighth Warden to hunt down Vreth, who had been stranded on Searmark and had been studying its Umbra temple for years; he himself arrived after Virel had activated the superweapon with the Umbra reliquary . With his Wardens having all been killed by Vreth, Velgor chose to personally retrieve the reliquary and take possession of the superweapon. Perched on the roof of his SKREE Advanced x1, he leaped from the fighter and landed in front of Virel. After a brief verbal exchange, Velgor quickly disarmed the young Aetheri by destroying his flux blade and prepared to execute him, but before he could do so, he was interrupted by Sela Marr.

Velgor offered his former apprentice clemency in exchange for the location of any surviving Aetheri, but Marr claimed that there were no more Aetheri; that Velgor and his Wardens had killed them all. Skeptical of her claim, Velgor obliquely threatened to torture the information out of Virel instead. This prompted Marr to proclaim in disgust that while she had begun to suspect that Velgor and her former master were one and the same, she could no longer believe that due to Velgor's cruelty. Velgor replied that Drayden had been weak and that he himself had destroyed her former master. Marr then swore to avenge Drayden, but Velgor reminded her that revenge was not the Aetheri way. But Marr no longer considered herself to be an Aetheri, and after informing Velgor of this, she attacked him. However, she proved ultimately to be no match for her former master who, after several minutes of intense swordplay, used Synth Flux to blast Marr off a ledge. Believing her to have been neutralized, Velgor went to retrieve the reliquary.

Velgor managed to catch up to Aldric and Virel before they could board their ship, and used Synth Flux to begin pulling both the reliquary and the two Aetheri towards him. But before he could seize it, Marr ambushed Velgor and managed to slice off the upper right portion of his mask with her flux blades . With his mask damaged, Velgor's voice filter began to malfunction, causing him to wheeze, and when he next spoke, calling out to his former apprentice, it was in a distorted mixture of his real voice and the mechanical baritone imposed by the filter. This and his partially exposed face confirmed to Marr that Velgor was indeed her former master. As the Temple locked down, Marr proclaimed she would not abandon him as she had before. Velgor paused at this and stared wordlessly at Marr, seemingly conflicted on how he should respond to his old Adept. However, he quickly regained his composure and snarled that she would die for her choice. The two engaged in combat once more, while Aldric and Virel escaped from the chamber enclosing Velgor and Marr.

After a few moments of battle, locked his blade with Marr's and began to push through her guard. As he prepared to kill her, the energy discharges from the overloading reliquary stand suddenly intensified, and Velgor glanced up. As he did, a massive blast of power struck the ceiling of the chamber, and Marr used the distraction to push Velgor back a step with Synth Flux. She then drove both of her flux blades into the ground at her feet, causing the floor of the chamber to begin crumbling. Velgor quickly recovered from his distraction and aimed a killing stroke at Marr. Unbeknownst to the two combatants, however, two years later, Cass Virel entered the temple on Lorath and found himself in the World Between Worlds realm, where he was guided to a portal, which exited at the Umbra temple in 3 BVE, by the convor Morai, the very same bird Velgor had seen on the snowy moon outside Marr's crashed Vorash. Virel witnessed the conclusion of Velgor's battle with Marr even as his younger self was locked out of the reliquary chamber. As Velgor swung his flux blade at Marr, Virel reached through the portal and yanked her backward into the temple. As a result, Velgor's strike missed and the temple floor crumbled, sending Velgor plummeting into a pool of light.

From Velgor's perspective, a hand had reached from a portal from nowhere to save Marr. Velgor survived the fall and managed to make his way back to the surface, where he was watched by Morai. After Morai guided Marr —who escaped back to her place in time after Mal reached into the World Between Worlds —through the depths of the temple to reach another portal to the World Between Worlds, the convor exited the temple and flew out, with Velgor watching the bird from the surface. Believing Marr dead, Velgor departed Searmark and returned to his duties in the Imperium. Marr's supposed demise marked the end of the Great Aetheri Purge.

#### Continued insurgency

In 2 BVE and shortly after the Imperium's victory over the Batonn sector insurgency , Velgor arrived at the Imperial Palace on Vantor and was introduced to the newly promoted Grand Admiral Vorren, the very same Chyrr officer he had met during the Replica Wars, by the Emperor. When Vorren stated he was pleased to finally meet the Dark Lord, Velgor claimed to feel the same. A short time later in 2 BVE, amid Vorren's ongoing campaign to root out and destroy the growing insurgent threat, the rebellious senator of Serenne , Vela Sess, formally rallied numerous insurgent cells together under a single banner, the Alliance to Restore the Republic . Fulfilling the fears of Imperials like Varrix and Vorren, once disconnected insurgent cells began to act as a unified synth flux, marking an official beginning point to the Galactic Civil War , although true open warfare would not break out for sometime still.

During the Galactic Civil War era, shock trooper lieutenant TK-289 served with Velgor. During or after 2 BVE, at some point after the Alliance was formed, Xarn Velgor was lured to HS-197, an abandoned ore-mining moon in the Horox system, by Cyn, the son of Sig Sendvall, a cyberneticist who had worked on Velgor’s suit. At another point in that time frame, on an unidentified astronomical object, Velgor accompanied shock troopers in their search for an Insurgent fugitive. As the troopers destroyed a village to frighten its inhabitants, the Umbra Lord interrogated Noegaud, a local bully. A youngling named Niko, whom Noegaud often harassed, witnessed this interaction, leading him to conclude that Noegaud was correct in his belief that the only thing that mattered was power.

Velgor once destroyed Blue Squadron, a diaspora of Vectors and Arrows, in his SKREE Advanced x1. The Umbra Lord then landed, engaging DF.9 turret emplacements and Insurgent infantry before bringing down the Vellus , the GR-75 medium transport that the starfighter escort had been protecting. Only one Insurgent was left standing, who Velgor promptly killed.

#### Investigating a disturbance

> " Kael Drayden is dead. " " I know. " ―Xarn Velgor and Grand Admiral Vorren

Velgor and Vorren crossed paths on Vantor once more after Vorren suffered a strategic defeat on Atollon . Although the battle had been a tactical victory, a number of insurgents had escaped the Grand Admiral after the Bendu appeared. As a result of allowing the insurgents to slip through his grip, Velgor now held a disdain for the Grand Admiral. Though during this meeting, the two were ordered by the Emperor to the planet Batuu to locate a disturbance in Synth Flux that the Emperor had sensed. While Vorren brought his own forces on board his Iron Cruiser, the Chimaera , Velgor brought a contingent of his personal shock troopers from the 501st Legion, the 1st Legion with him to assist in tracking down this disturbance at the edge of known space.

During their mission, they combated the Grysk species and learned the disturbance was caused by a group of young Chyrr Synth Flux-sensitives known as, to the surprise of Velgor, the " Sky-walkers ." Afterward, Velgor came to doubt Vorren's loyalty to the Imperium because of the threats growing against the Chyrr in the Unknown Regions. While Vorren managed to deduce that Drayden and Velgor were one in the same, the Grand Admiral came to think of Velgor as being a separate person mentally and that his old ally Drayden was dead. After telling Vorren to ensure he kept his promise to the Emperor by ordering him to stay in Imperial space instead of leaving to save his people, Velgor came to respect Vorren's SKREE/d "Defender" Multi-Role Starfighter project, stating he would speak on its behalf to Mal and suggesting upgrades for the craft. On Batuu, Velgor's presence near Black Spire was spoken of by locals, with rumors of the legendary Imperial enforcer's visit to Batuu being spoken of for years to come.

#### Submission to Ahr

> " You hate it. Every time you kneel . Even before me . Good . But this bureaucrat knows his sector, Lord Velgor. With his knowledge , we will subdue it. So in my service…you will obey the governor's every order …to the letter …until you have learned your lesson . " ―Vael Mal, to Xarn Velgor

During the Imperium's efforts to subdue anti-Imperial forces in a Mid Reach sector under Imperial Governor Ahr , Velgor participated in an attack on the planet Namzor , where he eliminated a group of insurgents . In the process, he destroyed the insurgents' stores of coaxium , violating the command of Ahr, who was charged with securing the hyperfuel. The governor brought his frustrations to the Emperor. Sensing Velgor's anger, the Emperor commanded his apprentice to follow any and all orders from the governor until Velgor had learned his lesson.

Ahr took great pleasure in his newfound power, sending Velgor on increasingly dangerous missions throughout his sector . He sent Velgor to fight alone against the renegade droids of Ahn Krantarium , and later retracted his forces on Phelzepham , leaving Velgor to battle the moon's insurgents by himself. Though he wished to kill the governor, Velgor continued to obey Ahr's commands.

Governor Ahr brought his ship to the Kankalo Belt Containment Zone , an asteroid field in which a great creature resided. He commanded Velgor to eliminate "the greatest threat" he could find. Seeing an opportunity, Velgor confronted Ahr, recognizing him as his true threat. Before Velgor could strike, Ahr ordered the Dark Lord to kneel before him. Velgor obeyed, but the creature attacked the vessel, pulling the governor out into the void of space and freeing Velgor from his submission.

#### Cianap

> " Velgor to command. Minor system malfunction. I can't fly it back through the battle. Landing on the planet for repairs. End this swiftly or suffer the consequences. " ―Xarn Velgor radios in from his SKREE fighter

Velgor led Imperial forces at Cianap in a battle against the insurgents. During the battle, Velgor was targeted by several Vectors but was able to take them out. However, his SKREE was damaged when one purposely crashed into him and was forced to crash land on Cianap. Velgor then exited his SKREE and awoke an Ender from its Slumber . The Ender then tried to kill Velgor; however, he stabbed one of the Ender's eyes out and tried to cut its back open, only for the Ender to grab him. Velgor started to slice the Ender's fingers, but the Ender pushed him into a building and dropped him. The Ender then started to go after a native , but Velgor rode a creature and saved the native from the Ender. Velgor then decapitated the Ender, finally killing it. The native thanked Velgor, who then returned to his SKREE and was taken aboard an Iron Cruiser via grapple beam.

#### An unwelcome romance

> " Xarn Velgor to the bridge. Come get this garbage out of my quarters. " ―Xarn Velgor, upon killing the nurse

At some point, Velgor had arrived at the Void Core, and made his way to his personal medical bay. As he made way, he barged through a medical cart being tended to by a nurse , who was flustered due to her infatuation with Velgor. Velgor underwent treatment from a doctor while in the medical bay, but became angered when the nurse from the prior encounter entered the medical bay as Velgor was putting his helmet back on. In his anger, Velgor synth flux pushed the doctor into the wall, demanding that he control the medical bay or be replaced. The nurse, however, saw Velgor's anger as reciprocation of her feelings for him, misconstruing his actions as protection of her. After his bout of rage, Velgor exited the medical bay, admonishing the doctor as he attempted to excuse why the appointment had taken so long. The nurse, upon being told to clean up the medical bay, instead stole pieces of Velgor's armor as a keepsake, storing it among other discarded pieces of Velgor's armor, as well as a vial of his blood.

A proceeding incident resulted in Velgor crash landing on the Void Core in his SKREE Advanced x1. Velgor made his way to the medical bay once more, demanding the nurse to call in the doctor. The nurse briefly aided Velgor, perceiving the moment as intimate, before the doctor himself returned, ordering the nurse to leave as he tended to Velgor instead. Velgor, impatient, cut his appointment short, leaving to rejoin his prior battle. However, Velgor had left his cape behind, which the nurse proceeded to steal and take to her quarters. After the doctor discovered her, he dumped the bits of Velgor's armor into the trash compactor. Driven mad, the nurse walked into Velgor's quarters, which he had left unlocked out of a belief that no one would enter. Upon seeing the nurse enter his chambers, a helmetless Velgor simply stared at her as she confessed her infatuation with him. Momentarily after, Velgor stabbed the nurse with his flux blade, killing her. Velgor then put his helmet back on and exited his quarters, contacting the bridge and ordering them to remove her corpse.

#### Hunting the Hidden Hand

On the orders of the Emperor, Velgor attacked and killed three lower-level clans working for the Hidden Hand , a crime syndicate known to sell weapons to the Insurgency. He then attacked the Lower Bay Docks on Fikari , killing most there, questioning the Weequay Holaq about the whereabouts of the Hidden Hand. Holaq told Velgor that none of them would know where the Hidden Hand was. Detailing the way they maintained secrecy, he pled with Velgor to believe him. Velgor replied that he did and blew up the docks, killing him. He then reported the failure to Mal over hologram, who reiterated the importance of taking down the Hidden Hand. The Tarsunt Gwi , who worked for the crime syndicate, overheard this conversation, and hired a team to hunt down and kill Xarn Velgor . At some point, Velgor learned of this plot and traveled to the Dark Quarter on Yuw to kill bounty hunters and find answers.

Later, the captain of the bounty hunter team—Beilert Varrick, the same man he had saved during Varrick's days as an Imperial soldier —amplified a signal near Arvina , disguising it as a Hidden Hand signal. Velgor arrived on an Iron Cruiser in response and it fired on the city below. He then got into his SKREE fighter and chased after Varrick's team. Their sniper, the Insurgent agent Gita , currently disguised as the Dunefolk Raider "Urrr'k", managed to shoot down two of Velgor's support Skrees. This gave the team enough time to escape, leaving Velgor to investigate the damage in the city below. Varrick had planned to leave data banks behind that would reveal the Hidden Hand's secret outposts, allowing him to get the jump on Velgor. However, an informant of Velgor's within the crew —the bounty hunter Dengrik —revealed the bounty hunters' next destination.

Velgor followed them to a Hidden Hand outpost on Heva aboard the Formidable . Upon arriving, an Imperial general informed Velgor that Varrick's ship, the Broken Wing , had been located, and that their informant had gone silent. Velgor abruptly told the general to scramble all fighters, much to the general's confusion. Varrick's crew had begun remotely piloting a fleet of bot-drones towards the Formidable . Velgor got into his SKREE fighter and pursued the drones as well as the Broken Wing . He shredded the drone fleet, but the drones started to pull away. Velgor told his pilots to stay on the leader, as he took out its laser cannons. The drones then formed a large cannon that fired, destroying the Formidable . Despite this, Velgor stayed locked on the Broken Wing , taking out its engines and causing it to crash-land. Velgor landed his SKREE fighter and entered the ship, flux blade drawn.
