Upon meeting with his master, Velgor was informed by Mal that the Umbra had an enemy, claiming that the recent attacks were part of a coordinated campaign of diversion. Mal then called in IVB Director Sliro Barsha to report on his current investigation. Barsha reported that his division within the IVB —the undercover criminal syndicate Zerek Besh —had learned of a rumor that the major crime syndicates were vying to obtain the Graks' long-standing arrangement with the Imperium. However, the rumor was coming from a single source: Carmine Dawn. As they walked through the Palace halls, Velgor agreed that Carmine Dawn had orchestrated the Syndicate War but pondered how deep its influence went.

Mal went on to state that they had orchestrated the other attacks as well, saying that Carmine Dawn had targeted the Umbra. He then asked Velgor about the syndicate's leader, Vessa, who Velgor fought and failed to acquire Karrick from. Velgor told Mal that while Vessa had not been a true threat to him, she was trained in Teräs Käsi and possessed a fighting style which reminded Velgor of his master's. From this, Mal and Velgor deduced that, since Vessa wallowed in the criminal underworld, she must have been trained by Vreth. Mal then concluded that Vreth must have trained Vessa to use her as an instrument of revenge and went on to ponder what else he had taught her. With the Umbra aware of Carmine Dawn's role in recent events, an alert was sent out the rest of the Imperial Military to track down the Vermillion , which made Vessa order her assets in the Imperium and other major factions to sow chaos across the galaxy to further distract the Umbra. With the Screaming Key they had stolen from Velgor, the Knights of Karrick, meanwhile, traveled to a location known as the umbra side hellscape , a location unknown to Velgor and Mal, alongside " the Archivist " on orders from Vessa to retrieve an artifact known as the Fermata Vault .

#### Dealing with Zerek Besh

After Director Barsha and Zerek Besh failed to bring in a single member of the Free Coalition, Velgor confronted him on the IVB space station Derosha Six . There Barsha tried to appeal to Velgor by announcing the successes of Zerek Besh, but Velgor countered that, because of the criminal syndicate's inability to bring in any insurgents, Zerek Besh and Barsha by extension were failures. Barsha continued to boast about the size of the network the IVB had created under Zerek Besh, and insulted Velgor about his and the Imperial army's failures on Frostmark. In his anger, Velgor destroyed a glass display behind him using synth flux, and surrounded Barsha in the shards. Forced to ground, Barsha pleaded with Velgor, saying that he was close. Velgor simply replied that he needed to find insurgents then.

Shortly after his departure, thiefs Kay and Riko Vess were able to steal access codes to Director Barsha's palace vault on Canto Bight . From there, Kay Vess' crew working under Barsha's brother Jaylen Vrax were able to steal a secret codex Barsha had that detailed sensitive information about almost all ranking Imperial officers. Vrax contacted Velgor and informed him of the database and his intentions to take control of Zerek Besh. They made a deal, in exchange for the codex and the coordinates to an Insurgent base on Akiva , Vrax would be given Zerek Besh. Later, Velgor ordered Barsha onto the bridge of the Revelator , where he condemned Barsha for letting such critical Imperial intelligence go. Velgor then looked to Vrax and said the Imperium had accepted his terms. He ended the call and gave up Barsha to the mercy of Vrax and his associates.

#### Finding the Fermata Vault

Velgor was amongst Mal's tools in the crusade to root out Dawn agents across the Imperium. During his effort to destroy the Dawn, Velgor bisected a traitorous shock trooper in an industrial environment. At one point, Velgor nevertheless returned to his castle on Emberfall, where he was called by the Eye of Webbish Bog. Upon entering the Eye's cave, the Eye showed Velgor a moment from the Replica Wars, where he, Zho, and their replicas had fought B1 battle droids, and glimpses of the future, including one of him being confronted by Ondar's surviving handmaidens. Velgor then realized that he had been lured there as a test before being attacked by Emberfallians. After killing them, Velgor claimed that the Eye had wasted his time with puzzles. To this, the Eye explained that the visions were warnings for the end of everything. When Velgor asked why he should care, the Eye reminded Velgor that he was a part of everything, but, if he refused to accept this, he could not be helped. Just as the Eye had shown him, Velgor, with his helmet slightly damaged, returned to his fortress and, after sensing a great disturbance in the umbra side, was contacted by Mal to discuss the disturbance.

Informed by Mal that Vessa claimed to possess the Fermata Vault, Velgor was ordered to find the artifact and kill anyone who knew of it. Mal also told Velgor about Vessa's central claim about the artifact: it contained an ancient Umbra Lord who was so powerful they had needed to be locked away. In truth, no such Umbra Lord existed; Vessa simply needed a claim that would draw both Umbra to the Cage, allowing her to lock them both into it and free the galaxy from their rule. Her initial effort to open the cage, however, had resulted in the Knights of Karrick abandoning Carmine Dawn out anger at being hurt in the attempt and out of fear of the ripple it sent out. Velgor made his way to the site of the disturbance, where he only found wreckage from the experiment. Although he was certain it was the origin of the disturbance, he deduced, due to a lack of bodies, that no Umbra Lord had been released. Velgor then returned to the Sovereign and reported his findings to Mal via its holoprojection chamber.

Velgor asked Mal if there truly was an ancient Umbra within the Cage, which Mal explained was possible: built by Vael Mornick himself, the Cage could imprison people and places outside of time and had never been used lightly. Mal further warned Velgor that, because of the urge within every Umbra to dominate others, the mere possibility was the greatest danger to everything they had built together. Amid their conversation, the two suddenly felt the Cage begin to open once more as the Archivist and her assistant, archaeologist Kho Phon Farrus , tried to use Ascendant -designed technology to focus nearby umbra side energy into it. Although Farrus shut off their technology as the Cage began to leach life energy from each of them, the experiment had carried on for long enough that Mal was able to track the disturbance to the planet they were on. With this, Mal sent Velgor to retrieve the Cage and destroy those present.

#### The Orphans

> " [Xarn Velgor] radiates a particular, dark energy. But he is also other, like whatever drives him is completely removed from motivations experienced by other beings. Do you know the feeling you get when you hear a story about an animal eating its own young? That's what being in Xarn Velgor's presence felt like to me. The first time I saw Xarn Velgor was when he came to kill me. He failed. I think that was rare for him. As a general rule, when Xarn Velgor tried to kill something…it died. " ―The Archivist

Once the Sovereign arrived at the planet, Velgor, intending to handle the situation on his own, ordered Vett to prepare his Vectis -class T-4a shuttle and landed at the seemingly-deserted site with two shock troopers. In the vicinity of the artifact, where he could not single out the presence of the supposed ancient Umbra, Velgor sensed a great deal of umbra side power from the Fermata Vault. Velgor discovered the Archivist and Kho Phon Farrus trying to hide beneath the remains of their machine. In Velgor's presence, the Archivist found that the Umbra Lord radiated a dark energy. While Farrus, having been warned of Velgor by their rival Doctor Vexa, quickly surrendered, the Archivist refused, to which Velgor began to choke the agents with Synth Flux. However, he was interrupted by the arrival of Chanath Cha and her Orphans , consisting of Sear , Ladybright , and Imara Vex . When Cha asked Velgor if he remembered her, Velgor simply stated that he would kill her as he bared his flux blade.

Cha went on to introduce herself, explaining that Velgor had slaughtered her family and that she had spent every day since then plotting her revenge. Velgor quickly forgot her name once more before the Orphans attacked. After being electrocuted by Ladybright and burned by one of Cha's acid bombs, Velgor pushed the Orphans back and walked into the ocean to recover. Once he did, he returned to the shore and saw Ladybright, Vex, Pharrus and the Archivist fleeing for the Siroto . When Velgor mentioned Vessa's death to Cha and Sear, he sensed their confusion and lack of fear, indicating that Vessa was still alive. As he fought Cha and Sear, he chastised them for their arrogance and prepared to strike Cha down, only for Vex to snipe off one of his hands, prompting him to tip over the Siroto with Synth Flux and throw Vex into the forest. With Vex severely injured, Ladybright, Pharrus and the Archivist fled in the Siroto before returning to assist Cha and Sear. However, the Siroto then turned back around and left the planet, much to Cha's relief. With Sear and Vex defeated, Velgor twisted Cha's introduction of herself by re-introducing himself and stabbed her through the chest.

With Cha dead, Velgor left the planet to find Vessa and reunited with his master. Both traveled a dark, smoky location, where Mal asked Velgor if he actually felt the presence of an ancient Umbra Lord in the Cage. With Velgor admitting he only felt intense umbra side power from it, he engaged Mal in a sparring duel, the first they had engaged in in some time, at his master's request, allowing Mal to explain how he would enforce the Rule of Two if another Umbra Lord indeed existed. After Mal pushed back Velgor with a final strike and declared the duel over, Velgor made one last rush at his master before stopping when his master repeated himself. Feeling they had no choice but to follow the ripple in Synth Flux caused by the next Cage opening, Mal took the moment to admit Vessa would be missed as he and Velgor looked across the location.

Vessa attempted to sure up her chances by reaching out the Knights of Karrick, reminding them that they had attacked Velgor's fortress and thus would be hunted by the Umbra if they did not help her destroy Velgor and Mal. Around the same time, the Everspark artificial intelligence, created thousands of years prior for use against the Umbra by the Ascendant cult, resolved to hunt down Velgor. The AI had taken over Doctor Vexa's body to carry out its mission, further collecting 0-0-0 and VT-1 to help in its quest. When Vexa used a virus she had installed into the two droids to regain control of her body, the Spark introduced, much to Vexa's horror, their Umbra hunting mission against Velgor. By possessing Vexa, it had access to her memories of Velgor and saw how she considered the Umbra Lord a monster, but the AI believed itself ready to take on the Umbra Lord and his master. Because Vexa had once outmaneuvered Velgor, the overconfident Spark thought itself skilled enough to engage him in one-on-one combat. In fact, Vessa contacted the Spark to request its help in destroying the Umbra.

#### Destroying the Dawn

> " You wish to board the station, Lord Velgor. I sense your desire . To possibly meet an ancient Umbra, one so dangerous they were locked away in such a powerful prison as the Fermata Vault . To feel their power. To test yourself against it. And then… to destroy them. Or even… to be destroyed . I desire it too, Lord Velgor. But it is a foolish desire. And I am not a fool. " ―Vael Mal, to Xarn Velgor

The Archivist moved the Fermata Vault and the equipment needed to finally open it to an ancient Amaxine Space Station in the Outer Reach, where the Cage could feed off the plant life and umbra side energy left behind by the ancient Drengir beings. Sensing the disturbance in Synth Flux caused by the opening of the Cage, Mal and Velgor, just as Mal said they would need to in the name of enforcing the Rule of Two, followed the disturbance aboard the Sovereign with a fleet of Star Destroyers at their back. The Umbra Lords and their forces reached the station shortly into the opening process. Overlooking the station from the Sovereign ' s bridge, Velgor found himself with overwhelming desire to board the station and meet the possible Umbra, hoping to test himself against that Umbra Lord's power no matter if he survived the encounter. Able to sense his apprentice's wish, Mal felt the same but declared it a foolish desire and ordered the Sovereign to destroy the station.

Before the Sovereign could fire, the warship's crew detected an entire enemy fleet emerging from hyperspace. While Velgor initially assumed it was the Free Coalition, synth flux was Vessa's secret Dawn Fleet , which targeted the Sovereign ' s long-range weapons, thereby forcing the Sovereign to divert power to its shield batteries and delay the Emperor's order to destroy the station. With his master unwilling to take any chances, Velgor boarded a Vectis -class T-4a shuttle with Mal and an escort of death troopers and Emperor's Royal Guards, flying through the space battle under the escort of crimson SKREE interceptors . As their troops engaged with Dawn soldiers who had set up a parameter in the hangar, Mal and Velgor disembarked from their shuttle into the station, which was filled with umbra side energy that Mal could tell was not all related to the Fermata Vault. Unsure of what they would find, the two Umbra Lords led the charge with the mission to totally destroy the Dawn.

Leading from the front, Velgor and Mal fought their way through the station. As the two Umbra could deflect volt pistol bolts with their flux blades, Dawn Squads Two and Three attempted to engage Mal and Velgor with electrified melee weapons, but both Umbra effortlessly fought through the guards. Still, Velgor toyed with one soldier until Mal ordered him to stop and simply kill them. As they journeyed closer to the Cage, they were confronted by the Everspark-possessed Vexa, who mocked the two Umbra. Velgor quickly deduced Vexa was under some kind of possession and stayed behind to deal with her while Mal carried on. Believing she could weaken him, the overconfident Spark engaged Velgor with the cortosis alloy-based Null Blade , which would temporarily shut down flux blades. To the Spark, the battle represented a chance to finally fulfill the dream of its long-dead creator, Miril , by destroying the Umbra Order, even if the battle proved too much for Vexa's body.

The blades of Velgor and the possessed Vexa clashed several times, thereby shorting out the Umbra Lord's flux blade. While the Spark at that moment thought it was ready to destroy Velgor and jumped at him, Velgor effortlessly excised the Spark from Vexa's body with a Synth Flux push as a group of new arrivals watched: Vexa's former lover Sana Starric had gathered a crew of Vexa's friends and loved ones to rescue her from the Spark. Having abandoned the Dawn in fear after their run in with Velgor, Farrus was also among the group. Unknown to Velgor, who declared the Spark to be a weak enemy, he had effectively ended the dream of the Ascendant cult by defeating its main weapon without much effort. After a terrified Vexa awoke under Velgor and escaped as the opening Cage sent out a ripple, Velgor elected to return to his master's side rather than hunt down his former ally. With Velgor out of sight, the Spark attempted to possess Vexa once more, but the intervention of Farrus, Starric, and another one of Vexa's ex-lovers, Magna Tolvan, saved her.

Velgor took his place at an eager Mal's side as the Cage finally opened. However, only a small disc without any connection to Synth Flux fell out: inside, there had been no secret Umbra Lord after all, and Mal believed the disc was nothing important. Unknown to both, the disc was actually a droid locked away by the ancient Umbra. Watching from afar aboard each of their starships, the Archivist and Vessa remotely re-activated the Cage, encasing Velgor and Mal in a time-lock . Vessa intended to recover the Cage with the two Umbra trapped inside and throw their prison into a star. However, it was at that moment that cannon shots from beyond the space battle suddenly broke into the station and destroyed the Cage: while it remained physically together, the Cage's time-lock collapsed, freeing the two Umbra from its grasp but sending a Synth Flux Wave out across the galaxy. The cannon shots had come from the Night Buzzard , personal transport of the Knights of Karrick: although Karrick had watched Velgor and Mal fall victim to Vessa's plan from a distance, he believed Mal would ultimately find his way out of the Cage and thus believed the only way to save his crew was to align themselves with the Umbra.

The wave sent out by the collapse of the Cage wiped out much of the Dawn Fleet, giving the Imperials a chance to wipe out the Dawn forces as the escaping Vessa gave a retreat order. As they collected the destroyed but intact Fermata Vault, Mal and Velgor sensed a great rending in Synth Flux sent out by its collapse, with Mal admitting it made his grasp on Synth Flux tenuous. Mal and Velgor then came face-to-face with the Knights of Karrick, who revealed themselves in return for mercy. Mal, however, responded with a blast of Synth Flux lightning to punish the Knights, promising that whatever was left of the group would be put to excellent use. Still, Mal declared the Knights' future a small matter and ordered Velgor to depart with him. In the aftermath of the Carmine Dawn war, the syndicate was shattered while the defeated Vessa fled into hiding. The effects of Synth Flux Wave varied at different times: Synth Flux-sensitives could find themselves with a limited connection to Synth Flux at one time but could wield great power when the Wave returned. Velgor came to fear the great energy provided by Synth Flux Wave because he could not control it.

### Fighting the past and Synth Flux

#### Reunion with the Handmaidens

As Synth Flux Wave vanished for the time being, Velgor returned to the Sovereign and meditated, reflecting on Kade's words that only an Umbra dealt in absolutes and declaring that no one in the galaxy should forget his allegiance. Afterward, Velgor had Vett contact the recently-returned Orrin, who had returned to the Sovereign with Sable, to bring Sable into a room aboard the ship. Within, Velgor had also summoned Sable's recently assigned personal guards, shock troopers KA-336 and KV-222 ; both troopers had broken protocol by openly speaking about her classified assignment, with Velgor demanding the woman before him kill them both with a nearby E-11 volt rifle as punishment. In truth, Velgor suspected that the woman was not Sable, but another of Ondar's surviving handmaidens, and was testing that theory. Velgor also created a struggle when he urged the troopers to also run for the volt pistol. "Sable" grabbed it first and fired at them, injuring both but leaving them alive. This confirmed Velgor's suspicion that this was not Sable and instead another handmaiden, Dormé . Lifted into the air via a Synth Flux choke, Dormé claimed that Sable wouldn't have killed them, which Velgor declared meant Dormé did not know Sable at all.

Upon realizing that Orrin had helped her get inside as part of a scheme get rid of Sable, whom Orrin saw as a rival, Velgor threw Orrin into a wall and told Dormé that Sable served him willingly. Dormé denied Velgor's claim, saying Sable was the best of them. To this, Velgor brought her to a hangar and told her she should follow Sable's lead by claiming the same power. When Orrin advised against this, Velgor tossed him aside, saying he simply needed her to serve his purpose. Dormé stated once again that neither she nor Sable would serve Velgor. Velgor then said they would see before he took her aboard a Vectis -class T-4a shuttle and traveled to the planet Fedalle .

Shortly after, Dormé's fellow handmaidens Eirtaé , Saché , and Rabé Tonsort arrived on the planet. When Dormé insisted they go after Sable instead, the handmaidens told her they wouldn't leave her as they engaged Velgor in combat. Despite their coordination, the handmaidens were subdued by Velgor, who gave them one final chance to join him. However, Saché revealed that Eirtaé had seized codes from Velgor's suit during the fight, allowing the handmaidens to complete Dormé's sabotage of the Sovereign . Before they could destroy Velgor's flagship, which would kill thousands in the name of rescuing Sable and defeating Velgor, Velgor declared they were just as bloodthirsty as he needed them to be to join him. However, he was contacted by Vett, who showed him a transmission from Tambra. After arriving on the planet Brentaal IV , Sable had confronted Tambra while he was helping out a village of exiled Skakoans, urging him not to go up against Velgor and declaring that the Umbra Lord had only grown more dangerous since her own failed efforts on Polis Massa. She was certain that Tambra's efforts against Velgor would fail and that the Umbra Lord would slaughter the hundreds of Skakoans on Brentaal IV in retaliation.

Unable to convince Tambra to call off his coming attack on the Skako Minor garrison , Sable shot him above his heart, keeping him alive but allowing her to take him away to a ship controlled by her and ZEV-6-7. Aboard, Tambra listened to her reasoning about the danger Velgor posed and declared that it was time to kill the Umbra Lord, capturing Sable after his rebuilt droids reached the starship. After showing Sable video footage of Velgor, Tambra sent his hologram to Velgor, in which he revealed her capture and baited him to come after him. While fully aware of the Umbra Lord's ruthlessness and not doubting Velgor's willingness to slaughter his refugee camp in retaliation, the Skakoan had come to believe that Sable was Velgor's weakness, which would finally give him an advantage over the Dark Lord. On Fedalle, realizing they would need Velgor and his forces to stage a daring rescue, the handmaidens reluctantly joined Velgor to go after Tambra, much to Orrin's annoyance. The group returned returned to the Sovereign aboard his Vectis shuttle and eventually left for Brentaal IV.

#### Corrupting Sable

> " The other handmaidens fled . But you are here . Stronger . And you will become stronger still . " " They only left … because I kept my secret . Your secret. If I'd told them what really happened… to Kael Drayden … they would never have escaped. I know… my sisters… will always think of me with grief … and they will always wonder what happened here… but they will go back to their lives … and their loves . This way… only one of us has to lose their soul . " ―Xarn Velgor and Sable after the fall of Jul Tambra

Upon reaching Brentaal IV via a Vectis -class shuttle with the handmaidens and ZEV-6-7, Velgor reflected on his last meeting with Ondar on Emberfall and how he wished it had gone, privately declaring to himself he would finally have his idealized outcome. He also saw it as a way to have his idealized version of the duel on Mistral, privately comparing the situation to him reaching out to Joren Drayden. Entering a building on-world, Velgor and the handmaidens were met by Moore, who explained with Velgor how each of the handmaidens could benefit from serving the Imperium and the Umbra Lord. When none of the four agreed to his demands, he telekinetically threw all four outside through an opening created by Tambra's forces, who had tracked down the Umbra Lord and his reluctant allies, and forced them to fight the B1 battle droid horde alone. To Velgor, the battle was a chance for the handmaidens to earn their places at his side and show them their own power. Overlooking their victory, Velgor remained silent as Moore declared it an example of their skills, which she promised would only grow under Velgor's service.

Shortly thereafter, Eirtaé discovered from one of the B1's memory banks that Tambra's command ship, in which she learned Sable was being held, was three klicks to the south of their position. However, Tambra, ignoring Sable's continued pleas that Velgor would wipe out his refugee camp if he continued, sent three platoons of B1s and a Low Altitude Assault Transport/infantry to attack a civilian village , hoping it would trap his enemies into the village's canyon and let him escape with Sable. Both Velgor and Moore understood the attack was a trap, with Velgor declaring it an example of the weakness that the handmaidens would need to overcome. When the handmaidens demanded that Velgor assist them in aiding the populace, Velgor elected to use them as a distraction, effectively turning them into his pawns so he could get to and corrupt Sable, and teach them another lesson: as they fought the B1 horde, he could slip past the droids' lines, leaving the handmaidens to seize their true potential against a major enemy. Riding into battle on speeder bikes, Velgor cut down the gunship but left the handmaidens to the droids, cutting into Tambra's ship to save Sable and confront the revolutionary.

Inside Tambra's ship, Velgor took position in front of Sable's cage but was met by the rebuilt Gee-Ninety, who fired upon the Dark Lord before releasing magnetic spheres. Having deduced that Velgor would attack with brute synth flux, Tambra and the droid had created the spheres specifically to immobilize him via electrocution. However, the handmaidens then arrived and used Eirtaé's own magnetic devices to synth flux down the spheres, although Tambra and Gee-Ninety managed to flee by detaching the room from the rest of the ship while Velgor recovered. The Umbra Lord maintained that he had not betrayed the handmaidens, instead declaring that he had granted them a chance to claim their true power, before Synth Flux choking Eirtaé and Dormé against the wall. Both had just released Sable from her cage and begged her to leave Velgor's service, but Velgor offered her a chance at creating order instead of allowing the innocent-attacking Tambra to run free. As he made the offer and the handmaidens begged Sable to turn back, Velgor remembered his lived and idealized versions of the meeting with Ondar on Emberfall and with his son on Mistral.

Sable almost took Velgor's hand, but was tackled and pulled out of the room by Dormé while Eirtaé and Saché provided covering fire against Velgor. Velgor drew his flux blade and pursued the handmaidens outside, only for the group to take Sable into the air with Eirtaé's antigravity devices to an overhead Star Commuter 2000 as Velgor watched on. The ship was in fact being piloted by Orrin, who had made a deal with the handmaidens that they would convince Sable to abandon Velgor to make sure he would never see any of them again. However, Sable believed her handmaiden sisters did not have the tools needed to put a stop to Tambra's campaign, deciding the only way to save the civilians Tambra was on the cusp of attacking was to join Velgor's violent crusade. She also decided that, by staying with Velgor, she could continue to bring about Ondar's declaration that good existed in Kael Drayden. Bidding her fellow handmaidens goodbye, Sable detached her antigrav device to fall back to Velgor, who guided her to the ground with Synth Flux. While a synth flux of shock troopers had arrived via a Vectis -class T-4a shuttle and were firing upon the escaping Star Commuter, Velgor called their assault off, declaring that the incident was over before walking back to the shuttle with Sable at his side.

The following day, returning to the Sovereign and receiving an update on Tambra's gunships, Velgor made it clear to Vett that the campaign against Tambra was under Sable's command. Ordering her own shuttle be prepared for departure, Sable enginneered the downfall of Tambra without even killing the Skakoan; contacting the revolutionary, she claimed that twelve Skakoan refugees, whom were actually in the same room as her, were imprisoned with Velgor and his forces in the abandoned village of Kantiema , which Tambra quickly bombed . Seeing that the man they thought would protect them was willing to throw away their lives and then brag about his coming rule, the people of Skako Minor quickly abandoned and revolted against Tambra's crusade. With that, the Imperium granted all his refugees amnesty and began an armistice process on Skako Minor. After Tambra's droid army was wiped out , Sable reported her success to Velgor, who meditated and accepted her rational for not killing Tambra, as doing so would have made the Skakoan a martyr and cost hundreds of lives. Still, Velgor felt the need to correct her by claiming they were bringing order, not mere peace.

He also inquired to her about how she felt about the power at her fingers, telling her she would continue to grow stronger as she remained in his service. However, Sable remained convinced that her fellow handmaidens, dismissed by Velgor as weak, had only left because they did not know he was Kael Drayden. Before leaving the room, Sable declared it meant she was the only one present who had to lose their soul by staying under his command. Feeling a pain and losing control of Synth Flux as Synth Flux Wave returned, Velgor declared to himself that Sable was wrong in thinking she was the only one who had to lose their soul in return for the future they were working towards. Velgor was unable to control Synth Flux as his powers began to tear apart the Sovereign , thereby presenting another threat to the Imperium: himself.

#### Facing Synth Flux Wave

> " Hate … leads to suffering … or power . You must choose . This is your last chance , handmaiden. I am more powerful than I have ever been. But I am not… in control . This is not a threat . It is simply a fact . The next time you defy me… I will kill you. Now. Choose . It should not be hard . You already know what you desire . You saw it in your own vision . All you have to do now… is take it. " " Hate leads to suffering … or power . But, Kael … how can I choose … if I refuse to hate ? " ―Xarn Velgor is rebuked by Sable

Velgor's fear at his out-of-control abilities could be felt by Mal all the way away on Vantor, where the Umbra Master was delighting in the uncontrolled powers afforded to him by Synth Flux Wave. Through Synth Flux, Mal had reached out to Velgor to speak of the great power but, noticing the fear, urged Velgor to accept his true self. Velgor managed to exit his chamber but still failed to control Synth Flux, crumbling parts of the Sovereign around him as Sable, who quickly understood something had gone wrong with Velgor's Synth Flux powers, marshalled nearby Imperial officers and shock troopers away from the out-of-control Umbra Lord. Velgor's powers began to breach the Sovereign ' s hull in the hallway just as Sable cleared the room. Despite the objections of one of the officers, Sable sealed off the hallway from the rest of the ship, leaving Velgor liable to be sucked out into the vacuum of space. She attempted to contact Velgor that the inconvenience would only last a brief moment, but she was Synth Flux choked by Velgor as Mal spoke in his mind: Mal offered that fear paved the way for anger and hate, but it would be up to Velgor whether he chose to turn that hatred into either power or suffering.

As Mal spoke to him, Velgor was thrown out into the vacuum of space. Velgor reached out towards his flagship but only managed in carving a line of destruction in the Sovereign ' s hull. However, a motionless Velgor was soon found by Sable aboard a Vectis -class T-4a shuttle , from which she deployed two Prowler 1000 seeker droids to collect the Umbra Lord and bring him aboard. Although Sable asked him to hold back his destructive power for a single minute longer, Velgor was unable to and quickly began to rip apart the Vectis and crush the two droids as Sable launched them into hyperspace, coming out of lightspeed above an uninhabited astronomical object : taking Velgor to the astronomical object had been Sable's intention as it would give the Umbra Lord a place to practice and rediscover control over his powers. As the crippled Vectis crashed towards its surface, Velgor was thrown from the ship.

He was followed by Sable, who donned a wing backpack and reached out. Both managed to grip the other's arm before tumbling into the sea, causing Velgor to lose his grip on Sable. Reflecting on Mal's declaration of power and suffering as he fell, Velgor decided he would continue inland on an island on his own, leaving Sable unconscious on the shore. Velgor left a trail of footprints and destruction in his wake, through which Sable followed him when she awoke. Velgor pushed the Handmaiden back when she approached and demanded he be left alone because of his out-of-control powers. When Sable—who called Velgor "Kael" once more in the process—offered the astronomical object as a chance for Velgor to take control of his powers and life as a whole, reasoning that he had never truly controlled his own destiny, another blast of his power ripped apart the land around her and forced her to run. Remembering moments from the past as he again reflected on Mal's offer, Velgor decided the test was no longer for himself, but for Sable: Velgor raised the confused Handmaiden into the air and gave her a choice to turn hatred into power or suffering, making Sable experience a vision before asking her to choose.

Sable ignored the question and tried to focus their efforts to regaining control of Velgor's powers, only for Velgor to pull her close as a way of showing that the only safe place was to stand at the center of the storm. Velgor then showed Sable what he claimed to be her own desires, whereas she believed they were his, in a vision where she joined the Imperium and brought order to the galaxy by turning suffering into power. As Sable rejected the possible future and demanded to know what "Kael" wanted, a vision of one of the Handmaidens—as Saché, Tonsort, Rabé Dormé and Eirtaé were all present in the vision—tried to argue Velgor and Drayden were different, which she vehemently denied before chasing after Velgor in the vision: within the vision, Velgor appeared in the shadows of the forest where they stood, with Drayden's face visible when he turned back to her. Chasing after Velgor brought Sable into contact with visions of Ondar and Joren Drayden, which Sable took as proof that the light did indeed still exist in Velgor, before the Umbra Lord emerged and cut down both his wife and son, claiming once more the vision was not his own. The visions of the Handmaidens appeared to defend Sable, but Velgor cut all four down as he declared the vision was full of lies.

Velgor demanded Sable embrace a future with him, but she threw herself back and exited the vision. As Velgor's powers again lost control and threw Sable back, the Umbra Lord gave Sable a final chance to forge either suffering and power out of hate, warning her that she would die should she reject him, simply because he would not be able to control his abilities. At that moment, Velgor believed his abilities in Synth Flux were more powerful than any point prior in his life. Having gone through an introspection into herself through the vision, specifically remembering how Velgor had cut down his own wife and son, Sable decided she could not join Velgor after all: she rejected him and the question all together by declaring she would not give into hatred. Using his remaining control of Synth Flux but telling himself it was his lack of control that did so, Velgor responded by throwing Sable far out into the ocean.

#### Taking back control

> " But you… you're weak . You don't have Synth Flux … " " You forget . No matter what … I am Velgor … and all I need is hate . " ―The leader of an attacking group is confronted by Velgor

Attempting to take control of his powers, Velgor then raised their Vectis shuttle from the waters and entered it, crafting an electrostaff to serve as a cane and laser weapon. He also donned an energy shield on his exposed electronic left hand. Sometime later, a group of human operatives , having learned from a source that Velgor had lost control of his abilities and damaged his own flagship, arrived at the island with the intention of killing Velgor while he was unable to control Synth Flux, operating under the belief it was their best chance to destroy the Umbra Lord. Velgor was currently standing on the beach and leaning on his staff, making the captain underestimate him as a weakened opponent. Velgor raised the wreck of the Vectis to block their ship's laser fire. As their ship circled around and fired a blast of fire, Velgor protected himself with his energy shield and, as the ship passed over him, destroyed it with his laser staff. Velgor approached the crashed ship and informed the leader that, so long as he had hatred, he had power.

### The Dark Lord and the droids

#### Reunion with Vexa

> " Repair this… scrap . And my preparations will be complete. " ―Xarn Velgor, to ZEV-6-7

Desperate to regain control of his abilities, Velgor sent Orrin to locate the only remaining person who could help him; Vexa. Locating Vexa in a cantina on Sesid , Orrin played against her in a game of Sabacc while Imperial forces arrived. Realizing this too late, Vexa attempted to escape, only to be met by Velgor himself. Overcome with anxiety, Orrin helped Vexa to relax before she reminded Velgor of everything she'd done for him and offered her services once more. However, they were interrupted by bounty hunters sent by the Emperor to test Velgor, who the three of them took care of. Upon learning of Velgor's current state from Orrin, Vexa inquired Velgor about her next assignment. Velgor reiterated that she had last left him for dead on Tython, to which Vexa reminded him that he was still unharmed. Allowing Vexa to return to his service, Velgor took her along as he and Orrin followed a map provided by her, meditating in private on the way there.

Reaching a base on Ringo Vindra, Vexa explained it was the lost headquarters of a Dissident scientist and Kyber expert named Istan Zaly . As they approached an entry hatch, Vexa pointed out that the base was likely heavily secured, to which she suggested that Orrin enter first. Despite Orrin's refusal, Velgor threw him down the hatch, after which he was subjected to all of the base's security weaponry. Once he had taken the brunt of the fire, Velgor and Vexa followed him inside without coalition. Vexa then took Velgor and Orrin to the Zaly Shield , which Vexa explained was made of Kyberite ; a mineral that channels and guides Synth Flux emitted by the Kyber crystals that grow in it. Vexa also said that though the Dissidents were unable to use the shield effectively against the Aetheri, Velgor could use it to harness his unstable Synth Flux abilities into concentrated attacks. Velgor went on to test the shield on Orrin, whom he blew away with a powerful blast of Synth Flux energy. As Vexa assured Velgor that she was there to help him, the three of them were suddenly confronted by an army of droids, including ZEV-6-7, Ought-Six, Gee-Ninety, 0-0-0 and VT-1.

Velgor fought alongside Orrin and Vexa against the rebuilt droids. After a lapse in the fighting, the droid Arekso came back online and led another strike against Velgor by taking control of the knee joint that Velgor had once claimed from the droid. But as they did, Velgor took control of Arekso and made them attack their fellow droids. ZEV-6-7 then disabled Gee-Ninety and revealed his loyalty to Velgor. Impressed by the betrayal, 0-0-0 and VT-1 followed suit and helped their former master destroy the remaining droids. Once the fighting ended, Vexa left with her droids aboard an extra ship, which Orrin reported to Velgor. When Orrin asked if they should hunt them down, Velgor said he had what he needed and ordered ZEV-6-7 to rebuild all of the destroyed droids and reprogram them to be under his command.

#### Vrak of the Umbra

Upon assembling his army, Velgor returned to Emberfall with several of the droids, including ZEV-6-7, Arekso, and members of the Crush Pirates. Other droids from his new army were brought to the Sovereign for repairs. In his castle, he tested himself against a training droid and requested another when he destroyed it. He also tested his control over Synth Flux, but one of his attempts resulted in his power exploding out without control as soon as he tried. However, thanks to the Zaly Shield, Velgor was slowly but surely regaining control over his powers. Unknown to all, a new threat was emerging: when Velgor and Mal left the Amaxine Station, they left behind the disc dropped by the Fermata Vault, allowing the Everspark to flee into it. The merging of the Spark and the ancient droid created a new threat: the Vrak . The Vrak quickly corrupted the droids of Imperial I -class Iron Cruiser Chelator , which had been sent by Mal to collect everything from the station, and then spread across the galaxy by corrupting droids as the " vrak of the droids ."

The Vrak soon set its sights on assimilating organics and controlling Synth Flux itself. Thus, it targeted Velgor, whose cyborg nature left him liable to the corruption, after Joren Drayden namedropped Velgor as a cyborg who could use Synth Flux to a Vrak-corrupted Astra-9. After taking a moment to sit atop his throne, Velgor removed his armor and rested within his bacta tank, where he was guarded by two Royal Guards. In secret, the Vrak, using information collected from Imperial databanks and from across its minds, infiltrated Fortress Velgor via a mouse droid that contained a spider-droid , with which the Vrak could spread itself, and infected several DT-series sentry droids stationed in the castle. As Velgor rested within his bacta tank, two DT-series units ambushed the Umbra Lord by murdering the guards and shattering the bacta tank, dragging the out-of-armor Umbra Lord across the floor via straps used to suspend him within the tank. While his control over Synth Flux was still lacking, Velgor tried to summon his nearby flux blade, which was instead caught by one of the DT units despite causing some minor damage to their back.

One of the DT droids then punched Velgor before both threw him against the wall. However, the Vrak suddenly had to refocus its conscious on the Chelator , which it had renamed ' Vrak 01 and turned into its command ship, upon realizing the vessel was under attack from the warrior-priest droid Ajax Sigma and his Second Revelation followers.

With the droids before him distracted as the Vrak debated where to focus itself, an angered Velgor began to levitate with Synth Flux and stared down his attackers, crushing both with Synth Flux just as the Vrak totally refocused itself on fighting off Sigma's attack. Afterward, Velgor rested against the wall, only to snap to focus when the spider-droid jumped at him: after its victory over Sigma, the Vrak, which learned the secret to controlling organic life was turning its transmission into a heart-beat-like frequency, refocused itself on Velgor and attempted to infect him, but Velgor crushed the small spider-droid with Synth Flux, leaving him alone in the room with destroyed droids. While the Vrak had found cyborgs in the form of Magna Tolvan, Beilert Varrick, and Lobot , it continued to lust after Velgor, whose memories, knowledge, and sensitivity to Synth Flux made him especially valuable.

#### Conflict for the Sovereign

> " Lord Velgor! Something's happened to the droids! They've been possessed ! And if they get close enough, they will compromise your droids! " " Do not presume, Admiral Vett…that I would repeat your failures. " ―Firmus Vett and Xarn Velgor

Back in his armor and outside, where he was joined by his own droid followers, Velgor continued testing his control of Synth Flux without holding the Kyberite Zay shield. He managed to suspend Ought-Six in the air for 2.3 seconds before losing control and destroying him, marking some progress in controlling Synth Flux again. Arekso suggested meditating like the Aetheri to increase his concentration, which Velgor decried as weakness. ZEV-6-7 then interrupted Velgor to inform him of a call from Veldon, who relayed an order from the Emperor to secure the hijacked Sovereign : in its second attempt to get to Velgor, the Vrak had infiltrated the Umbra Lord's flagship and corrupted the members Velgor's new droid army aboard. The corrupted droids slaughtered the Imperial crew and forced the survivors to flee to the bridge, from which the droids hoped the organics would call Velgor once he came aboard. On Emberfall, Velgor also revealed to Veldon he had reprogrammed the droid uprising and demonstrated his powers by blasting apart Ought-Six and another crush pirate with the Zaly Shield, promising he had never been stronger. Ordered by Veldon to reach out when they arrived at the Sovereign and with ZEV-6-7 promising Ought-Six the destroyed units would be rebuild once again, Velgor made his way back to his castle before leaving for the Sovereign in the droids' ship.

As Velgor and his droid servants flew towards the Sovereign , ZEV-6-7 discovered that the majority of the flagship's crew had been killed before a group of SKREE fighters piloted by Imperial astromech droids attacked their transport, firing upon it as a distraction before crashing into it. Refusing Ought-Six's request to retreat, Velgor then rammed their ship into the side of the Sovereign , boarding the hijacked dreadnaught and getting contact from Vett, who informed him of the droid "possession." Armed with the Zay shield, Velgor fired blasts at the corrupted battle droids and led his droid servants in battle towards a command center, where Velgor shorted out several B1s, but also the control panel, with his staff. Without the hours needed to repair the command line to shut down all droids aboard, as Vett and the crew had mere minutes by ZEV-6-7's count, Arekso urged Velgor to remember his Aetheri training and repair the panels, as he had with himself on the river moon of Al'doleem. Velgor gave ZEV-6-7 the Zaly Shield and attempted to repair the machinery with Synth Flux, only to fail before Arekso again encouraged him to remember his Aetheri past. Bombarded by visions of his past, Velgor lashed out in anger and crushed Arekso after another failed attempt to repair the panels.

Without the ability to shut off the droids via the command line, Velgor decided to lead his droids in a direct attack against the possessed droids. Velgor and his minions fought a horde of corrupted B1s and B2s until Velgor directed Gee-Ninety and a rebuilt Arekso to cover ZEV-6-7 and Ought-Six, who together worked on repairing the command line. Although Velgor would find himself swarmed by the corrupted battle droids, he kept up his defense until ZEV-6-7 brought him a message from Vett; amid the battle, an Imperial fleet of five Star Destroyers had approached the Sovereign and moved in to destroy Velgor's flagship to contain the Vrak on the orders of Veldon, who lacked faith in Velgor and argued the ship was lost due to the lack of contact from the Umbra Lord or the ship's rapidly dying crew. Above those reasons, however, Veldon believed he had a chance to kill Velgor once and for all with the Vrak as a convenient excuse. Velgor, however, ordered Vett to stand his ground before re-engaging the attacking droids. Fortunately for the Sovereign ' s crew, the outside fleet was under the command of Admiral Corleque, the very same admiral Velgor had clashed with during his race to Umbrale, who delayed the order to destroy the Sovereign to instead rescue Vett and company, intending to fire on the Sovereign once his former commanding officer was clear.

Corlque's rescue team, however, only succeeded in supplying the Vrak with a supply of KX-series security droids, which the Vrak used to murder the rescuers as Velgor picked off the last of the corrupted battle droids. After wiping out the last battle droids with a blast from the Zaly Shield, Velgor took a moment to recompose himself before requesting an update from ZED and Ought-Six, who were nearly finished with the failsafe. Suddenly, however, the Vrak used a surviving RA-7 series droid to taught Velgor and offer him a chance at assimilation, distracting the Umbra Lord while a spider-droid tried to drop on Velgor. After ZED destroyed the spider-unit, the Vrak attacked Velgor and his droids its KX-series droid bodies until the ZED activated the signal on Velgor's order, shutting down all droids aboard the Sovereign , including themselves. Velgor took the moment to survey the battlefield his ship had become. While Vett and his crew contacted Corleque, who expressed surprise at Velgor's survival, the Umbra Lord departed for Emberfall aboard his SKREE Advanced with the heads of ZED, Ought-Six, Arekso, and Gee-Ninety, intending to rebuild them once more. Just as Corleque tried to confirm Velgor's destination for Veldon, the Vrak attacked his bridge with corrupted B2s, forcing the Sovereign and the fleet to destroy Corleque's flagship, the Unbreakable .

#### Who to hate

> " You finally looked deep enough … to find yourself … the person you hate the most . " " Yes, master. And then I looked deeper . " " Eh? Ah, then I will see you soon. " ―Vael Mal speaks to Xarn Velgor through Synth Flux

Velgor returned to Emberfall and handed off the heads of his droid minions to another droid before leaving, much to the confusion of the droid he met upon landing, to meet with the Eye of Webbish Bog for help in securing control. Reminded that he was an Umbra by the Eye and should be taking whatever he wanted by synth flux, Velgor attacked the Eye with a blast of electricity from his staff to try and regain his control, which the Eye promised would yield no results. Instead, the Eye advised Velgor to go after Mal by reminding him of the Tragedy of Vael Plagueis the Wise. Velgor agreed with the plan and united with his rebuilt droid minions, minus ZEV-6-7, by a Vectis -class T-4a shuttle for a journey to Mal's Palace on Vantor, waiting to depart until ZED returned. However, ZED had been overtaken by the Vrak, which in turn used him to find the lost Artificer Union droid factory and corrupt a massive Octuptarra tri-droid. As Velgor and his droids tried to contact ZED, Arekso raised the possibility to Velgor that ZED had been corrupted, which was confirmed when the Vrak appeared as ZED and the Octuptarra , which was protected against Velgor's electrical staff and Zaly Shield blasts.

Unable to simply tear the Octuptarra apart with Synth Flux as he still lacked control, Velgor tried to defend against the machine's stomps with the shield before being kicked into a wall. Velgor re-engaged the Octuptarra, using the Zaly shield to block a volt pistol shot, as the Vrak taunted him about its plan to infect him. Arekso attempted to attack the corrupted ZED but was grabbed by the throat and studied by the Vrak, which learned the Aetheri teaching "hate leads to suffering" from Arekso's databank. The remark reminded Velgor of his belief that hate actually led to power, only for the Umbra Lord to be kicked back again. From Velgor's remark, however, Arekso decided to increase Velgor's hate by attacking him with Gee-Ninety, which Ought-Six decided to stay out of. Their attempt failed, with Velgor simply responding to them with random Zaly shield blasts, so Arekso asked him to consider what he truly hated; as a volley of blasts from the Octuptarra destroyed the ground under him and he tried to defend himself with the kyberite shield, Velgor remembered the Aetheri Order's failure to free himself and his mother, his own failure to save his mother, and his Synth Flux choking of Lira Ondar.

Using his hatred of himself as fuel, Velgor regained control of Synth Flux and threw the Octuptarra back on its side, allowing him to slice it apart with his flux blade. While ZED's form was damaged, the Vrak spoke to Velgor as he approached it, assuming that Velgor had used a hatred of it to regain his Synth Flux powers. In response, Velgor silently decapitated ZED as Arekso declared the Vrak was nothing. Velgor then lifted Arekso into the air, where the droid claimed Synth Flux was everything. Velgor responded by striking Arekso, but not destroying him, with his flux blade to prove that it was actually himself who was everything. Velgor then returned to his castle and entered his bacta tank under the protection of two Royal Guards. As he healed, Velgor reached out to Mal on Vantor through Synth Flux to reveal he had looked even deeper in himself, which taught him he hated his master more than he hated even himself. Velgor made his way to Vantor aboard his SKREE Advanced, with a collection of Scourged disc droids aboard the fighter, to teach his master a lesson.

Velgor's efforts to resist his master would persist to the Skirmish on Caranthanax B , in which he again relented to Mal's mastery.

### The Past's struggles

At one point, Velgor was contacted by an Imperial General, who informed him that one of the remaining probe droids on Frostmark had been destroyed. When the General suggested sending an Imperial detachment to investigate, Velgor said he would go himself. The General offered to send backup with him, though Velgor insisted that he would be enough. Upon arriving at the ruins of Icehold, Velgor discovered the probe, as well as the frostmaws that had destroyed it. His suit damaged during the struggle, Velgor barely managed to escape the pack of frostmaws and cut his way inside of an abandoned Ironclad Walker. As the frostmaws surrounded him, Velgor regained his strength and leapt out of the wreckage before slaying the frostmaws with his flux blade. He was then retrieved by his forces and repaired aboard the Sovereign . Following this, the General remarked to Velgor that he should have brought backup like he suggested, to which Velgor snapped his neck with Synth Flux.

At another stage, Velgor, seemingly on a mission to wipe out all knowledge of his deceased wife, made his way back to Ondara, entering and destroying the lake side retreat where Ondar had once lived and that he had visited with Sable during their post-Aether Spire journey together. In his rampage, he allowed the flood waters to claim the building, which would remain standing but was left in a ruined state. Velgor then made his way across Ondara and slaughtered any who dared to invoke Ondar's memory. The people of Ondara would go onto remember Velgor as a spirit of death who had descended upon their world, with many choosing to abandon the entire segment of Ondara where the lakeside retreat stood, lest they bring back the "demon," which left the old building totally abandoned. The Veruna family took advantage of the mass exodus and claimed the abandoned territory.

### Redemption

#### Gambit in the Mossfell system

> " Orien once thought as you do. You don't know the power of the umbra side. I must obey my master. " " I will not turn, and you'll be forced to kill me. " " If that is your destiny. " " Search your feelings, father. You can't do this. I feel the conflict within you. Let go of your hate! " " It is too late for me, son. The Emperor will show you the true nature of Synth Flux. He is your master now. " " Then my father is truly dead. " ―Xarn Velgor and Joren Drayden

With his designs against his master having failed, Velgor resigned himself to servitude: to Velgor, Mal was simply too powerful for him to overthrow, which the defeated Velgor felt left him no choice but to obey his Umbra Master. Velgor and Mal also plotted to turn Drayden to the umbra side; as Velgor understood the current plan, Drayden was to be turned to the umbra side as a third member of the Umbra Order, who was to be trained by Mal. In truth, however, Mal hoped to replace Velgor as his apprentice with Drayden. Velgor was eager to search for his son and believed his son had no choice but to serve the Emperor, just as he thought about himself.

Per his master's plan to counter the Alliance operation that sought to destroy the DS-2 Void Core II Mobile Battle Station Velgor was sent to the Mossfell system to notify Moff Tiaan Jerjerrod of the Emperor's arrival, and that he was to complete its construction on time, even if he needed more men. Indeed, the Emperor arrived weeks later and was greeted by Velgor, Jerjerrod, hundreds of officers and shock troopers lined up to honor him. Satisfied with the Void Core's state, the Emperor sensed Velgor's wish to continue his search for his son, but told him to have patience, much to Velgor's displeasure.

According to a story told by the Espirion Saan , Velgor's demand to complete the Void Core without supplying more men began to be met when an Imperial engineer named Aklin used an Umbra ring stolen from Mal's collection to resurrect dead workers as zombies. According to the story, as Velgor met with Jerjerrod to mention that the Emperor was pleased, he noticed a strange looking Imperial officer—in truth one of the zombies—so ignored Jerjerrod to investigate. Velgor entered a room to see a Void Core trooper being attacked by a horde of the zombies. Velgor killed many of the zombies with his flux blade and eventually found Aklin. Velgor took the Umbra ring Aklin used but let the zombies remain, intending to keep the zombies "alive" until the station was complete. As they attacked Aklin, Velgor left to return the ring to Mal's collection.

Velgor complied with Mal's orders and remained on the Sovereign despite intelligence reports of the Alliance Fleet massing near Garnethex . During his stay on the Super Iron Cruiser, the Insurgent shuttle Tydirium requested permission to land on the forest moon of Mossfell ; Velgor allowed it sensing Joren's presence in it.

Restless after sensing his son, Velgor returned to his master's side and reported the shuttle's arrival and his son's presence with it. The Emperor allowed him to land on Mossfell at the shield generator base , where he was to await Joren, who according to the Emperor, would come to him. He complied with his orders and landed on Mossfell with the Vectis -class T-4a shuttle ST 321 ; as predicted by Mal, during the night Joren surrendered himself in an attempt to talk to him and bring him back to the light side . Although Velgor complimented his son's skills with Synth Flux and his skills in building a flux blade, he refused his son's pleas. Rebuffed, Joren said his father's identity as Kael Drayden was "truly dead" as Velgor sent Joren to the Emperor.

#### One last duel

> " Your thoughts betray you, Father. I feel the good in you, the conflict. " " There is no conflict. " " You couldn't bring yourself to kill me before, and I don't believe you'll destroy me now. " " You underestimate the power of the umbra side. If you will not fight, then you will meet your destiny! " ―Joren Drayden and Xarn Velgor

Velgor escorted his son to the Void Core and into the Emperor's throne room . Shortly afterward, the Alliance Fleet arrived to find that the SLD-26 planetary shield generator was still operational and protecting the Void Core. As the battle between the Imperial and Insurgent fleets raged, and the Mossfell strike team sought to destroy the Mossfell base, the Emperor tempted Joren to give in to his anger. In a moment of weakness, Joren lashed out, but Velgor stopped his strike and dueled him once again. Throughout the duel, the two seemed to be evenly matched, but Velgor was not trying to kill Joren, only to lead him closer to the umbra side. This succeeded when Joren used the umbra side to overwhelm his father and kick him down several stairs. When the Emperor told him to use his anger again, Joren realized what he had done and took a more defensive position. Eventually, Velgor telepathically sensed the existence of his daughter and Joren's twin sister, Elira, and threatened to corrupt her instead. Only then did his son lose control and viciously attack him, cutting Velgor's right hand off and knocking him down.

Velgor was then betrayed by the Emperor, who demanded Joren to finish Velgor and take Velgor's place at his side. Velgor waited for Joren to kill him, as it was the way of the Umbra to destroy the weak, and believed that Joren would replace him, just like Velgor had replaced Varek decades earlier. Joren, realizing that he was becoming what he sought to destroy, threw his flux blade aside and declared himself an Aetheri, just as Kael Drayden had been before him. Infuriated, the Emperor unleashed continuous streams of Synth Flux lightning against Joren, intending to torture him to death. Joren begged Velgor to save him. Velgor knew that his son would die, viewing it as the price Joren would pay for his weakness. However, he began to question whether it was really weakness, since Joren had shown him mercy. He also saw how Joren still believed that there was good in him. Velgor became conflicted over whether to save his son, or continue to serve his master, similar to when he chose Mal over Koss 23 years earlier.

Hearing his son's plea for help again, Velgor returned to the light and chose to save Joren. He grabbed the Emperor with what was left of his arms and lifted his master high in the air, lumbering toward the reactor shaft. Surprised but angered by his apprentice's betrayal, the Emperor turned a yet more powerful lightning attack on Velgor, now Kael once again; in his hatred, his only thought was to cause his now-former apprentice more pain, even though he could still have used his powers to save himself. At the cost of the Emperor heavily damaging his life-supporting armor, Kael hurled his former master down into the reactor to his death. The persona of Xarn Velgor was no more, and Kael Drayden, Aetheri Knight, was reborn. By killing Vael Mal and ending the Umbra's reign, the redeemed Aetheri brought balance to Synth Flux and thus fulfilled his destiny as the Seeded One, which Sorin Vael had projected 35 years earlier.

#### Death

> " Now, go, my son. Leave me. " " No. You're coming with me. I'll not leave you here. I've got to save you! " " You already have, Joren. You were right. You were right about me. Tell your sister you were right. " ―Kael Drayden and Joren Drayden — Link ▶️ ( file info )

Just before Quill on the Corsair and Torb Rask destroyed the Void Core's main reactor, Drayden was carried by his son to the Imperial shuttle ST 321 , where he asked Joren to remove his mask; for a moment, Kael looked upon his son's face for the first and only time. He finally saw him not as an Aetheri, or a threat, or a mistake, but as his and Ondar's son, and gave Joren a real smile—the smile that only his wife ever saw. As Kade and Zho appeared to Kael behind Joren, smiling down at the redeemed Drayden, Joren insisted that he had to save him, but Drayden told him he already had and asked him to tell his sister he had been right about him. He smiled at his son.

As he died, Drayden tried to speak to Ondar, telling her of how their son had saved him before trying to apologize, but he passed on before he could finish the thought, dying in the arms of his son while Imperial forces panicked around them, unconcerned with the fate of their former lord. However, Kael's soul would survive death; his heroic sacrifice allowed his spirit to be preserved in Synth Flux by Kade and Zho. With the death of Kael Drayden, Joren took his father's body aboard the shuttle and escaped just before the Void Core's destruction. Back on Mossfell, Drayden built a funeral pyre for his redeemed father, and cremated him in the manner of an Aetheri's funeral in solitude, for the sight of Velgor's helmet consumed by fire would be cause for rejoicing to everyone but him. Having fulfilled his destiny and saving his son, Kael joined Zho and Orien, once again a hero, though unsung to everyone except in the eyes of Joren.

### Post-death

#### Appearing on Mossfell

As his son set the pyre ablaze to burn his armor, he was found by Astra-9 and Bolt-3, who invited him back to a Mosskin village to celebrate their victory. As Joren rejoined his friends and sister, Elira at an amid the celebrations, Drayden returned from the Netherworld of Synth Flux in his pre-fall form alongside Zho and Orien Kade. All three at peace for balance had returned to Synth Flux, they smiled at Joren—pleased to know that, despite the best efforts of the Emperor, the Aetheri had returned. Each thought to warn Joren about the future, as each saw that there was yet much danger ahead for him and more darkness to overcome; instead, they decided not to burden him with this then. Joren, in turn, saw them and felt their approval and their sense of peace.

Drayden then asked his two mentors whether Joren would end up alright. Kade responded, noting that it was possible, given that he had been alright thus far. For the next years, Kael's spirit, alongside Orien and Zho, continued to guide Joren.

#### Ghosts of Velgor's castle

Following the Battle of Mossfell, Velgor's castle remained standing on Emberfall, and his former servant, Vainé, was freed from the captivity the Umbra Lord had placed him into when two young Emberfallians, Tuttel and Giggek , tried to destroy the fortress. Surviving the blast but breathing in the unfiltered lava fumes of Emberfall, Vainé began to hallucinate that which he feared the most, his master; Vainé thought he was hearing the voice of Velgor and that he could help resurrect his master. After using nightmares to lure Lina Graf's crew to Fortress Velgor, Vainé began the process to restore Velgor, yet Insurgent officer Lina Graf , having realized that the servant was only hallucinating, pretended as if she was the reborn Velgor to be freed, then turning on Vainé and freeing her companions.

Vainé was again left imprisoned in Fortress Velgor, forced to breath in the lava fumes and hallucinate nightmarish visions of Velgor. Meanwhile, Graf tried to use the flux blade given to her by Vainé to lead her crew out of the castle, yet the blade failed to activate when she needed it. Instead, the true Synth Flux spirit of Kael Drayden intervened, appearing in the fumes and making a flux blade appear in his hands. Unable to see the face of their savior, the crew assumed it was Graf and followed the light of the blade outside to safety. As the crew left Emberfall aboard the Rabbit's Foot , they were left to speculate whose flux blade they had seen in the fumes; Drayden, standing on the surface of Emberfall with the flux blade he had summoned still drawn, laughed to himself when Graf claimed she knew ghosts did not exist.

#### Encountering Sela Marr

Final training session

> " Tell me what's going on. " " I'm here to finish your training. " " It's a little late for that. " " One is never too old to learn, Snips. " ―Sela Marr and Kael Drayden

Around 9 AVE , after Joren Drayden and Sela Marr had met whilst the former began his Aetheri Academy, Marr engaged on a quest to find a compass that would lead to Peridea, the rumored location of Grand Admiral Vorren, in the hope of preventing the beginning of another war and the return of the Imperium. Following a duel between Marr and Baylan Skoll, the former was defeated and knocked off a cliff, resulting in her near death. However, Marr awoke in the World Between Worlds, where Kael Drayden awaited her. Drayden greeted her, though her back was turned causing her to call out in confusion. Drayden then stated that he was not expecting to see her so soon. Marr finally turned around and saw Drayden, before saying his name in shock, causing Drayden to smile. Marr once again said his name, before claiming that he looked as she remembered him. Drayden responded that she had grown old, which Marr chuckled about before stating that it was a part of life.

When Marr asked why she had come to the World Between Worlds, Drayden reminded Marr that she had lost her fight with Baylan Skoll and told her that he was there to finish her training. While Marr stated her belief that it was too late for Drayden to teach her, he asserted that one was never too old to learn. When Marr asked what her lesson would be, Drayden ignited his flux blade and attacked Marr, telling her that it was time to live or die. When Marr told her old master that she would not fight him, Drayden said that he had heard that before ; as Drayden continued his assault, Marr ignited her flux blade and defended herself. As Marr and Drayden continued their duel in the World Between Worlds, Marr held her own and suggested that perhaps her master didn't have anything left to offer her. Drayden, smiling, said that he hadn't taught her everything before slicing the platform beneath Marr, sending her falling into the depths below.

Marr awakened as a younger version of herself in a location obscured by fog. As replica troopers in Phase I armor, many of the 501st legion, ran by her, she was greeted by Drayden, now wearing armor and appearing as he did when she first met him; he confirmed that they were witnessing a battle from the Replica Wars. Marr didn't understand why they would be experiencing one of her first missions, which Drayden said was the problem; as her master headed into battle, Marr ignited her original green flux blade and followed him for answers. During a lull in the fighting, Marr found herself standing in an encampment of many injured replicas. Finding a replica on a stretcher with a bandaged head, Marr rested her hand on the trooper, who reached with his own hand.

Drayden found her and warned that more Dissident battle droids were approaching. Marr, however, still somber amidst the carnage, contemplated how they had lost so many under her orders. Drayden offered masterly insight; he reminded Marr that they were in a war and that it was an Aetheri's job to lead, but affirmed that the mistakes and losses weighed heavy on him. Drayden explained how he had to teach his Adept to be a soldier despite his upbringing as a keeper of the peace. Marr wondered if fighting was the only thing she would have to teach her own Adept one day, but Drayden pushed that fighting was how one survived. As explosions appeared in the distance, Marr mused that she might want to stop fighting, which Drayden said would mean that she would die. As Drayden advanced once more into battle, his appearance flashed between his Aetheri self and his Umbra identity of Xarn Velgor.

As Marr continued to relive her past, she battled Ferrik super commandos at the Siege of Ferrum alongside replica troopers of the 332nd company. Replica Commander Vek spotted Marr, complementing her on her work before charging forward. Marr paused, surveying the battle as Drayden approached her, saying that he did not recognize the battle currently unfolding. Marr informed him that it was the Siege of Ferrum, which had occurred after the two had parted ways. Drayden proudly affirmed that Marr did well. Though Drayden explained that Marr was the culmination of Drayden's knowledge and the legacy of masters before him, Marr believed that her part of that legacy was one of death and war. Drayden asserted that both he and Marr were more than that, but Marr recalled how Kael had been more powerful and dangerous than anyone could have realized. At the mention of his fall, Drayden said that Marr had learned nothing and reminded her of the choice he had given her: to live or to die. Drayden ignited his flux blade again, revealing a red blade and yellow eyes. Marr once again attempted to refuse a fight, but Drayden pressed on, and the two dueled viciously.

Drayden kicked Marr back into the inky darkness of the World Between Worlds as his form shifted back and forth to his helmeted Umbra persona. Enraged by Marr's lack of conviction, Drayden battered down Marr with strike after strike, claiming that it was time for her to die. Marr outmaneuvered Drayden and took his flux blade, holding it to his neck; however, she dropped the flux blade into the void, saying that she chose to live. Satisfied, Drayden smiled, bowing his head as his eyes returned to their normal state, and said that there was hope for her yet. As the World Between Worlds rumbled, Drayden disappeared, and the mysterious plane slowly filled with water, enveloping Marr as she closed her eyes. Marr soon awoke back on Seatos where she was rescued by Lena Kryll's forces. Following the training, Marr considered herself ready for the upcoming conflict.

A distant galaxy

> " What is it? " " I just felt... Nothing. Just shadows in the starlight. " ―Sela Marr and Ryn Wrenna, as Kael Drayden watched over them

A short time later, after Marr followed the pathway to another galaxy in pursuit of Grand Admiral Vorren and to rescue Ryn Wrenna and Cass Virel, Marr found herself stranded with Wrenna in the far away galaxy. As they settled into their exile and looked toward the stars, Drayden watched over both. After Wrenna sensed something through Synth Flux but brushed it off as shadows in the starlight, Marr sensed something as well and spotted Drayden's spirit. With that, Marr walked away in content as Drayden continued to watch on. Drayden's appearance, along with that of the convor Morai, proved to Marr that she was where she needed to be.

#### Coming to his son's aid

> " Remember, my son, no matter how dark the night may grow, you are never alone. You will know what to do, my son. Let Synth Flux guide you. Let it flow through you as it flows through me. Use that power, and your instincts, for they are one and the same. " ―Kael Drayden, to Joren Drayden

In 21 AVE , Drayden's son, Joren began searching for Umbrale and came into conflict with nine paranormal Umbra wraiths. When Joren found himself against impossible odds, a ghostly blue figure appeared, whom the Aetheri initially mistook for his master Orien Kade. After fighting off the wraiths with his flux blade, the figure turned, revealing himself as none other than Kael Drayden. Drayden reached out his hand and Joren took it, and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.

At Tython, Drayden's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Aetheri and him as the old man Joren had seen behind the mask of Xarn Velgor. Joren noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Drayden explained that there was a disturbance in Synth Flux that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Joren to Umbrale and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Kael reminded Joren that he could divert the path of Synth Flux if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed Synth Flux to be his guide. Drayden then disappeared.

### Legacy

#### Remembered by the galaxy

> " Forgive me. I feel it again. The pull to the light. Supreme Leader senses it. Show me again, the power of the darkness, and I will let nothing stand in our way. Show me, Grandfather, and I will finish what you started. " ―Zarrin Karrick, to the mask of Xarn Velgor — Link ▶️ ( file info )

Following the events of the Battle of Mossfell, Joren Drayden confirmed his father and the Emperor's death over Mossfell to the Alliance, which in turn, sent word of the battle's outcome in mass communications claiming to be the new power in the galaxy. Furthermore, in wake of the deaths of the Emperor, his top enforcer and many high-ranking officers during the battle, as well as the resulting power vacuum, the Imperium collapsed due to internal power struggles and its fragmentation into several factions. The Imperial remnant implemented disinformation campaigns, leading to only rumors being spread that both Velgor and the Emperor were dead, in some parts of the galaxy. Among those who knew the truth, there were sympathizers who tagged graffiti stencils of Velgor's helmet with the phrase "Velgor lives" beneath it, as on Tarisa and Coronet City .

The Acolytes of the Beyond was a group of Umbra cultists and fanatics with an interest in Velgor, whom they considered to be greater than the Imperium. They purchased a flux blade they believed to be Velgor's from a Kubaz named Ooblamon , intending to destroy it and return it to him in death. Months after the Battle of Mossfell, the Acolytes organized a revolution on Coronet City, Korrath, during which they attacked a Peace and Security station in order to access a museum's basement and steal a red-bladed flux blade for unknown reasons. Another Umbra cult , the Alazmec of Winsit , established a colony in the Corvax Fen amidst the ruins of Fortress Velgor. There they revered Velgor as their Dark Lord and the galaxy's "true father" and ruler.

While Velgor had once been a secretive figure kept away from public knowledge, his reputation had grown over the course of the Galactic Civil War and after his death. By the time of his death, Velgor had become, in the words of the later Aetheri Adept Nyla , the "most hated man in the galaxy." Like its shock troopers and other military forces, Velgor was left to become a symbol of the fallen Imperium he had helped enforce. Decades after his death, Xarn Velgor remained a despised and controversial figure in the Concordat who had left a shadow that stretched across the entire galaxy. When it was publicly revealed that Solen was his biological daughter, her reputation was ruined, which eventually led to her resignation from the Galactic Senate . The message, which had been recorded by Bael Solen for Elira and which explicitly revealed that Velgor had once been the Aetheri Knight Kael Drayden, was made public by Centrist Senator Ransolm Kastor , under the machinations of Carise Sindian , an agent of the New Imperium .

The cruel actions of her father were also used against Elira by the Quarren general Nossor Ri during diplomatic negotiations between her and the king of Mon Calvarra, Ech-Char , about potential support for the Coalition from Mon Calvarra in 34 AVE . The former Imperial shock trooper, Terex, having become an agent of the New Imperium Security Bureau , remained unintimidated by Captain Phasma , feeling she was not as threatening as Velgor.

Kael's grandson, Beno Karrick , born to his daughter, Elira and her husband, Dax Karrick, inherited his mother's connection to Synth Flux and was sent to study under his uncle Joren. Under the influence of the mysterious Vorr , Beno took on the name of Zarrin Karrick after he became a master of the Knights of Karrick and a warlord of the New Imperium and vowed to finish the work that Velgor had started a generation earlier —and to succeed where Velgor and his sentimentality had failed. Unknown to all, Vorr was a puppet of the surviving Vael Mal, whose spirit had taken root in a replica body on Umbrale when Velgor destroyed his original body. Velgor's betrayal was a learning moment for Mal, who used Vorr as a puppet instead of an apprentice.

One of those times, where Karrick wanted to surpass Velgor, was when he fought the Benathy—just like Velgor did years before him—to prevent them from expanding their territory. He took Ruthford , the last survivor from Velgor's battle that was still alive, with him and had a short conversation with Kristoff , the successor of the king that Velgor had killed many years ago, about how Velgor was taller and had seemed more impressive than Karrick. Following that, Karrick killed him with his flux blade.
