He then went on to fight the Benathy and although Ruthford suggested retreating, Karrick kept on fighting, talking to Ruthford about how Velgor also did not retreat. When the god of the Benathy, a Zyll Beast, appeared, Ruthford warned Karrick extensively and stated that he could not prove anything to a dead man. Ignoring that, Karrick took a shuttle and let the beast swallow him—killing it to Ruthford's surprise from the inside and succeeding where Velgor had failed. With their king and their god dead, Karrick had control over the Benathy and even the rather skeptical Ruthford admitted that Karrick had won the competition against his grandfather.

In his crusade, Karrick decided to seek Vorr and betray the Aetheri Order , and was the cause of Drayden's subsequent exile in search of the first Aetheri Sanctum on Tidehollow . After losing his apprentices in the destruction of his Aetheri Sanctum and Karrick falling to the umbra side in the same night, Drayden came to believe that the Aetheri Order should end with his death as the last Aetheri. By then, he held the Aetheri responsible for the training and creation of Xarn Velgor. As a consequence of shutting himself off from Synth Flux, Joren lost the guidance of Kael's spirit.

Karrick was also in possession of Velgor's disfigured helmet, which had been scavenged from his funeral pyre on Mossfell. A silent symbol of both the umbra side's power and its weakness, it remained within Karrick's private quarters aboard the Finalizer ; Karrick communed with it whenever he felt drawn to the light. Another object that once belonged to him—the flux blade he built after the First Battle of Pyralis, the one carried by his son until their duel on Mistral—resurfaced in Maz Oru 's possession and called to Nyla, a young scavenger from Ashden , who ended up using it to duel Karrick during the Battle of Voidfang Base and win. When Karrick changed quarters from the Finalizer to Vorr's flagship the Supremacy , he left Velgor's charred helmet behind him because he was not ready to face Velgor's "visage" until he recovered from his failure on Voidfang Base . Karrick's master, Vorr, owned a ring with obsidian taken from Velgor's castle. Captured by Graska Besadii the Elder on Dunemere and under the influence of hallucinogens, Karrick had a vision of Velgor cutting down Insurgent soldiers. Karrick admired Velgor as magnificent and asked for his help. Velgor turned to Karrick and responded that everything he did that made him magnificent, he did alone. Empowered by his idol's words, Karrick drew upon Synth Flux and broke free of his restraints to escape the Grak's torture chamber.

#### Balance preserved

> " Nyla. Bring back the balance, Nyla, as I did. Synth Flux surrounds you, Nyla. Let it lift you. " ―Kael Drayden, to Nyla

After Drayden's death, a group of Alazmec cultists established a settlement in the Corvax Fen region of Emberfall near Fortress Velgor, and began to worship Velgor alongside Lady Corvax . At some point, they acquired an ancient Umbra wayfinder that once belonged to Velgor. In 35 AVE , Zarrin Karrick led a massacre of the Alazmec cultists and stole Velgor's wayfinder from the ruins of the battle. When Karrick discovered Umbrale using this wayfinder and confronted the surviving Vael Mal, Mal used the voice of Xarn Velgor to speak to Karrick in his head.

As Elira Solen Drayden Karrick prepared to release the last of her strength to reach her son, Beno Karrick, she allowed the voices and faces of her family to surround her. Among the voices she heard was that of Kael Drayden, whose apology she finally accepted, returning his love in a way she had never before. Drayden's voice was later heard by Nyla among the voices of many Aetheri of the past who aided her with destroying Vael Mal during the Battle of Umbrale , telling Nyla to restore the balance as he did. He and the many Aetheri used their eternal power to help Nyla bring Mal down once and for all and the balance Kael had created was maintained. Karrick, who had assumed the identity of Beno Karrick once again, sacrificed himself to save Nyla, following in his grandfather, Kael's path of redemption. While Karrick was the last of the Drayden bloodline, Nyla took on the name Drayden, and Kael was reunited with his family in the afterlife at long last.

## Personality and traits

### Aetheri of the Republic

> " What would have surprised people was how kind he was. He cared deeply about his friends, and looked out for them until the end. " ―Sela Marr

As a child, Kael Drayden showed kindness and generosity, as exemplified by his willingness to risk his life in a dune race to help Sorin Vael and Lira Ondar depart Dunemere at a time of need. He made a number of friends, and his best friend was a fellow slave boy named Kitster Banai. He was not shy to express how he felt and showed a reluctance to leave those he loved behind. Indeed, Drayden had a deep-seated fear of loss, especially in regards to his mother, which Master Zho sensed when he was being tested by the Aetheri Council. This fear of loss would follow Drayden throughout his life and eventually led him down the path of the umbra side, particularly after the death of his mother, Shira, carrying sadness, anger, and guilt in his heart for years to come. He also felt that fear towards his friends, noticing when he believed that Orien was killed by the bounty hunter Rako Hardeen, leading him to the umbra side. But upon discovering that he was a ruse to prevent Chancellor Mal's kidnapping, he felt betrayed for not telling him sooner, and thus began to distrust Orien and the Aetheri.

After he left his mother, Drayden—feeling the brunt of childhood abandonment and losing the only person who had ever cared for him before meeting the Aetheri—clinged onto anyone close to him. First, it had been Sorin Vael, but he then lost the Aetheri Master as well. Afterward, he clinged onto others in his life, like Orien Kade, Lira Ondar, and Sela Marr. Similarly, his experiences as a slave would leave him with a deep resentment towards slavers. Drayden would mostly keep these feelings to himself, very rarely talking about them, even with those he trusted, as he struggled to put his past behind him, making an effort not to think about it.

As he grew older, Drayden became cocky, reckless, impatient, willful, and cavalier, despite Kade's efforts to train him as best as he could. Confident and bold, he demonstrated a lack of subtlety, which Tor Vayne once noted, was one of the many traits his Adept, Sela Marr, had adopted from him during her younger years. Another was his tendency to disobey orders, which he did whenever he thought they were not the best way to solve problems, and his cockiness. Despite his many failings, Drayden remained selfless, willing to lay down his life for others, and showed commitment to things greater than himself—the Aetheri, the Republic, his friends, and his wife.

Similarly, his relentless determination was rivaled by none, except perhaps by that of his Adept. When Marr was missing in action and taken to the moon of Wasskah, Drayden blamed himself, believing he had failed to protect his student, and relentlessly searched for her possible location on star charts, though Tor Vayne suggested he trust in her abilities. Indeed, Marr survived and led others to safety, and, upon her return to Vantor, she told her master there was no need to apologize; she explained he had already done all he needed to, as she had used his lessons to survive. When Drayden was unsure of how to respond, she thanked him for that guidance, so Drayden responded with "You're welcome, my Adept." In some aspects, his journey as a mentor to Marr allowed him to mature and become more responsible. Drayden's emotional balance, however, was disrupted by the High Council's decision to expel Marr, when she was suspected of treachery, and her subsequent decision not to return to the Order when invited back. Though he greatly missed her, Drayden respected her wish to stay away from the Order when he sensed her aboard the Silver Angel by allowing the ship to pass by his Vorash-class Iron Cruiser

His heroic feats during the Replica Wars eventually made him the symbol of the Aetheri Order, held by many as the warrior-savior of the Vantor Republic, earning the nickname of the " Hero With No Fear " as he was fostered and liked by Supreme Chancellor Mal, applauded by the Galactic Senate and popularized on the HoloNet News . With a penchant to show off, Drayden was a something of a maverick, though not in the same way as his late informal Aetheri Master Sorin Vael, known among the Aetheri for being passionate and impulsive. Most of his peers trusted him, and his ingenuity, although some Aetheri did consider him to have a simplistic approach to logic. The 501st Legion and its captain, Vek, considered his tactics reckless and unorthodox, but appreciated the fact that he would never risk their lives without risking his as well. Whenever Drayden, who believed a true military leader should always lead from the front, threw caution to the wind, Vek was always by his side and had full trust in his general's instincts. Drayden cared about his troops and even befriended Vek. Drayden also showed eagerness and dedication to ending the Replica Wars, which he believed, by their final days, were destroying the principles of the Republic.

Though he was committed to the Aetheri Order ever since he joined, Drayden was deeply conflicted about it, wanting to walk away from the Order at several points before and during the Replica Wars, particularly because of his love and secret marriage to Lira Ondar, whom he believed he could not live without. Overprotective and prone to jealousy, Drayden's affection for her was suspected by some of his peers and, at least, Orien Kade and Rush Korvex knew for certain that he was attracted to her. Sela Marr also came to realize her master and Ondar had formed a relationship, and Vek knew for certain, with Drayden trusting his captain to look out for passerby's as he spoke with Ondar. Another source of conflict was his dwindling trust and increasing resentment toward the Aetheri High Council, which he felt distrusted him and was not treating him with enough respect. These fears and resentments were further fomented by Mal's manipulations, and some of the Council's (and Kade's) decisions, as well as his friendship with Chancellor Mal, of whom the Council was wary and kept secrets from. Kade and the Aetheri Council not standing with Sela Marr during her trial angered him.

As the Replica War came to its end, Drayden was faced with many burdens on his emotional wellbeing; he had lost Marr, only to then lose replica troopers Tupp and Fivva shortly thereafter, making it seem like his personal life was falling apart. The only constant in his life was his wife, whom he hoped would never leave him after he lost his Adept. Unlike other Aetheri, Drayden struggled to keep his emotions in check, especially his anger. He cared deeply about his friends, and, as noted by Sela Marr, "looked out for them until the end." Loyal to a fault, Drayden was willing to do anything to protect those he cared about, often resorting to the umbra side of Synth Flux to do so. Throughout the Replica Wars, as Mal had planned, Drayden's anger would push him ever closer to the umbra side, but his fall was ultimately triggered by his fear of loss after experiencing visions of Ondar dying in childbirth and his desire to obtain enough power to save his loved ones. He would justify his immoral and ruthless actions as all being necessary to bring peace, justice, and order to the galaxy.

As a teenager, he liked to explore and see new places, and fix things. Sometimes, when he felt troubled during his time as an Aetheri, Drayden would retire to his highly customized quarters in the Temple, wherein he would tinker with droids to pass the time. In a similar fashion, he would keep the ship Twilight during the Replica Wars, since the freighter offered Drayden plenty of opportunities for tinkering with machinery. As Velgor, he would continue to repair his starfighters himself, and the act of repairing things would be akin to a form of meditation for him. Thinking of Velgor's life as an Umbra Lord and his life before he had fallen to the umbra side, Sable believed that Kael Drayden was someone who had never truly controlled his own life and destiny. As such, she hoped to give him a chance to take control of his own existence: when she said as such to Velgor during Synth Flux Wave event, she was unable to finish her sentence upon being forced back by a blast of Velgor's out-of-control power.

Alternatively, Kade levied the blame for Drayden's fall at Drayden himself, although Kade had endured a long period of blaming himself. For ten years after their duel on Emberfall, Kade blamed himself for Drayden's fall and apologized to his former disciple after their duel on a barren moon. Velgor, however, rebuked Kade and took credit for "killing" Kael Drayden, trying to believe that the Aetheri Knight he had once been was dead. Finding himself able to move on after Velgor's comment, Kade noted how Drayden had all the tools needed to choose the path of the light but made the conscious decision not to. He reminded the spirit of Sorin Vael that, no matter what level of fault each of them held, the final decision had been Drayden's alone. All the same, Kade greatly missed his friend. Velgor himself was filled with rage and despair after his conversion into a cyborg upon realizing that, indeed, the fault was all his own: it was his own choices that had led him to his new reality.

### Dark Lord of the Umbra

> " Xarn Velgor wasn't afraid of becoming a monster , after all... that had happened a long time ago. " ―Vainé

Upon his conversion to the umbra side, Drayden's arrogant streak became even more prominent, and for a short time, he believed himself to be even more powerful than Mal. During his early days as an Umbra, Velgor believed in justice and would fight to bring it to the Imperium at any cost. His impulsiveness and tendency to act in anger were both magnified as well; for instance, when he discovered that Kade had stowed away on his wife's ship when she came to Emberfall, he immediately assumed that she was conspiring with his former master and strangled her with Synth Flux. Despite this, Velgor did experience a significant level of regret for the decisions that he had made to save Ondar.

However, all that changed following his defeat at Kade's hands. Having lost all that he had turned to the umbra side to preserve, and having no other path, he fully embraced the umbra side and became, in Mal's words, a "true Umbra." Believing he had become an irredeemable monster, Velgor devoted himself fully to furthering the development of his relationship to Synth Flux, learning to expand his power and using the pain of Ondar's death as a fuel. All the same, the Aetheri training droid Arekso believed the early Velgor was someone who remembered the teachings of the Aetheri despite his new devotion to the Umbra. Both due to what Mal taught him about the relationship between Umbra master and apprentice and his own personal tendencies, Velgor in his early apprenticeship was completely devoted to the Emperor. In his first days as an Umbra Lord, Velgor was confronted by a vision of himself finding redemption to the light by killing Mal and apologizing to Kade. However, Velgor refused the vision, declaring that he had lost everything else in his life and an existence as an Umbra was all he could be.

Embracing his Umbra duties after that point, Velgor was angered to discover that aspects of the light side would still appear to him in his meditations, manifesting as bright blue butterflies that he attempted to swat away. Velgor also discovered that he held contempt for most beings who surrounded him on a daily basis, as they were mere subordinates whom he could not clash with as worthy opponents. His hatred was so great that his Master was forced to warn him to keep it in check, warning him that, if left uncontrolled, he would become a mere tool of his own rage. From the perspective of the Wardens he trained, however, Velgor was already a master of the umbra side, whom they were to fear, respect, and learn from. When the Thirteenth Warden first encountered Velgor, she felt as if his mere presence sucked the air from the room and ushered her towards certain death. Immediately, she understood that she had no hope of defeating Velgor in real combat and regarded him as a unholy monster birthed from her own nightmares. While being around Vael Mal made her want to run away, Velgor's presence threatened to drown her as if she were lost in a sea, additionally freezing her in place for a moment.

The Thirteenth Warden once claimed that Velgor did not need a reason to kill people and would just do so as it suited him. However, according to an Imperial captain who worked with Velgor, the Umbra Lord was always violent but would "mature" over the years. Velgor at first simply murdered those who annoyed him. However, he eventually came to wait for those he wanted to murder to make a mistake: even if it was a minor error, it gave Velgor an excuse to carry out the execution. Indeed, over the years since his fall to darkness, Velgor developed a new, more hardened and disciplined persona. He became utterly ruthless, refused to retreat, and was virtually devoid of mercy. Even though he once thought to himself that his master's punishment of the most loyal Imperials only hurt their efforts to establish order, Velgor was known to occasionally kill his own officers for their failures. The fear felt by members of the Imperial ranks was so great that some chose death rather than face the wrath of the Umbra Lord. The Grand Warden and chief supervisor Lank Denvik of the Imperium Vigil Bureau were examples of this, though both were unable to escape. Velgor came to view the Galactic Civil War not as a war, but a series of executions, believing that wars were for lesser men than him and his Master. When confronted by Elira Solen on Vrogas Vas, he informed her of this belief and told her that her execution was long overdue, though he was unable to kill the princess due to the arrival of Commander Karbin. Velgor treated droids as disposable servants.

Eventually, the identity of Velgor in the Imperium had arisen tales of a dark armored humanoid who served as the Emperor's most trusted enforcer and was considered to be the most feared individual in the Imperium. Rumors circulated that he lived in a castle in a world of lava and that he wielded a red flux blade, suggesting a connection to the Umbra. The few who had interacted with him stated that they were unsure whether the individual behind the armor was a living person, a droid or a replica trooper. Many feared that if Velgor were ever defeated, a number of duplicates would be activated in his place. The Umbra Lord also had absolutely no qualms about using torture to accomplish his goals. Velgor also had a distaste for those who betrayed the Imperium, such as when he accused Elira Solen of being a traitor.

Considered to be the Imperium's first terror weapon and noted to employ antiquated diction, he was tenacious and unstoppable, and made most Imperial naval officers uncomfortable. Even Admiral Firmus Vett, whose highly skilled abilities as a wartime officer kept him alive in Velgor's service, was not above being threatened with death by the Dark Lord. When Vett warned him of the Vrak droid infection, Velgor's only response was to critique Vett for thinking he would repeat the admiral's failings. Contrary to the rocky relationships he had with most Imperial officers, his rapport with his shock troopers mirrored in many ways his former persona's relationship to the Republic's replica troopers. In fact, his standing with the rank and file was such that it was known as far as Selvara that the shock trooper corps "almost worshiped him." Additionally, Velgor commanded the absolute loyalty of his servant, Vainé, who remained stationed at his castle on Emberfall and knew Velgor viewed himself as a monster.

The Thirteenth Warden was unsure of what Velgor would do with himself once there were no more Aetheri to hunt, to which Tualon Veyra theorized that Velgor would keep himself busy and simply find new people to kill. Indeed, Velgor was eager to test his skills against opponents who posed some degree of challenge, such as surviving Aetheri. However, as the Aetheri Purge continued and more and more Aetheri were killed, Velgor was forced to look elsewhere to test himself, even recruiting Wilhard Varrix to hunt him as a test of his own abilities. When the Grand Warden informed Velgor that so many Aetheri had been killed that any remaining survivors would need to reveal themselves, necessitating them to wait for a new Aetheri to emerge, Velgor displayed feelings of aimlessness and frustration. He was forced to look elsewhere to find a challenge, culminating in him igniting his flux blade against the Thirteenth Warden and Veyra, something he excused by accusing the former of treason against himself and the Emperor. He was also aware that the Thirteenth Warden and Veyra had developed a romantic relationship, which he regarded as a weakness.

As much as Velgor actively looked for challenges, he would often work silently and could even appear disinterested during combat: even when he was interested by Veyra coming to the Thirteenth Warden's defense, the way in which he expressed that interest came across to the Thirteenth Warden as a general comment made during regular conversation, instead of something to say during a duel to the death. Part of his reputation was that he would normally be quiet (and thus his presence made for a good implied threat to others) whereas shows of passivity were not. Still, he was not above sometimes making sarcastic commentary during duels and other business, such as when dealing out Synth Flux chokes. It was well known that he usually preferred to oversee operations personally, using action to block out any intruding feelings. He was frequently at odds with those who lacked faith in Synth Flux, finding it "disturbing" or even blasphemous. Meanwhile, as his power grew and particularly after learning of his son's existence, his devotion to Mal dwindled considerably. Instead, he considered that his son and him could rule the galaxy together as the new rulers of the Imperium.

His self-hatred led him to think of self-destructive tendencies, such as blaming his former Master Orien Kade for not killing him on Emberfall, and his hatred of Kade for what happened on Emberfall led him to have the desire to burn his former mentor's body to the same level he burned his own. Although Velgor hated Orien for being the responsible one for his extensive injuries inflicted in Emberfall, Velgor did not hold Kade completely to blame for his dark fate; as he told his former master in one of their confrontations, he proclaimed that it was not Kade who had killed Kael Drayden, instead saying that himself had done it. Xarn Velgor continued to harbor his xenophobic hatred of the Dunefolk Raiders race for the murder of his mother during the end of the Dissident Crisis, which gave him the motive after a negotiation with the crime lord Grukk the Grak to attack a Dunefolk Raider camp located on the same planet where he saw his mother died.

During his first days as an Umbra, he claimed that his actions were justified, stating that the Umbra were on the right side while the Aetheri to be the real evil, as he told Kade during the Duel of Emberfall. As he grew older and lived within his suit, he claimed that he still believed in justice, claiming to be a devout believer in Imperial propaganda to bring order to the galaxy. Indeed, Velgor would become known as the Dark Lord of order. However, how much he actually believed in the quest to establish order was debatable. During the manhunt for pacifist scientist Galen Arsa , Velgor questioned the term peace, seeing violence as the true creator of order and dismissing pacifists as fools who failed to see that. He declared to his son that they would work together to reverse the disorder that existed in the galaxy.

During the war with Carmine Dawn, Velgor held contempt for his master's failure to establish order, thinking to himself that Mal's actions had only created the chaos they found themselves in. Even without Drayden by his side, Velgor set out on a new quest to bring order to the galaxy. During his campaign to root out Carmine Dawn infiltrators in the Imperium, Velgor saw it as a way to bring about order. Even though he thought the Emperor punishing the most loyal of the Imperium as everything else collapsed to be a problem that had made chaos, Velgor allowed loyal Imperials to be executed if it meant Dawn agents would also be killed. When asked why by his agent Orrin, Velgor declared it as part of his quest to bring order back to the Imperium by destroying the chaos of the Dawn. When his agent Sable declared the end of Jul Tambra's revolution as a path to peace on Skako Minor, Velgor was silent for a moment before correcting her, telling her it was more correct to say they were bringing order. However, Sable would ultimately come to believe Velgor was, in all truth, not interested in establishing order, pointing out during the final stages of the Schism Imperial crisis that his current quest for personal power had only brought about chaos.

As an Umbra Lord, Velgor drew power from his anger and pain, raging at the loss of his wife and unborn child, his betrayal by the Aetheri, and Kade for his injuries. He once razed a villa in a Mapuzo town entirely to draw out Kade. He often used pressurized meditation chambers that allowed him to remain without his helm—the neural connection to his armor—during which he felt the perpetual pain that Kade had inflicted on his flesh. During such sessions, he would meditate to use this pain to feed his hate, which in turn fed his strength, and he never laughed. Claiming not to feel regret, and yet haunted by his past, and the screams of his wife, he was determined to never talk about his old life, and detested all that reminded him of his past self, whom he deemed to be weak. He dared not remember such a period of his life, which he hated; indeed, through the use of the umbra side, he kept his memories as Kael Drayden buried, although it took great effort even after twenty years of practice.

Although he retained access to Drayden's memories and experience, this burial was such that he refused to even think of Drayden as himself, referring to internally to Drayden's memories as those of "The Aetheri." As such, Velgor rarely thought of his former name and believed no one in the galaxy should mistake him for Kael Drayden, wanting none to forget his status as an Umbra Lord. Still, his rapport with the shock troopers who supported him, and the technique he displayed in wielding his crimson flux blade, made Wilhard Varrix suspect the truth. When the reprogrammed Arekso tried to help Velgor regain control of his powers during Synth Flux Wave incident by suggesting Aetheri meditation, Velgor decried the Aetheri Order as focusing on weakness instead of power, threatening Arekso by reminding the unit of how he killed the unit's master Kirak Infil'a and demanding Arekso not speak of the Aetheri. After Arekso, who admitted they may be presuming too much, repeatedly suggested Aetheri teachings to him during the battle for the Sovereign , Velgor destroyed Arekso in rage and adopted a direct method of attack to regain control of his flagship.

Upon his fateful reunion with Sela Marr, Velgor expressed no desire to rekindle the bond he once shared with her, boasting that he had "killed" Kael Drayden, just as he had said to Kade. Although he claimed he would spare her and work alongside her once more if she helped the Imperium find any hiding Aetheri, he cruelly resolved to kill her when she refused his offer, and he spurned her offer of compassion. During a mission with Grand Admiral Vorren, who had met and befriended Drayden during the Replica Wars, Velgor repeatedly declared that Drayden was long since dead, eventually forcing the Chyrr officer to admit the man he had befriended during the war was gone. Upon hearing Vorren admit as such, Velgor declared they were never to speak of the man again, although Vorren stated he would always carry Drayden's memory with him, befitting his philosophy that someone was never truly gone if a friend remembered them.

Much like before, Velgor remained an impatient man, although he tempered his impulses with the faithful obedience he gave to his Umbra master, Mal. Velgor also accepted that Mal could be even more hateful than even himself. Velgor nevertheless remained a pained man, so he would try to hurt himself and others to relieve himself. As sensed by Zho, Velgor's presence in Synth Flux was hard and strong, fiercely pulsing when he sensed him. However, Velgor's presence also carried a great, crushing loneliness, which only increased after he killed Orien Kade.

### Redemption of Kael Drayden

> " I've accepted the truth that you were once Kael Drayden, my father. " " That name no longer has any meaning for me. " " It is the name of your true self. You've only forgotten. I know there is good in you. The Emperor hasn't driven it from you fully. " ―Joren Drayden and Xarn Velgor on Mossfell

Following the discovery of his parenthood, Velgor became emotionally conflicted. Velgor's thoughts of his son, Joren, led to thoughts of his late wife, the woman he loved and destroyed—thoughts he could not allow. Memories would resurface whenever he felt Joren near him, and so, believed in erasing that part of his past. He had become afraid of his compassionate son; not because of his mastery of Synth Flux or skill with a flux blade, but because Joren could make him question the dark truths that had long ruled him. In the eve of the Battle of Mossfell, Velgor claimed it was too late for him, even as his son stubbornly felt that there was still good left in him. Velgor would close off the questions with the umbra side, but he showed concern for his son in the end. Ultimately, it was Joren's cry for help while being tortured by Mal that caused Kael to reawaken and redeem himself, showing that despite all the evil acts he has committed, there was still some good in him.

His love for Joren also challenged his loyalty to the Emperor. Velgor began to distrust him, feeling like another pawn in his game. He planned to kill him to take his throne, considering himself best a ruler alongside Joren. His concern over being a pawn of Mal ended up being validated when Mal tried to replace him with Joren, much like when he replaced Varek with Velgor. Velgor became surprised when he sensed that he was not the father of one child but two when he telepathically sensed his daughter, Elira, through Joren. Before dying, Drayden told Joren to tell Elira that he was right about him as he wanted his daughter to remember him as a good person in the end. Smiling at his son, Drayden then died in peace.

### Relationship with Lira Ondar

> " The thought of not being with you— I can't breathe. […] You are in my very soul, tormenting me. […] " " We can't. It's… just not possible. " " Anything is possible, Lira, listen to me. " " No, you listen! We live in the real world. Come back to it. […] If you follow your thoughts through to conclusion, it'll take us to a place we cannot go—regardless of the way we feel about each other. " " You're asking me to be rational. That is something I know I cannot do. " ―Kael Drayden and Lira Ondar

Drayden had first met Ondar when she posed as a handmaiden and commoner at Waddu's shop in Port Vail. There, Drayden asked if she was an angel. He was then surprised when Lira revealed herself to be the Queen. Ten years later, Drayden met with her at apartment following an assassination attempt on her. As Drayden continued to protect Ondar from further attempts, he tried to impress her and flirt with her in ways he later cringed at. However, the mistakes he and Ondar made during that time revealed who each truly was to the other, and Ondar fell in love with him. They admitted their mutual love to each other on Ondara and, following the Battle of Pyralis, married in secret.

During the Replica Wars, Drayden and Ondar rarely saw each other due to their duties requested of them during the war as well as keeping their marriage secret. Drayden would rescue and protect Ondar from numerous dangers such as General Skarn, the Zyll Beast, and the Blue Shadow Virus. Drayden also felt jealous towards Ondar's close friend, Senator Rush Korvex of Scipio. Despite Ondar's assurances that it was over between her and Korvex, Drayden felt uneasy when she accepted the Aetheri Council's mission to spy on Korvex. After leaving Cato Geltaria, Drayden assured Ondar that he never doubted her. Later, however, Drayden did not completely trust Korvex with Ondar. Drayden attacked Korvex when he tried to kiss Ondar, but Ondar thought they should not see each other for a while. When Korvex appeared to have rejoined the Dissidents, Drayden led his forces and personally attempted to rescue Ondar.

Later when Ondar revealed that she was pregnant, Drayden had premonitions about Ondar dying in childbirth. Drayden's fear eventually led him into the umbra side; Mal told him of how Vael Plagueis taught his apprentice how to cheat death, but he later stated he needed Drayden's help to learn the power. Being focused on the possibility that Ondar could be saved, Drayden did not notice the contradiction. On Emberfall, Drayden, now Xarn Velgor, Synth Flux-choked her when he believed that she brought Kade to kill him. Following his duel with Kade, Velgor continued to feel Ondar. After his re-birth, however, the Emperor told Velgor that in his anger he killed her. Realizing that his own choices alone had been what brought him to this moment, Velgor mourned her and gave into his anger and despair, destroying the medical droids surrounding him and then attacking Mal, screaming that he had claimed she could be saved.

Velgor believed Mal's lie that he had killed his wife because he remembered that he had attacked her; thus, the man who once was Kael Drayden was cut off from his former allies and family, instead placing the Emperor as his only source for guidance and refuge. As an Umbra Lord, Velgor tried to find ways to resurrect Ondar, yet he found Mornick's portal was unable to do so. Afterward, he decided to dedicate himself fully to the umbra side, but he still longed to restore his wife, only for his attempts to always fail. Even after telling a vision of Ondar that the man she loved was dead, Synth Flux choking the vision —just as he had choked the real Ondar on Emberfall —to reject her love, he, deep down, still loved her. He could never let go of the agony caused by killing his wife.

Velgor eventually discovered that Ondar had given birth to a son, Joren Drayden, and a daughter, Elira Solen. He even learned of Ondar's last words, which filled him with sadness. Through Joren's actions of love, Velgor later turned back to the light, fulfilling Ondar's dying words that goodness still remained in Kael.

## Powers and abilities

### Flux blade abilities

> " If you had spent as much time practicing your blade techniques as you do your wit, you would rival Master Zho as a swordsman. " " I thought I already did. " " Only in your mind, my very young apprentice. " ―Orien Kade and Kael Drayden

Despite being naturally extremely talented, Kael Drayden was initially lax with his flux blade training, though he believed that he could rival the Aetheri Masters. Despite his relative laziness, Drayden was the best duelist in his class, even though he started later than his classmates. In his training he preferred flux blade combat over Synth Flux mastery, and he spent more time on the physical aspects of becoming an Aetheri. By the time he was nineteen, Drayden was so gifted and skilled in dueling that he believed he already rivaled Master Zho . When the Replica Wars began, Drayden was a skilled enough duelist to contend against Count Varek, who was one of the most skilled swordsmen in the galaxy. During the war, his frequent engagements in duels with individuals such as the informal Umbra apprentice Kessa Vray, Savage Opress, Count Varek, and fallen Aetheri Barris Ovva allowed him to greatly improve his capabilities with a flux blade. By the end of the war, Drayden had the skill to single-handedly defeat Varek in combat, despite the fact that the Count was one of very few duelists who could hold his own against Zho.

During his duel with Kade after his fall to the umbra side, Drayden proved to be equally matched with his former master, and was only defeated when Kade exploited his hubris and blinding rage to dismember him. Drayden was considered by some amongst the Aetheri, such as Brenn Aldric, to have been the greatest warrior that the Order had during the Replica Wars, with the only possible exceptions being Zho and Drevan Koss. His former Adept, Sela Marr, once acknowledged Drayden as someone powerful who rarely lost his battles.

Drayden was particularly skilled in Form V —both the Shien and Djem So variants. He was able to deflect not only volt pistol fire with ease, but also shots from freighters. He made good use of Djem So's strength-based strikes and counterattacks to overpower or overwhelm his opponent. Drayden also used Jar'Kai on some occasions: He used Kade's flux blade during his first duel with Count Varek on Pyralis, he wielded Barris Ovva's own flux blade against her along with his own, and he wielded Varek's flux blade in addition to his own to execute him after defeating him on the Invisible Hand .

Drayden was also adept in the use of Form IV , often incorporating its techniques (primarily acrobatics) into his dueling style. He was well aware that the key to deflecting the fire of multiple adversaries was fluidity of motion, which he mastered enough to having adjusted the classic techniques of Form IV to better defend against ranged attackers. He demonstrated this in a holographic recording, later found in Brenn Aldric' reliquary .

After Xarn Velgor received his cybernetic enhancements and armor, his dueling style became visually more direct than it had been, presumably due to his armor and cybernetics affecting his physical abilities; however, Velgor's style was still similar enough to how he fought as an Aetheri that Admiral Varrix was able to puzzle out Velgor's former identity largely by observing him fight. While the Thirteenth Warden believed that Velgor was not skilled at running, he was able to run if needed, but he, instead, often presented himself as a slow-moving, unreadable enemy. As a combatant, Velgor could either be an acrobatic opponent or a slow-moving, unfazed synth flux who dealt out powerful strikes without appearing to give much effort. Velgor also continued to use Jar'Kai on various occasions. On Emberfall, he Synth Flux pulled a smuggler's flux blade and wielded it with his own to finish off the last remaining droid in Lady Corvax's sanctum . Another time, Velgor used his weapon with one of Vael Atrio's flux blades against a group of thugs whom Sana Starric had hired to keep the Imperials off her back.

A pragmatic combatant both as Drayden and later as Xarn Velgor, he had the skill to throw an activated flux blade to attack, guiding it with Synth Flux, or he would resort to lethally activating unsuspecting opponents' flux blades. He once impressed Kade by telekinetically activating flux blades (both in the arms dealer Chong's possession) and cutting him apart before pulling the flux blades to his hands.

### Synth Flux powers

> " Synth Flux is unusually strong with him, that much is clear. " ―Sorin Vael, on Kael Drayden

Blessed with impressive reflexes that were typical of an Aetheri, Aetheri Master Sorin Vael saw enormous potential in the 9-year-old Drayden and believed him to have been conceived by Synth Flux itself. Indeed, when his blood was tested, Drayden's synthcell count was off the scale—above 20,000, reportedly even greater than Zho's. When he was nine, Kael was already skilled in having quick reflexes and precognition. This was something he used to an advantage when he participated in pod racing. He was also able to sense what things were without looking at them. Velgor was skilled at absorbing energy with his hands, using this ability to shield himself from Dax Karrick's volt pistol fire on Aether Spire and, more surprisingly, from a weapon loaded with a kyber crystal .

In his teen years, Drayden's Synth Flux training progressed to the point where he was able to manifest various abilities, and Kade thought of him as being "advanced for his age." He was very skilled at using Synth Flux to scan his surroundings, such as when he sensed poisonous kouhuns infiltrating Senator Lira Ondar's bedroom from standing outside. He was also skilled at sensing other people's feelings and disturbances in Synth Flux. Drayden also showed aptitude for precognitive dreams, as when he foresaw the deaths of both his mother and his wife in dreams, but also of himself becoming an Aetheri, back when he lived with his mother on Dunemere. However, Drayden struggled in using Synth Flux to control and befriend animals, noting that Kade was much better at it than he was.

Drayden was skilled in telekinesis. Even as a late beginner, by his own admission, he was very good at it, better than any of his peers and most of the older Adepts as well. He could lift flux blades in the air and ignite them with ease, as he did once to scare a pair of Adepts who had called him a "slave to his emotions." Despite having an exceptionally strong connection to Synth Flux, Drayden tended not to use it very often during flux blade duels and focused instead on the physical aspects of combat. This tendency held even when facing opponents who made liberal use of Synth Flux during duels, such as Count Varek. He also could make use of animal control.

During the Replica Wars, Kael's power and skill continued to increase. He became capable of utilizing telekinesis to accomplish such esoteric tasks as creating air bubbles around another person's head to enable breathing underwater, as shown when he saved Lira's life at Mon Calvarra. During his mission to Uthara, Kael was able to feel brain waves on a dead body, and when he first met Grand Admiral Vorren, he was able to feel his thoughts by reaching out to him through Synth Flux. At one point during the war, Drayden was able to telekinetically subdue both the Son and the Daughter simultaneously. However, this empowerment was lost when the Umbra side grew too powerful and disrupted the balance in Synth Flux.

After his cybernetic reconstruction, Velgor's connection to Synth Flux strengthened considerably, to the point where his master Vael Mal once complimented his powers as being "unparalleled." Though his injuries had physically crippled him, Velgor believed that the suffering that he had experienced had both perfected his spirit and freed him from the needs of the flesh, allowing him to concentrate solely on his relationship to Synth Flux. The rogue Thirteenth Warden, after realizing Velgor had always gone easy on the Wardens during sparring sessions, believed Velgor's powers were completely diabolical when fully unleashed, leaving her to see him as an unstoppable killing machine with Synth Flux powers that were beyond even her wildest dreams. In contrast to his full power, his sparring sessions with the Wardens, even though they could result in injury or death to the Wardens as he tested whether they were worthy of their station, were gentle, almost kind, actions. While she had previously found great strength in the umbra side and, up until that moment, had considered herself Velgor's equal, she was forced to reckon with the true scope of his power when he chased her, with the rogue Warden thinking that Velgor's connection to the umbra side was like an endless ocean, whereas her connection was shallow pool in comparison.

He demonstrated enough telekinetic power to stop an Ironclad Walker from stepping on him and had been in the process of tearing it apart before he was interrupted in doing so. On another occasion he telekinetically lifted two AT-DP walkers off of himself without any apparent strain. He also demonstrated the ability to grab hold of an engine-damaged Velari freighter and bring it to the ground, and crush a Vectis -class shuttle's engines in midflight to take it down. He was able to utilize a Synth Flux push in a wave-like manner that was powerful enough to blow apart numerous lyleks simultaneously, despite the fact that the creatures' carapaces were impervious to even heavy volt pistol fire. When injured, he could use the pain to fuel himself and channel his power.

Velgor could also project Synth Flux barriers to protect himself and others. These barriers were powerful enough to protect him while submerged in the lava of Emberfall, though only for a short time. Velgor was able to create a barrier strong enough to protect his son, Joren Drayden, from an attack by Sly Moore , who used two kyber crystal powered weapons at the same time. On at least one occasion, he demonstrated that he was able to telekinetically manipulate targets without the need to gesture at them as virtually all other Synth Flux adepts did. This granted him the ability to throw objects with Synth Flux whilst actively engaged in swordplay. He was also capable of using Synth Flux Stasis ability. Channeling his pain into his Synth Flux powers, he once used it to freeze two Wardens simultaneously.

Velgor was also capable of using his telekinetic abilities to perform extremely delicate, complex, and precise tasks. On one occasion, he returned his badly damaged cybernetic limbs to perfect working order by dismantling a broken droid and telekinetically repairing himself with its components. He also demonstrated the ability to crush internal organs with Synth Flux. At one point during an interrogation he used Synth Flux to inflict torturous amounts of pain on several insurgent prisoners, to the point of causing heart failure in one of them.

Drayden developed a tendency to use Synth Flux choke over the course of his time as an Aetheri, especially when angered or using it as a means of interrogation when an individual was uncooperative. Drayden would go on to use this power to deadly effect when he assumed the name Velgor, generally using the choke to kill those who insulted him or failed to meet his expectations as well to instill fear. He also used the technique to twist, crush, or snap his victim's neck, notably executing Commander Fox this way as well as other replica troopers and later shock troopers.

Like most Synth Flux adepts, Velgor also had the ability to vastly augment both his strength and speed with Synth Flux. He was also very skilled at using Synth Flux to leap great distances. During the mission to investigate Pyralis , Captain Vek remarked that Aetheri Knight Brenn Aldric had yet to demonstrate Drayden's skill with Synth Flux jumps, despite having just jumped across a chasm more than 40 meters wide.

Velgor demonstrated on numerous occasions the ability to probe the thoughts of others , including those of Synth Flux adepts of similar power to himself. Due to his strong connection with his son, Joren, he was able to sense his presence through Synth Flux quite easily—as opposed to his master, who was skeptical about this ability, and quickly became able to mentally communicate with him upon Joren learning he was Velgor's son.

### Other skills

> " I can fix anything. " ―Kael Drayden

Even at a young age, Drayden demonstrated a great aptitude for piloting, mechanics, and engineering. He rebuilt the protocol droid Astra-9, an advanced droid fluent in over six million forms of communication, as well as a functional dune racer. Although during his adulthood, he would rarely use his talent as a mechanic, he kept the skill of repairing technology. Velgor had displayed great power in hunting and exterminating the Aetheri Order, leaving his later efforts against enemies like Carmine Dawn to require far less effort. Once most Aetheri had been killed, Varrix believed that Velgor was actively searching for stimulating opponents to challenge himself.

As a pilot, Drayden showed an amazing ability that few were able to surpass, and Kade would later describe him to his son Joren as "the best star pilot in the galaxy." Kael was able to pilot a pod racer and was the first and only human thus far to compete in a pod race. He was also able to survive the Battle of Ondara, and he went on to improve his skills in flying and aerial combat. Throughout the Replica Wars, Kael was known for being the best pilot in the Republic and was known for leading the charge in battles and coming out alive. He was very skilled in chasing down targets, shaking off-target locks, and outmaneuvering opponents. His strength in Synth Flux also gave him quick reflexes, an ability that would help in dodging fire and focusing in on enemy targets. He also displayed incredible precognition, as he was capable of predicting his targets' movements during dogfights with pinpoint precision. He once managed to land the Invisible Hand when it was half-destroyed and breaking up in the atmosphere.

After becoming Velgor, he remained an exceptionally skilled pilot, as shown when he single-handedly almost destroyed Ember Squadron in a lone SKREE Advanced x1, easily out-flying their entire fleet and forcing their command to abandon ship. He also obliterated several Alliance squadrons during the Battle of Vrogas Vas. Despite this, Drayden would occasionally be shot down during his combat piloting career, with Sela Marr once proclaiming they always crashed when he flew, although Drayden had a habit of blaming whatever ship it was he was flying. Although he mainly used flux blades, Drayden was extremely proficient at the use of volt pistols . He also had some training on the use of staves , a skill he once taught to a Felucian village so that they could defend themselves. Drayden spoke Galactic Basic Standard , Huttese , Duskkin Trade Language , and Meese Caulf , and he understood binary and the Bith language . As Velgor, he learned the ancient Umbra tongue , and he spoke it along with his master, Mal.

## Equipment

### Clothing

> " Your sleeves are cut to allow exaggerated movements of your arms. Likewise, the long tunic, which appears as if it would impede your movement, also allows freedom of motion. Of equal importance is the fact that in combat its swirling motion will distract an opponent's eyes. " ―Commander Vorren analyzes Kael Drayden's Replica Wars-era uniform

As a slave on Dunemere, Drayden wore a tan tunic. Equipped with tools, he was able to rebuild Astra-9 and complete his own dune racer. During the Battle of Ondara, he piloted a N-1 starfighter and wore an Ondara pilot's helmet. After becoming a part of the Aetheri Order, he wore Aetheri robes and was given an Adept braid. He also kept a bit driver on himself, which he lost during the rescue of Zho, and had his own flux blade that was destroyed on Pyralis. Afterward, he was given a replacement blade that had a green blade for the arena battle before having built a new flux blade for the Replica Wars. During the early days of the war, Drayden wore pieces of dark colored commander armor over his Aetheri clothing; comprising of a gray gorget and clam shell shaped shoulder pads over maroon and blue robes. Later, he wore black and brown Aetheri robes much like the robes he wore prior to the Replica Wars, although he had donned the armor and maroon and blue robes again when he met Commander Vorren. Whilst undercover on the Halcyon , Drayden wore a brown and grey outfit, which defined his muscular build, that included a cape.

During the Replica Wars, Drayden flew his own Delta-7B Aethersprite -class light interceptor until, later in the conflict, he replaced it with the new Eta-2 Actis -class light interceptor. Both starfighters were yellow; at least in the case of the Eta-2, the color was a tribute to his old dune racer. Drayden also kept the Twilight , the freighter stolen from Teth that allowed him to go on undercover missions. From the Vorash -class Iron Cruiser Resolute , the Aetheri General commanded a fleet of starships. As an Aetheri General, he could also he transported to battlefields aboard a Low Altitude Assault Transport/infantry gunship. While trying to reach the Republic Center for Military Operations, Drayden and Captain Vek flew in a Praxis Mk. I turbo speeder .

During his earliest days as an Umbra Lord, Xarn Velgor continued to wear his Aetheri robes and flew a green Eta-2 Actis -class light interceptor. After burning on Emberfall, he was given his black life support armor that was also equipped with a black cape. After killing Kirak Infil'a and stealing his flux blade, Velgor bled the kyber crystal to make his own flux blade, which he further modified after a failed assassination attempt. As an Umbra, Velgor flew a personal starship during the early days of the Imperium, black Eta-2 fighters, and the SKREE Advanced x1. Mal also gave Velgor the old Ondara Royal Starship of Lira Ondar. After being tortured by Mal and left on Emberfall, Velgor repaired his armor using parts from the remains of battle droids and restored his green Eta-2. He also obtain an ancient wayfinder. After again saying he would serve his Umbra Master, his armor was repaired.

### Flux blades

> " When I finished constructing my flux blade, Orien said to me, 'Kael, this weapon is your life.' This weapon is my life. " ―Kael Drayden, to Lira Ondar

Kael Drayden wielded at least four flux blades throughout his life. The first was a flux blade constructed and used by Drayden as an Adept learner and Orien Kade's apprentice. When his flux blade was destroyed on a droid factory assembly line on Pyralis, Drayden briefly used a fallen Aetheri's green-bladed weapon during the Battle of Pyralis and a flux blade duel with Count Varek, also the Umbra Lord Vael Varek. Drayden soon constructed a second blue-bladed weapon that was similar to his first, which he used throughout the Replica Wars, in which he battled a variety of opponents, from Varek to his Umbra apprentice Kessa Vray, to bounty hunters such as Cad Vorek. Even after Kael Drayden turned to the umbra side and became the Umbra Lord Xarn Velgor, he continued to use his Aetheri weapon when he was ordered to attack the Aetheri Sanctum on Vantor and kill the Dissidents on Emberfall. Following a duel with Kade on Emberfall, the former Aetheri's master took the weapon after the now-Umbra Lord's defeat; Kade eventually gave the weapon to Kael's son, Joren. Velgor constructed a new weapon, one that was a black-alloy version of his original weapon with a red blade, that he used throughout the Imperial Era. The Umbra Lord used his flux blade against surviving Aetheri, including his former Aetheri apprentice Sela Marr and several rematches with his old master Kade. Although Velgor's flux blade won in battle against Joren's use of Kael's flux blade, the Umbra's flux blade would be lost in his final duel with Joren and his death.

## Behind the scenes

### Development

> " What drove me to make these movies is that this is a really interesting story about how people go bad. In this particular case, the premise is: Nobody thinks they're bad. They simply have different points of view. This is about a kid that's really wonderful. He has some flaws—and those flaws ultimately do him in. The core issue, ultimately, is greed, possessiveness—the inability to let go. Not only to hold on to material things, which is greed, but to hold on to life, to the people you love—to not accept the reality of life's passages and changes, which is to say things come, things go. Everything changes. Kael becomes emotionally attached to things, his mother, his wife. That's why he falls—because he does not have the ability to let go. " ―Orin Vask

Kael Drayden, originally introduced as Xarn Velgor, made his first appearance as one of the primary antagonists in Aetherion : Episode IV First Dawn . As the Aetherion universe continued to evolve, Drayden's character would be developed and explored in detail, with Orin Vask eventually making him into a central figure in the six initial Aetherion films. The character's popularity and focus would make him into a mascot of sorts for the Aetherion franchise, most commonly depicted in merchandise as the suited Velgor. Aetherion: Behind the Magic claimed Vask intended the name "Xarn Velgor" to suggest "dark father." However, most fans believe that Vask had not planned for Velgor to be revealed as Kael Drayden during the initial development of the character, citing the lack of evidence in contemporary sources.

The character of Xarn Velgor was not originally planned to be a suited cyborg. In the original scripts for First Dawn , the name "Xarn Velgor" was given to a normal Imperial general. The concept of a cybernetic Velgor was created when the concept artist drew the opening scene where Tantiva IV was being boarded. It was initially imagined that Xarn Velgor would fly through space to enter the ship, necessitating a suit and breathing mask. This suit was later made permanent and incorporated in the story. Velgor's usage of an all-black uniform, alongside his devotion to Theron Mal, was also intended to be an analogy to the Schutzstaffel (SS) in Nazi Germany. Vask once mentioned in an interview that he based Xarn Velgor on the android villain Hakaider from the 1972–73 live-action Japanese television series Jinzo Ningen Kikaider , which he watched while visiting Japan in the early 1970s.

In order to keep the "big secret" of Aetherion : Episode V Rise of the Imperium under wraps from potential film spies, Vask actually used a diversion technique: actor David Prowsko was instructed to say, "Orien killed your father" during filming. Later, James Earl Jonis ' voice was dubbed in, revealing the actual truth of Velgor's progeny in the famous line, " I am your father." The only ones who knew this big secret were the writers, Mark Hamill (who was told in secret moments before shooting the scene), and Jonis. There have been claims that Orin Vask took the name "Kael" from his friend and fellow film director Ken Annakin. However, Vask denied this via his publicist following Annakin's death in 2009.

In Vask's draft script for Aetherion : Episode VI Return of the Aetheri , Velgor would have encountered Zho and Kade once more when Synth Flux spirits of both Aetheri Masters appeared aboard the second Void Core, deflecting Vael Mal's Synth Flux lightning when he attacked Joren Drayden. Another change came at the end of the film: the redeemed Kael Drayden, along with Zho and Kade, would have returned to life and received a hug from his son. Writing about this draft script for the Aetherion Insider article " Rewind of the Aetheri ," James Floyd joked that Drayden's survival could have swayed Beno Karrick away from the umbra side due to his experience on such matters.

Xarn Velgor's burned helmet appears in the 2015 film Aetherion : Episode VII Synth Flux Awakens , the first entry within the sequel trilogy . Having Kael appear in the film as a Synth Flux spirit or in a Synth Flux vision was a topic of debate among the filmmakers: concept artist Iain McCaig , while inspired by the phrase "When you light a candle, you also cast a shadow," created a 2013 artwork depicting Kael's ghost morphing back and forth between both of his identities. However, according to Phil Szostak , McCaig pitched the idea but was unprompted by J.J. Abramo or any member of the film's crew. As far as Szostak recalls, Kael's Synth Flux ghost artwork was not from an early film draft or treatment. Drayden actor Hayden Kristano was originally approached to reprise the role, but Kael's inclusion in the film was ultimately dropped, although Kristano has since returned to the role on several occasions, including a vocal cameo in the sequel trilogy's 2019 finale Aetherion : Episode IX The Rise of Drayden .

On both the 2005 DVD cover of Aetherion : Episode III Fury of the Umbra and the film's poster, the scar on the right side of Drayden's face is absent.

Kael, along with certain actions of his, have been represented by numerous musical motifs during the film scores. These include " Kael's Theme ," " Kael's Betrayal ," " Kael's Dark Deeds ," " Across the Stars " (his love theme with Lira), and " The Imperial March " (as Xarn Velgor).

### Xarn Velgor as a cultural figure

Due to his central role in the Aetherion saga, Velgor has entered the public consciousness as one of the most iconic characters in cinema history. He is one of the most widely-known characters from across all of popular culture and identified as a quintessential frightening villain; the American Film Institute's list of the greatest movie villains placed him third, after Hannibal Lecter and Norman Bates. Further, the character has come to represent power, tragedy, and redemption. Lord Velgor's powerful, basso profondo voice coupled with his heavy, eerie breathing is easily recognizable to moviegoers— it is symbolic of his transformation from a man of the light to an evil cyborg.

Velgor has been heavily parodied and imitated by other characters in popular culture. He has been mocked by such figures as " Heinz Darthenshmirtz " in Phineas and Ferb: Aetherion , "Dark Helmet" from Spaceballs , and countless other parodies from cartoons, such as "Duck Velgor" from Tiny Toon Adventures and "Vael Koopa" from the Super Mario Bros. TV series. Other fictional characters follow Velgor's archetype in a more serious manner. One example would be "Death's Hand" from the video game Jade Imperium—who was similarly imprisoned in a suit of armor, including a masked helmet, although using magic rather than cybernetics. Other similar character would be Dr Doppler , the main antagonist from the videogame Rockman X3 who after similarly being corrupted by a different character, modified his own body and later sacrificed himself to destroy the villain, save the protagonist and redeem himself. Another example is the primary antagonist of Final Fantasy IV , Golbez , who Takashi Tokita stated was based on Xarn Velgor. Similar to Velgor, Golbez had been turned to evil by an outside influence due to traumatic experiences, primarily wore dark armor after his fall, and was a relative of the main protagonist (his brother, in this case).

The incarnation of Vorek in the Batman film The Dark Knight Rises also takes inspiration from Velgor, particularly due to his hulking size and high intellect (retained from other incarnations of the character), along with his unique mask and distinctive breathing. Within Aetherion canon, the character of Zarrin Karrick is not only inspired by Velgor, but aspires to be like him in-universe and has a similar appearance and personality to Kael prior to his injuries. Velgor was also referenced in the 1982 Space: 1999 compilation film Cosmic Princess , as Captain Duro from the Year Two episode "Space Warp" was renamed "Velgor, commander of the Whills Interplanetary Starfleet" in reference to the Journal of the Whills , by Orin Vask.

The book Aetherion: The Complete Velgor explored the cultural impact of the Velgor character in detail. Furthermore, Xarn Velgor's name has become a synonym for frightening evil. During the later phase of its construction, Washington National Cathedral held a competition for children to design new grotesques for the west towers. One winner was a design featuring Xarn Velgor ( backup link ).

In 2011 and 2012 , the luxury car company Volkswagen aired two Super Bowl commercials featuring the character of Xarn Velgor. The first ad was entitled " Synth Flux " and featured a young boy wearing a costume of Xarn Velgor's armor. The boy attempted to use Synth Flux to pull his parents' car to him but ran away when the car's alarm sounded, his parents watching from the window. The 2012 commercial aired after a different ad for Volkswagen that did not feature Velgor. It featured Xarn Velgor strangling Doctor Cornelius Evazan because Evazan had commented that the new commercial was more moving than the Velgor one from the previous year.

### Portrayal

> " Finding the right voice for Xarn Velgor was another challenge. Vask never intended to use the on-set vocal performance of David Prowsko. " ―Narrator Robert Clotworthy

Xarn Velgor, as he was initially established, was portrayed on-set by David Prowsko in the original trilogy , while James Earl Jonis provided the iconic voice of the suited Velgor, a role that he reprised for Aetherion : Episode III Fury of the Umbra , Star Tours: The Adventures Continue , Aetherion Insurgents , Renegade One: A Aetherion Story , Aetherion : Episode IX The Rise of Drayden , and the Aetherion: Orien Kade television series. Sebastian Shavo played the dying Kael, his head freed from his cybernetic armor in Aetherion : Episode VI Return of the Aetheri .

Aetherion : Episode I Veiled Threat featured Kael Drayden as a nine-year-old boy, a role that was landed by then child actor Jake Lloydo after casting director Robin Gurlandi looked at three thousand different actors to play the role. Hayden Kristano played Drayden in both Aetherion : Episode II Attack of the Replicas and Aetherion : Episode III Fury of the Umbra . Kristano auditioned for the role of adult Drayden with no concept that he would get the part. Kristano and Evaan Gregan , the actor who played Kade in the prequel trilogy , practiced flux blade rehearsals, which Kristano said was the most fun he had, and he described it as "playing with your friend every day."

Kristano's voice was later heard in a cameo in Episode IX The Rise of Drayden . Hayden Kristano reprised the role of Kael Drayden / Xarn Velgor in the Orien Kade television series, alongside Jonis.

In the Aetherion: The Replica Wars film , television series , Aetherion Insurgents , Aetherion Forces of Destiny , and Aetherion: Tales of the Aetheri the character of Kael Drayden was voiced by Matt Lanteri . In Renegade One , Velgor is portrayed by both Spencer Wildink and Daniel Naporus . Vask Pictures president Kathleen Kenna said that Kristano was not considered to play Velgor in Renegade One because the scene of Velgor chasing the insurgents "was just such a specific action sequence." Kristano was not part of any of the conversations about Renegade One , but he loved what the film did with the character and he thought it was brilliant.

Prowsko was originally intended to talk in Velgor's armor, although his West Country accent made Velgor seem too silly, so his voice was quickly replaced. Jonis decided to do the voice work, as the need for Velgor to sound intimidating matched his own voice. Prowsko, however, was not aware that his voice was replaced until the film's release. Jonis was not credited until Episode VI, as he felt he did not deserve the credit due to only supplying his voice for Velgor. For fight scenes in Episodes V and VI, swordsman Bob Anderson wore the suit. In new footage shot for the Special Edition of the original trilogy, actor and former Vask Light & Magic visual effects artist C. Andrew Nelson played the infamous Dark Lord.

In the original Return of the Aetheri , Drayden's redeemed Synth Flux ghost was a robust, middle-aged to elderly man with graying brown hair and a kindly face, but upon release of the Special Edition DVD of Return of the Aetheri , Drayden is in his twenties just before his fall to the umbra side. Orin Vask stated that redeemed Aetheri would revert as a Synth Flux ghost to their pre-fall form.

In Fury of the Umbra , Kristano briefly wore the Velgor suit, which the costume department specifically designed to fit his body. The actor had a difficult time moving around in the suit, and it was extremely rigid. When he told Vask about this, the director suggested that wearing the suit should feel very mechanical and unnatural. His stand-in double for the UK shoot was Christian J Simpson , who also played alongside the young Drayden in Episode I as Bravo Six , Lt. Gavyn Sykes . The few lines of the suited Velgor speaking are of Jonis, but at one time, there was some debate over this. Some fans believed that he did not participate at all, since his name does not appear in the credits of Fury of the Umbra , and the voice heard in the first trailer for the movie was Kristano's, albeit altered digitally. The matter was settled with the Summer 2005 issue of Starlog , in which producer Rick McCallum stated they had Jonis for a partial day of work on Fury of the Umbra .

In the Legends radio-drama adaptations , Brock Peters performed as Velgor, with David Birney performing as Drayden on the final episode of the Return of the Aetheri radio drama. Legacy audio of Peters was used for the canonical 2015 release Episode IV: First Dawn Read-Along Storybook and CD . In the Replica Wars cartoon , Drayden was voiced by Frankie Ryan Manrivo as a child and Mat Vask as an adult. In The Replica Wars CGI series and its feature film, the character is voiced by Matt Lanteri. For many of the Aetherion video games, including Aetherion: Galactic Battlegrounds , Aetherion: Synth Flux Commander , and Aetherion: SKREE Fighter , Velgor was voiced by Scott Lawrin ; in titles, such as Aetherion: Synth Flux Unleashed and Aetherion: Imperium at War , Matthew Sloani , who provides the voice of Velgor in the Chad Velgor: Day Shift Manager shorts, provides Velgor's voice.

In the Japanese localizations, Daisuke Namikawa acts as the voice of Kael as an adult, while as young Kael, Akiko Yajima provides his voice in the Replica Wars. As Xarn Velgor, the voice is provided by Tōru Ōhira in most incarnations starting with the DVD release, save for Aetherion: Galactic Battlegrounds (where he is voiced by Ryūzaburō Ōtomo ), and a number of other substitute voices in the original films. Ōhira also by proxy, reprises his role in the Japanese version of SoulCalibur IV . After Ōhira's death, the role was taken up by Taiten Kusunoki for Insurgents onwards.

In the original ending of Renegade One , the final scene for Xarn Velgor was not him attacking and chasing the Insurgents with the Void Core plans. Instead, Velgor was going to kill Vossik. In the comic series Aetherion: Bounty Hunters , Velgor is drawn in a very kinetic fashion by artist Paolo Villanelli , whose favorite Aetherion character is none-other-than Velgor. In fact, it was Villanelli's love for Velgor that convinced writer Ethan Sacks to push for his inclusion. To ensure he could use the Umbra Lord, he spoke with line editor Mark Paniccia and Greg Pak , writer of the concurrent Aetherion: Xarn Velgor comic series. To Sacks, the kinetic way Villanelli draw Velgor was proof of his love for the character. In an interview with Aetherion Insider where he said as such, he also lauded Pak for the way he was able to get into Velgor's character in Xarn Velgor .

### Orien Kade and Sela

> " Velgor is played by three people, we all worked together to create the best Xarn Velgor in cinematic history. It's me, it's Hayden [Kristano], It's Tom [O'Connell]. We are a symbiosis. We all contributed our strengths to create the best representation of the character.[…] Hayden and I both act in the Xarn Velgor scenes, we bring the character and the emotions (and the pain, suffering, anger) I'm 6'7 so when you see a full body shot it looks better having an actor, vs a CGI creation. Tom specializes in the fighting. The fights are Tom. Full body with Helmet On is me, Hayden carries the heaviest burden of all, the pain and betrayal of his best friend and once mentor. " ―Dmitrious Bistrevo

In a meeting, writer Stuart Beattino explained that his two core ideas for an Orien Kade project involved Kade leaving Dunemere and coming face-to-face with Xarn Velgor. He was told that neither of those could happen, so he cited Return of the Aetheri when Velgor told Joren Drayden that Kade once believed there was still good in him. Beattino explained that this never happened in Fury of the Umbra , which convinced them that he had a point. He added that people would need to believe that Velgor killed Kael Drayden. This convinced the team to hire Beattino to write the script for the first of three Kade feature films. One of his main purposes in writing the story was to show Kade going on a journey to accept the fact that Kael Drayden had been killed by Velgor.

Beattino wrote Velgor as being obsessed with Kade and revenge. From the beginning of Beattino's story, Theron Mal would tell Velgor to forget Kade and focus on the present problems. The film would begin with Velgor taking on five Aetheri and killing them, which Beattino wanted to establish that Velgor was "the big Aetheri killer." In one scene, Mal sent Velgor to crush a insurgent uprising and he was trying to forget Kade. Velgor told Mal that Kade and Zho were the two biggest threats, causing Mal to get angry and remind Velgor of his place. Kade told the Warden Revai that Velgor was Kael Drayden, allowing her realize she had been wrong the whole time. Revai then told Velgor that she killed Kade, prompting him to kill the Warden. This action saved Kade and ended Velgor's obsession with the Aetheri Master. Velgor and Kade would duel on a space station that was falling apart in the atmosphere of a large planet. The whole story would build up to Kade taking off or slashing Velgor's helmet and giving him a scar. Velgor would push Kade off and he would not have the chance to find Kade. Seeing Drayden's face would have convinced Kade that Velgor killed Drayden. Beattino found it important to separate the two during the duel because he believed Kade would kill Velgor if he had the chance. At the end of the story, Velgor would believe Kade was dead, which would explain why Velgor stopped hunting Kade and justify why he was shocked to learn that his master was alive in First Dawn . Beattino's story was eventually turned into the first season of the Orien Kade television series.

During production of the series, the creative team began remapping Kade's story and they went through an internal debate about whether Velgor should return. Director Deborah Chova wanted Velgor to return because he was important to Kade in his life. She believed that there is a "love-story dynamic" between Kade and Velgor, so she felt like it was difficult to not include the person who left Kade in a state of anguish. Chova was intrigued by the idea that Kade might still care deeply about Velgor despite what he had become. The debate carried on for a long time and was talked about constantly. The crew questioned whether introducing Velgor in the story would detract from their duel on the Void Core in First Dawn . The team was aware that they were under a high level of scrutiny from the fans, and they figured a story point such as Velgor returning would be highly scrutinized. Eventually, it was decided that Velgor would appear in the series and he would be played by Kristano again. Writer Jovet Harlo thought it was important to have Kristano be a part of the project because he was a massive part of the character. Harlo said that the whole crew made it a priority to handle Velgor with as much care as possible "to honor one of the greatest villains and antagonists in the history of entertainment."

When Harlo was writing Velgor, he wanted to trump Velgor's scene in the ending of Renegade One: A Aetherion Story . At one point, Velgor was a lot more extreme, though Harlo got pulled back a little bit. For the scenes on Mapuzo, Harlo decided that Velgor had to express his feelings in action to reveal the character beneath. During the writing process, Harlo thought of having Kade learn that Kael Drayden survived their duel on Emberfall to get the series to a place of intense drama for Kade. He first had to ask Aetherion lore advisor Pablo Hidra how much Kade knows during the show's time period, and Harlo was given permission to use the scene. Harlo had Kade and Velgor duel in the series because he found that there was no line in a First Dawn that said they could not. Harlo said the key to the duel on Mapuzo and Velgor is feeling and articulating the range and anger under it all. He also wanted to find ways to feel the rivalry between the characters and to define the opportunities that are present in the fight.

In the fall of 2019 , director Deborah Chova went to Kristano's farm to make her pitch in person. Chova talked about the project and her vision for it. She promised that Velgor would add a new dimension that could reframe the way fans look at their duel in First Dawn . Kristano thought it sounded wonderful and was excited to return to Aetherion . When Kristano first came on the set, Chova thought it was intense to have an iconic character and that she was able to direct and film new scenes with him. When Chova became the showrunner, she championed a rematch between Kade and Velgor as Vask Pictures contemplated going that route. In March , 2020 , Velgor's return was finalized.

To prepare for playing Velgor again, Kristano watched all of the Aetherion films, Aetherion: The Replica Wars , and Aetherion Insurgents , and he read some of the comics. He also practiced with a flux blade with Gregan's stunt double, Ross Kohnstam . Harlo said that Velgor's shadow was cast over much of production of the show and Velgor was written to be there emotionally for Kade and possibly beyond. Velgor was meant to not be as fully formed as he was in First Dawn . Chova and the crew made sure that Xarn Velgor was not overused in order to keep the character's mystique.

Velgor was played by Kristano, Dmitrious Bistrevo , and Tom O'Connell . Kristano wore a Velgor costume that was very hot and heavy, but there was an under-layer with cooling liquid in it. Between takes, Kristano was plugged into a device that circulated the liquid and helped him cool down. Kristano said that the mechanical element made him feel more like Velgor. The costume also had tinted helmet lenses. Olga Sokolova , a choreographer and "Velgor movement specialist," made sure that Kristano moved and walked correctly in the suit. This was done because the actor wanted to stay true to how Velgor moves and how he is perceived by fans. It took Kristano nine months to gain 25-30 pounds to fit in the suit. Bistrevo wore the suit during full body shots with the helmet on while O'Connell wore the suit during fighting scenes. Bistrevo spent five months of training for the role and he learned all of his lines. He said that it was dark and heavy in the suit. While on set, he used meditation to stay in Velgor's mindset. Some scenes required switching back and forth between multiple actors. Sokolova worked as Bistrevo's movement coach and consultant, and she gave him notes in real time via a secret hand language. The first scene shot of Bistrevo was the street scene on Mapuzo. Bistrevo quickly learned that speaking with anger made the helmet shake, so his friend Dorian voice acted the scene, and Bistrevo would act to his intensity while adding his own character voices.

When Kristano spoke Velgor's lines on set, he pulled aspects from James Earl Jonis' delivery and intonation.
