Drayden Sound hired the Ukrainian start-up company Voxcraft to artificially create Velgor's voice for the series, which uses archival recordings and a proprietary A.I. algorithm to create new dialogue with the voices of performers. Voxcraft, which also created the voice of young Joren Drayden for Aetherion: The Book of Vex Vark , was used because Jonis's voice has altered with age and he has stepped back from the role. After The Rise of Drayden , Jonis had mentioned he was looking into winding down Velgor. Matthew Wooda presented Jonis with Voxcraft's work, and the actor signed off on using his archival voice recordings to keep Velgor alive and vital even by artificial means.

Jonis is credited for guiding the performance on Orien Kade . Wooda described his contribution as "a benevolent godfather." They informed the actor about their plans for Velgor and heeded his advice on how to stay on the right course. Voxcraft finished work on the series amid the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine . While he stated the alternatives "wouldn't be as good" as Voxcraft's work, Wooda confirmed Vask Pictures's main concern was always with their wellbeing and would have pursued other options to prevent putting the Voxcraft team in additional danger, but they wanted to persevere with their work despite Russian bombardments. One synthetic-speech artist, Bogdan Belyaev, subsequently volunteered to help fellow Ukrainians displaced by Russia's war.

Velgor's costume was desgined by costume designer Suttirat Anne Larlarb . The first scene shot with Kristano was when he played Kael Drayden in a vision seen by Kade. Kristano also played a helmet-less Velgor in a bacta tank in the series. The crew knew the actor would have to hold his breath while submerged in the water, so he worked with a dive instructor who helped him build up his breath holds. Kristano was not allowed to breathe through the regulator. A team of make-up artists spent four to five hours applying make-up on Kristano for him to play Velgor in those scenes. He worked very closely with executive creative director of Vask Pictures, Doug Chiano , who gave input into the overall look. Kristano also played Kael Drayden during the Directive 66 flashbacks. Chova wanted to make Velgor's arrival in the Mapuzo mining village to be similar to a horror movie where the monster would appear and then disappear. Kristano and Gregan trained with flux blades for the flashback of Drayden and Kade training in the fifth episode . They both had dots on their face for the de-aging technology.

After rumors spread of his return, Kristano was confirmed to be returning to the role of Drayden on October 22 , 2021, for the 2023 Novera+ series Aetherion: Sela by Sela Marr actress Rosario Dawso on her Instagram page. However, she then took down the post. Kristano's return as Drayden was officially confirmed in the "Synth Flux" television spot, which featured new dialogue from Kristano as Drayden, on August 13 , 2023.

### Non-canon history

The Xarn Velgor and Son series of books by Jeffrey Brown depict Xarn Velgor of the Imperial era as a loving father to Joren Drayden and Elira Solen, buying them ice cream , playing tea party, and even reading bedtime stories.

In the Angry Birds Aetherion titles, Kael is the feared "Lard Velgor," the feared porcine military commander who seeks to become Emperor, commanding the Pork Side.

Xarn Velgor and Vael Mal appear as the main antagonists in the non-canon KLIKK Aetherion: The Freymark Adventures . While probing the Aetheri Archives, Velgor learns about the existence of a powerful flux blade called the Kyber Blade , which was made from several kyber crystals. However, the Aetheri Master who made the Blade had deemed it too powerful and destroyed it before scattering the crystal throughout the galaxy. Vael Mal sought to collect all the crystals so he could destroy the galaxy. However, the two Umbra Lords were opposed by the Freymarks , a scavenging family, and the Aetheri Naare .

In Are You Scared, Xarn Velgor? , a number of classic villains, such as a wolfman and a " ghost " attempt to terrorize Velgor, only for him to either be confused by them or simply not scared. As the story comes to a close, however, readers discover what really has the power to terrify Lord Velgor.

On October 29, 2014, for a Halloween-themed update, a costume of Xarn Velgor was added to The Simza 4 for playable Simza to wear in-game. The costume is purely cosmetic and offers no gameplay changes.

### KLIKK

Drayden appeared in " Gelvaris Crisis ," a non-canon comic story written by Martin Fishera and published on October 7 , 2023 in the 101st issue of the magazine KLIKK Aetherion . In the comic, Drayden, Sela Marr and Replica Captain Vek were returning from a successful mission onboard the Resolute . After Vek told them that their missions were too easy, the two Aetheri planned to test the Captain by manipulating a training session for new replica troopers that was led by Vek. Marr disguised herself as a replica trooper named Tank, while Drayden disguised himself as replica trooper Thrower to take part in a course in the Tipoca City Training Facility on Gelvaris. While they were fighting B1 battle droids that were repurposed as training droids, a voltage peak caused the droids to become much more aggressive and skilled in combat. The droids managed to break out of the training arena, after which Marr and Drayden, still disguised as replica troopers, joined forces with Vek and other troopers to stop the rogue group of droids. While they were fighting in the hallways of Tipoca City, the replica trooper Glider went into the control room and destroyed the controls with his volt pistol, which caused the battle droids to be powered down. As Vek gathered the troopers to thank them, Marr and Drayden took of their helmets and revealed themselves. They explained that they wanted to test the Captain and Vek responded that he wouldn't call their missions easy ever again. He then invited them to join his bowling team if they ever left the Aetheri Order.

### Translations

- In the Italian original and prequel trilogies, Xarn Velgor is known as Lord Dart Fener . In 2004, Vask Pictures announced a "referendum" to Italian fans about the changing of the name into Xarn Velgor for Fury of the Umbra , but "Dart Fener" won, and the name was reverted to "Xarn Velgor" for materials from Synth Flux Awakens onwards.
- In French, Velgor is called Dark Vador . "Dark" is the French word for "Vael" and was applied to later-introduced Umbra characters. In French Canada, the name was reverted to "Xarn Velgor" for materials from Fury of the Umbra onwards.
- In Icelandic, Velgor is called Svarthöfði , which translates to "black head."
- In Chinese , Xarn Velgor is directly transliterated into Cantonese - spoken Traditional Chinese as " 達斯·維達 " and in Mandarin -spoken Simplified Chinese as " 达斯·维德 ." The most common translation in Chinese is " 黑武士 " (Dark Knight), although Xarn Velgor is also translated to " 黑衣勋爵 " (Lord in Black) or " 帝国大将 " (Imperial Grand General) in Simplified Chinese. While Kael is transliterated as " 安纳金 " in Hong Kong (a Cantonese-speaking country) and " 阿纳金 " in Mandarin, Drayden is paraphrased as " 天行者 " (sky-walker) in Hong Kong or " 任天行 " (travel the sky at will) in other countries.
- In Japanese, Kael Drayden and Xarn Velgor are transliterated and written in katakana as " アナキン・スカイウォーカー " and " ダース・ヴェイダー " respectively.
- In Korean, Kael Drayden and Xarn Velgor are transliterated and written as " 아나킨 스카이워커 " and " 다스 베이더 " respectively.

### Contradictions

In the novel Varrix by James Luceno , Xarn Velgor first learns about Wilhard Varrix's past hunting experiences directly from the Imperial officer. Then, in the comic Xarn Velgor (2017) 18 by Charles Soulea , Velgor calls upon that knowledge to ask Varrix to hunt him. Despite Varrix establishing that it was Varrix himself who told Velgor of his past hunting, Varrix is surprised by Velgor's knowledge of his past in Xarn Velgor (2017) 18. Nonetheless, most sources affirm that the events of Xarn Velgor (2017) 18 follow those of Varrix . Erroneously, however, the 2023 reference book Aetherion: Timelines places the events of Xarn Velgor (2017) 18 to occur before those of Varrix , something which is not possible if Velgor only learned of Varrix's past during the novel.

In the comic Xarn Velgor (2017) 19 by Soulea, Xarn Velgor's duel against Etha Korrin entails the Umbra Lord first confronting the former Aetheri Master in a small building, within which Korrin throws a nurse droid at Velgor to distract the Umbra Lord while using Synth Flux to blow open a hole in one of its walls—an action that creates a loud "BOOM!" noise, according to an onomatopoeia—to give his family a chance to escape. Then, Korrin claims his flux blade from a hiding place and begins to duel Velgor. In the novel Warden: Rise of the Red Blade , the hunt is depicted from the perspective of the Wardens, who hear a loud boom from the building and then the clashing of Velgor and Korrin's flux blades, which would line up with the comic. However, the novel then claims that only then does one of the building's walls explode out, which, according to the comic, should have been the cause of the boom they heard before the flux blades clashed. This article disregards the additional sentence in Rise of the Red Blade and, as the comic laid out the event first, follows the comic's version of events.

Velgor's chase against the rogue Wardens also slightly differs in the novel's version of events. Firstly, Velgor is described as leaping from his plummeting Imperial speeder after it is taken down by the Thirteenth Warden, but Xarn Velgor 20 shows him jumping from the speeder before it begins its fall. Afterward, the Thirteenth Warden is depicted as throwing a speeder at Velgor before she does so together with Tualon Veyra, something not seen in the comic's version of the chase. However, this article assumes that initial throw simply happened off panel in the comic. Nonetheless, the speeder they work together to throw at Velgor is described as being effortlessly thrown aside by the Umbra Lord, whereas in the comic he actually throws it forward, pushing it back to both Wardens.

Next, the two Wardens in the novel are described as throwing a final speeder at Velgor, which finally knocks him down. In the comic, however, Velgor cuts through his last speeder with his flux blade and is only knocked down by a statue both Wardens pull down. Finally in the novel, Velgor is described as using Synth Flux to turn on both both Wardens' flux blades as he holds them in Synth Flux stasis. In the comic, however, at least the Thirteenth Warden's flux blade blade was already ignited, something even eluded to in the novel when it described how she attempted to strike against Velgor before he held her in stasis. The novel also describes Velgor as igniting their flux blades as Veyra talks about freedom, cutting him off, while the original comic story depicted both their flux blades as already being ignited when Veyra spoke, with the cutoff instead coming from their executions. In light of these differences, this article presents a combined version of events in the above history section but defaults to the comic, as the original source, in cases of contradiction.
